Call a proxy's anchor its position at its last build or repair, where its radius and inflated AABB were set and its travel `d` was reset. After a repair the anchored inflated AABBs of a pair that is not in the list do not overlap: nothing changed for a pair without a member of `E`, and step 4 finds every overlap of a member's new AABB (candidates outside `R` through the main grid, whose cells cover their unchanged AABBs, and candidates in `R` through the repair grid). The AABB gap between the anchors therefore exceeds `r_a + r_b`. Each proxy is at most `d` from its anchor, so the gap at any later substep exceeds `(r_a − d_a) + (r_b − d_b) ≥ |v_a|·h + |v_b|·h + 4·linear_slop`, which is at least the `D-064` admission margin. Each substep's contact set still equals per-substep pair finding's.
- **Impulses (`D-076`).** A repair leaves the keyed map and the seed flag alone. A kept pair keeps its slot's value, a re-admitted pair gets its own value back through the scratch map, and a pair that was not in the list, or whose contact was absent in the previous substep, starts at zero. Those are the values the keyed map would give.
- **Pair order.** A repaired list is the kept pairs in their old order followed by the re-admitted ones in member order, which is not the order of a fresh build. Solver order, and so trajectories, differ from `D-075`'s. They stay deterministic: the step is serial and every pass runs in proxy or pair order.

**Why:** Exact call counts on 2026-10-01 (`docs/plans/physics-ecs-perf-pass.md`) showed `D-075`'s list never surviving a substep: 3.99 builds per frame in `physics` and 3.20 in `physics-sparse`, 70% and 54% of all samples. With 8,000 moving bodies one always tripped: every falling body through the short gravity term, half the bodies in a world without gravity because both sides of the check are equal and `f32` rounding decides, and 20–100 bodies a substep whose speed a contact changed. Each trip restaged 8,521 proxies and ran 8,000 queries. A margin alone does not fix it: under `D-075`'s rule the margin must reach 8 px before `physics` builds once per frame, and the list then holds 98,958 pairs. Re-pairing only the offenders is what Box2D does with fattened AABBs and its move buffer.

The margin was swept on the sum of the two rows' `physics_step` p50: 10.46 ms at 0, 9.55 ms at `2·linear_slop` and 9.67 ms at `4·linear_slop`. Without a margin the rounding case still trips more than a quarter of the sparse bodies and falls back to a rebuild. With `2·linear_slop` a frame is one build plus 3.0 repairs of 153 proxies each in `physics`, and one build plus 2.1 repairs of 164 in `physics-sparse`; the fall-back never fires in either.

Against the build before it (`just perf compare`, five runs a side), `physics_step` p50 went from 10.04 to 5.86 ms in `physics` and from 4.77 to 3.67 ms in `physics-sparse`, whose p95 fell from 5.84 to 3.75 ms because the four-build frames are gone. `integrated` did not move (`total` p50 10.65 → 10.75 ms, `unchanged`): 1,003 of its 1,403 builds in 400 frames follow a contact wake, which this decision keeps as a rebuild.

**Consequences:**
- **Supersedes in `D-075`:** invalidation rule (a) for a budget trip ("Any of these rebuilds from current state") and the radius `|v_i|·t_left + ½|g|·t_left² + 2·linear_slop`. The build itself, rules (b) and (c), the speculative margins, the wake clause and the event clause stand.
- **Amends `D-076`:** a repair carries impulses by pair key through a scratch map, without a keyed-map synchronization. The synchronization before a rebuild and at frame end stands, and so does the rule that a contact absent for one substep returns with zero impulse.
- **Digests.** Pair order changes trajectories, so the three physics-bearing benchmark digests changed: `physics` `c972b2818d486617` → `86ffcabcdb15eed1`, `physics-sparse` `c95fadc938a3689e` → `5899f9c8a69d79b1`, `integrated` `9b2617e4c1ab23f7` → `5f031f4d947964cb`. `workload_version` stays 1, because the benchmarks' work did not change (`D-078`).
- **`physics.pairs`.** The list is built once for the whole frame's travel plus the margin, not rebuilt for the remainder at every substep, so the final-substep count rises from 15,150 to 36,144 in `physics` and from 1,831 to 15,298 in `physics-sparse` (6,677 to 7,034 in `integrated`). Compare flags that as workload drift against older captures. Contacts per substep are unchanged (8,861 and 8,846 in `physics`).
- **`D-075`'s bench gate.** `projectile_stream` is 41–57% faster at 3,000, 10,000 and 25,000 bodies; `pile_plus_bullet` reads no change at 10,000 and 21% faster at 25,000.
- Tests: the per-substep contact-set and warm-start oracles now also run over repaired lists; a hand-driven frame covers a repair with the build count unchanged, an untripped awake neighbour of lower index, a pair between two members, a pair between bodies repaired in different substeps, a body repaired twice and the carried impulses; the quarter fall-back on both sides of its limit; the gravity term against a replay of the integrator. `tests/physics_determinism.rs`, `physics_tunneling.rs` and `physics_containment.rs` pass.
- Not done here: a contact wake still rebuilds the list. Re-pairing the woken proxy instead would remove most of `integrated`'s builds and needs its own decision, because a woken body becomes an initiator.

## D-082 — Physics sleep state sits in arrays parallel to the proxies
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** The per-body sleep entries of `D-065` live in an array indexed by proxy, beside an array holding each dynamic entity body's key (an empty key for statics and tiles, which never sleep). At frame start the step compares the frame's proxies with that key array in one sequential pass. When the bodies are last frame's in last frame's order, every entry is already at its proxy's index and nothing is rebuilt. When the sequence differs (a spawn, a despawn, a body that turned static or lost its collider), entries carry over by entity key through an index refilled at that point, and sleeping entries that do not carry over wake their island, as before. `wake` and `is_sleeping` look an entity up through the index; inside a frame every access is by proxy index.

**Why:** `D-065` rebuilt a keyed map every frame: a clear of a table sized for twice the proxies and three probes per body at frame start, and one more per body at frame end. With no sleeper in the world that cost 0.42 of 13.26 ms in `physics` and 0.27 of 8.06 ms in `physics-sparse` (captures with sleeping disabled), a larger share once `D-080` and `D-081` had cut the rest of the step. In a settled or steady scene the body sequence does not change, so the rebuild was pure bookkeeping. Against the build before it, `physics_step` p50 went from 5.86 to 5.60 ms in `physics` (−4.6%) and from 3.67 to 3.43 ms in `physics-sparse` (−6.7%), both `improved`.

**Consequences:** Amends `D-065`: "Sleep state is a side table in `PhysicsBuffers` (flat open-addressing map keyed by the warm-start entity key)" becomes parallel arrays with a key index, and "the per-frame sleep-map rebuild" happens only when the body sequence changes. Every wake path, the thresholds, island tags and event semantics are unchanged, and so are the benchmark digests. A frame that spawns or despawns a body with a collider pays one rebuild, about what every frame paid before. No `std::HashMap`, no new dependency. Tests: `D-065`'s wake-path tests pass unchanged; new ones cover a sleeper that stops being a dynamic body, and the rebuild count and carried sleepers across a spawn that shifts proxy indices and a despawn.

## D-083 — ECS column storage: sorted column list, unboxed row moves, slot-table random access
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** An archetype keeps its columns in a `Vec` ordered like its sorted type key and moves rows between columns without boxing a value. No query result, iteration order or benchmark digest changes.

- **Sorted column list.** `Archetype::columns` is a `Vec<Box<dyn AnyColumn>>` in the order of the archetype's sorted `TypeId` key, so a column's index is its type's position in the key. The columns are created with the archetype: an insert or removal that reaches a new archetype builds it from empty copies of the source archetype's columns (`new_empty`), plus the inserted type's column at its sorted position. The pass-through `TypeIdMap` stays for the add and remove edge caches.
- **Unboxed row moves.** `AnyColumn::move_row_to(row, dest)` downcasts the destination column once and pushes the swap-removed value; `swap_remove_drop(row)` drops a despawned row's value in place. A move between archetypes is one merge walk over the two sorted keys, with no `Vec<TypeId>` and no hash lookup. `insert<T>` and `remove<T>` push and take their own value through the typed column. `push_erased`, `swap_remove_erased`, `get_erased`, `get_mut_erased`, `as_any` and `as_any_mut` are gone: downcasts go through trait upcasting to `dyn Any` (stable since Rust 1.86, no `unsafe`).
- **A column is found by type in two ways.**
  - *Key scan*, for structural changes and query setup (`Archetype::column_index`). A one-type query finds its column in the scan that filters the archetype. The scan is `#[inline(never)]`: inlined beside a query's row loop it changed how that loop compiled (`bounds_wrap` rebuilt its constants on every row, +0.05 ms).
  - *Slot table*, for random access by entity (`World::get`, `get_mut`) and for the values a flushed insert run writes (`D-084`). Every archetype carries 128 one-byte slots; slot `(hash >> shift) % 128` holds the column index of the one key type that hashes there, and the shift is picked when the archetype is created so that no two key types share a slot. A lookup is two loads inside the archetype. A type the archetype lacks finds an empty slot or another type's column, which the typed downcast the lookup does anyway rejects. A key no shift separates, or one longer than a slot can index, falls back to the scan. `World::get`, `get_mut` and `Archetypes::get`, `get_mut` are `#[inline]`.

**Why:** The profiles of 2026-10-01 (plan `docs/plans/archive/physics-ecs-perf-pass.md`; what a later session needs from it is in this entry, in `D-084` and in `docs/perf/benchmarks.md`) counted 293,960 `malloc` calls per frame in `churn`, 85% of them one `Box` per moved component value: `swap_remove_erased` returned `Box<dyn Any>` and `push_erased` took one, so moving a row allocated per component and a despawn allocated per component only to drop it. Each move also collected a `Vec<TypeId>` and made three hash lookups per moved column. Against the capture before it (`just perf compare`, five runs a side), `churn` `flush` p50 went from 8.58 to 3.82 ms and p95 from 9.39 to 4.16 ms, with `ecs`, `particles` and `integrated` reading no `regressed` owned metric.

How a column is found by type was measured four ways before the split above, because the first three each regressed a row:

| Lookup | `churn` `flush` p50 | `ecs` `follow` p50 | What it cost |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Before: hashed column map (the capture before the change) | 8.58 ms | 1.91 ms | — |
| Key scan everywhere | 3.82 | 2.84 | `follow` and `update` `regressed`: the scan leaves the key at an iteration that depends on the archetype, so its exit mispredicted on 0.88 of the lookups (0.06 before), and that branch resolves only after the entity lookup's cache miss, so consecutive lookups stopped overlapping |
| `TypeId` → index map everywhere | 3.98 | 1.92 | `cooldowns`, `team_bags` and `faction_histogram` `regressed` by 0.02–0.03 ms each: a query's per-archetype setup read the map and the column list, one cache line more than before |
| Queries resolving all their columns in one pass over the key, the map for `get` | 3.87 | 2.02 | `particles` `unattributed` `regressed` (2.37 → 2.47 ms): the new iterator shape changed how the particle systems' row loops compiled |
| Key scan out of line, the map for `get` (kept for the column storage) | 3.82 | 1.98 | none |

The slot table then replaced the map for `get`. One iteration of the benchmark's `follow` loop is about 160 instructions with either lookup, too long for a third lookup to overlap, so a shorter `get` does not help by putting more lookups in flight; what counts is the loads between the two cache misses. Each archetype's hash index is its own allocation, two cache lines per lookup that sit outside L2 with 488 archetypes, and a key compare reads a third. On a headless replica of the benchmark's population and `follow` system: 1.39 ms with the map, 1.38 with `#[inline]` and one downcast added, 1.27 with a slot table and a key compare, 1.17 with the slot table alone. In the suite, against the capture before it, `follow` p50 went from 1.98 to 1.75 ms (`improved`) and `update` p50 from 10.65 to 10.44 ms (`noisy`).

One more candidate was measured and dropped: letting the slot name a type's column and the key confirm it for query setup, `has` and structural changes as well, in place of the scan and of any type mask. That reads one key entry where the scan walks the key. It took 0.07–0.13 ms off `update` at the default against a threshold of 0.32 ms, `unchanged` in two captures. Over the `fragmentation` knob (three runs a value, `update` p50 before → after) it read 9.14 → 9.13 ms at 64 archetypes, 10.52 → 10.37 at 488 and 19.15 → 18.74 at 3,189. So the same 250,000 entities cost 10 ms more in 3,189 archetypes than in 64, and cheaper setup recovers 0.4 ms of it. The profile of the starting build put setup at about 55 of the 220 ns an archetype costs per query pass; the rest is consistent with the first cache lines of each column missing, because every column of every archetype is its own allocation and a hardware prefetcher starts only after two or more misses in a pattern. This decision does not reach that cost, and a software prefetch is not callable without `unsafe`.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-036`:** the storage it points to in `DESIGN.md` ("archetype graph, `AnyColumn`/`TypedVec<T>`, lazy edges") is a sorted column `Vec` in place of a hashed column map, and columns are created with the archetype instead of on the first transition into it. The archetype graph, the lazy edges, generational IDs and the query set stand. `DESIGN.md` §ECS follows.
- **Accepted reading, `ecs` `update` p50.** Against the baseline of the pass (`pre-physics-ecs-pass`, five runs a side) the final suite reads `follow` p50 1.95 → 1.74 ms (`improved`) and `update` p50 10.74 → 10.46 ms: −0.28 with a 95% interval of −0.37 to −0.20 against a threshold of 0.32, which is `noisy`, where the plan's final check asked for `improved` or `unchanged`. Three clean captures read −0.26, −0.30 and −0.28. The gain is real and sits just under the 3% threshold, where compare can say neither: the interval reaches past the threshold while the mean stays short of it. The owner accepted the reading as it stands on 2026-10-01; no capture was taken and no code changed to move the label. A later check on this metric should ask for "not `regressed`".
- An archetype always holds all its columns, also when it has no row. `D-084`'s batched insert relies on that for the archetypes a row skips.
- Rows, swap-remove order and archetype creation order are unchanged, so iteration order and every benchmark digest are.
- An archetype is 129 bytes larger (the slots and the shift).
- The flush of `D-084` finds a run's destination columns through the slots. With the key scan in that loop, the build that introduced the slot table read `churn` `flush` p50 at 3.1 ms against 2.7 before; the profile put 25% of the flush's own time on the return from the per-value write call, with equal branch mispredictions, and the cause below that was not found.
- Small `ecs` system rows move by 0.02–0.05 ms with how each benchmark system's row loop compiles around the inlined query setup; `brain` read 2.17 to 2.26 ms across the builds of this pass with no change to its code path. Changes to the query functions should be checked against all 14 rows.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency. The draft plan `docs/plans/debug-cleanup-docs-pass.md` removes `AnyColumn::len`, `AnyColumn::type_id` and `Archetype::id`; this change leaves the three as they were.
- Tests: the column list follows the sorted key and every archetype on a transition path keeps its columns; a merge walk over every pair of subsets of four types; moved and removed rows drop exactly once; archetype ids follow first-transition order; the slot table gives every hash its own slot, falls back when no shift separates a key, and finds every key type and no other over keys of up to 32 types; `get` agrees with `has` for every entity and type over 300 entities in more than 100 archetypes.

## D-084 — Command buffer: typed value queues, function-pointer removals, a reused buffer, one move per run of inserts
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** Recording a command boxes nothing, the app's buffer keeps its storage across frames, and a flush applies consecutive inserts on one entity as one archetype move. The result of a flush is the result `D-039`'s replay gives.

- **Typed value queues.** A `CommandBuffer` holds one `VecDeque<T>` per inserted component type, behind a private `InsertQueue` trait object, found through a pass-through `TypeIdMap`. An insert command is plain data: its target and the index of its queue. The flush handles every insert command exactly once and in recording order, so a command's value is the oldest one left in its queue; an insert whose target died drops that value, which keeps the queue in step.
- **Function-pointer removals.** A removal command holds the entity and a `fn(&mut World, Entity)` monomorphized for the component type.
- **Reused buffer.** `World::flush_reusing(&mut CommandBuffer)` drains the buffer and leaves its allocations: the command `Vec`, the queues and the pending-entity scratch. `App` flushes its buffer through it and puts the same buffer back as the resource. `World::flush(CommandBuffer)` stays for a caller that builds a buffer for one use.
- **Insert runs.** A run is an insert command and the insert commands that follow it directly on the same target. The flush walks the add edges for the run's types in command order, creating every archetype one-by-one inserts would create, in the same order and with no row in the ones the entity skips; moves the row once, from the first archetype to the last; then writes each value in command order, as a push for a type new to the entity and as an overwrite for one it already had or that the run names twice. A run on a dead target is skipped whole.

**Why:** A spawn with k components recorded as k inserts walked the entity through k archetypes and moved k(k−1)/2 values, 15 at the `churn` default of six components, and every insert and removal boxed a command: 43,750 allocations per frame, in a buffer the app dropped and rebuilt every frame (measured 2026-10-01, plan `docs/plans/archive/physics-ecs-perf-pass.md`). Flecs batches deferred operations for the same reason, and Bevy removed its box per command twice over; a packed byte queue like Bevy's needs `unsafe`, typed queues do not.

Each part was judged against the one before it with `just perf compare`, five runs a side:

| Part | `churn` `flush` p50 | p95 | Other |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One move per run of inserts | 3.82 → 3.48 ms | 4.16 → 3.86 ms | churn system rows `unchanged` or `noisy` |
| Typed queues, function pointers, reused buffer | 3.48 → 2.68 ms | 3.86 → 3.07 ms | `churn_spawn` 0.36 → 0.33 ms, `churn_toggle` 0.08 → 0.04 ms |

All cells read `improved`. With `D-083`, `churn` `flush` p50 is 2.69 ms against 8.48 at the baseline of the pass (p95 3.08 against 9.41), and a `churn` frame makes 208 `malloc` calls against 293,960 (exact counts; the flush's profile shows no `malloc` or `free`). On the Criterion bench `command_buffer_flush_1k_spawns` (two inserts per spawn, native flags) the readings are 127.6 µs before, 100.4 with `D-083`'s unboxed moves, 105.1 with insert runs and 66.8 with typed queues: on boxed commands the bookkeeping of a run costs more than the one move it saves for a run of two.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-039`:** "`Vec<Command>`", "a private `ComponentSetter` trait object" and "`Box<dyn FnOnce(&mut World)>`" become plain-data commands, typed value queues and a function pointer; "`App` inserts a fresh buffer before each frame's systems run" becomes one buffer drained every frame. The two-pass flush, replay in registration order, the stale-despawn guard, the dead-entity guard on live inserts and the next-frame visibility rule stand.
- `CommandBuffer::len` still counts one per recorded command.
- A command on another entity, and a removal or a despawn on the same one, ends a run: nothing is reordered.
- Archetype ids, rows and iteration order after a flush are those of one-by-one application, so the benchmark digests are unchanged. A randomized test replays command lists through a reused buffer and through the immediate API and compares every archetype's key, rows and values.
- No bundle-insert API: `World::insert` called k times still moves the row k times.
- A `PendingEntity` is valid for its buffer until the next flush, as before; a reused buffer restarts its pending ids at 0 each flush, which a fresh buffer also did.
- **Accepted regression, `particles` `animate_sprites` p95.** Against the baseline of the pass the final suite reads 0.35 → 0.38 ms (`regressed`, +0.04 with a 95% interval of +0.01 to +0.06 against a threshold of 0.02; p50 `unchanged` at 0.30), from the run change on: 0.34 to 0.36 ms per run before it, 0.36 to 0.41 after. In the same compare the row's other owned metric, `unattributed` p50, improved from 2.37 to 2.10 ms and its `flush` from 0.70 to 0.40 ms. The row's allocation counts did not change with the run change (5,398.6 → 5,399.4 `malloc` calls per frame), its sprite strings are as scattered before as after, disabling transparent huge pages changes nothing, and without telemetry logging both builds take the same cycles; the mechanism behind the one metric was not found. The row's time follows the allocator's state under its own traffic (a `String` clone per animation frame change, a `String` per particle, the extract's per-frame buffers), and that state shifts when allocations are added or removed elsewhere, as this decision does. The owner accepted the regression on 2026-10-01; this bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for. Interned sprite IDs (`docs/perf/benchmarks.md`, "Engine findings") would take the strings out of that system.
- The same row's default extract, which `particles` reports and `gpu` owns, reads 7.03 → 7.68 ms p50 in that compare, from the run change on and again from the typed queues on. `integrated`'s extract reads 2.61 → 2.80 ms, from the typed queues on. Neither change touches the extract. The checks above were made on `particles`; `integrated`'s reading was not investigated. `gpu` and `gpu-throughput`, which own the extract, read no `regressed`. Both readings are recorded here and in `docs/perf/benchmarks.md` and judged nowhere.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency.

## D-085 — Render path: bounded text layout cache; post-chain GPU objects kept between frames
**Amended by D-090:** the "Uniform writes" bullet for stock effects only; the bytes a params buffer holds are tracked per post-stack slot.
**Amended by D-117:** the text layout cache only; it lives in the device-free `TextEngine`, its key gains `TextSection.layout`, and a font change is a `FontEpoch` rise that still drops every layout; its bounds, spare list and unchanged-frame skip stand.
**Amended by D-161:** the frame count only; the map serves two prepared groups, the sections and the world labels, and ages once per frame in which either changed, touching both groups' layouts; its bounds, spare list and unchanged-frame skip stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** The text pipeline keeps about three frames of shaped layouts and does no work for a frame of unchanged text, and the renderer builds the post chain's GPU objects once per target allocation instead of once per frame. Neither changes a pixel.

- **Text layout cache.** `TextLayoutCache` (`crates/tungsten-render/src/text.rs`, no GPU types) owns the font system, the layouts and the sections of the last prepared frame. A section is looked up before any buffer exists; a miss shapes into a recycled buffer or `Buffer::new_empty`, never `Buffer::new`, which shapes an empty line first. A layout unused for more than 2 prepared frames leaves the map, and its buffer goes to a spare list of at most 256. A frame whose sections (layout key, position, color, clip bounds) and viewport equal the last prepared one skips glyphon's `prepare` and the atlas trim and ages nothing; a failed prepare is retried on the next frame. `load_font` and `reload_font` empty the map, the spare list and the last-frame record.
- **Target generation.** `RenderTargetPool::generation()` counts reallocations of the scene targets, and `TargetCache` holds one value with the key it was built for. Keyed on the generation: bloom's uniform buffers and bind groups per post-stack slot (also on the slot's source, destination and mip count), one source bind group per source target shared by every stock effect, SMAA's four bind groups and the present blit's one. `Renderer` drops them all when a resize or a `post_aa` switch reallocates the targets, so the old textures are freed at that point.
- **Uniform writes.** A write is skipped when the buffer already holds the bytes: camera, lights, a material's block, a stock effect's parameters, a bloom stage's block. Payloads that differ within one frame are all still written.
- **`stage_render`** borrows the `PostStack` instead of cloning it every frame.

**Why:** The render-path measurements of 2026-10-01 (plan `docs/plans/gpu-perf-pass.md`, Session A; captures are machine-local) put 87% of `gpu`'s `render_encode` in text. The pipeline built and shaped a buffer before its cache lookup, kept every miss for 360 frames and rebuilt glyph vertices for unchanged text. The cache grew by 139 MiB/s, its prune made one frame in 120 take 41–43 ms, and the fresh pages it took every frame slowed whatever allocated beside it. The post chain created 31 bind groups and 38 buffers per frame in `gpu`.

Each step was judged with `just perf compare` on the full suite, five runs a side:

| Step, and what it is compared with | `gpu` | `integrated` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Text layout cache, against the session baseline | `render_encode` p50 2.72 → 2.22 ms, p95 3.15 → 2.49; `extract` p50 2.00 → 1.30, p95 2.35 → 1.56; peak RSS 720.9 → 169.7 MiB | `total` p50 10.59 → 9.77 ms, p95 11.25 → 10.36; peak RSS 271.8 → 169.1 MiB |
| GPU objects and uniform writes, against the tree before them | `render_encode` p50 2.25 → 2.18 ms | `render_encode` p50 0.95 → 0.90 ms (reported only) |
| Both, against the session baseline | `render_encode` p50 2.72 → 2.18 ms, p95 3.15 → 2.53; `extract` p50 2.00 → 1.27, p95 2.35 → 1.54 | `total` p50 10.59 → 9.64 ms, p95 11.25 → 10.27 |

Every value in the table reads `improved`. `gpu` waits on the GPU, so its `total` p50 and frame rate stayed (11.04 ms, 88 FPS): `render_acquire` took up what the CPU stages gave back. Its tail moved: p99 14.22 → 13.22 ms, the largest frame 23.34 → 14.41 ms, and no frame above 1.5 × p50 in 300 or 900 frames, where there were 2 and 8. Peak RSS at 900 frames is 168.7 MiB against 904.7. `integrated` runs at 104.7 FPS against 94.9. The `extract` gains come from the heap, not from the extract's code: page faults per frame fell from 387 to 0 in `gpu` and from 61 to 0 in `integrated`. A frame of 100 unchanged sections costs 0.03 ms of `render_encode` against 0.51, and 100 changing ones 1.25 ms against 2.32. Per frame `gpu` creates no bind group and 5 buffers and makes 1,928 `malloc` calls against 4,709 (exact counts).

**Consequences:**
- **Pixels.** 19 fixed-frame screenshots of the bench (text changing every frame, static and half-changing text, every AA mode, the full post chain, 720p, one bloom mip, `gpu-throughput`, `particles`, `integrated`, `physics`) are byte-equal to the build before the change. A scripted resize and SMAA switch in example 04 over three post stacks, one with two bloom slots, gives 18 of 18 byte-equal screenshots before and after the GPU-object change. `just visual` passes with its fixture untouched.
- **API.** `PostStackRenderer::record_pass` and `record_bloom_slot`, `BloomPipeline::record_pass`, the three `SmaaPipeline::record_*` functions and `Renderer::update_lights` take `&mut self`. The SMAA functions also take the target pool and their source target, and `record_bloom_slot` the slot index. `RenderTargetPool::generation` is new.
- A zero font size takes a new buffer: `Buffer::set_metrics` panics on one where `Buffer::new_empty` does not.
- **Recorded reading, `ecs` `brain` p50.** Against the session baseline the final suite reads 2.19 → 2.26 ms: `regressed`, +0.07 with a 95% interval of +0.05 to +0.08 against a threshold of 0.066. It is where the linker put the system, whose instructions did not change. Across five builds of that day it reads 2.25–2.26 ms when it starts 16 bytes past a 64-byte boundary (the final tree; the text-cache tree plus one 54-byte function that no frame calls) and 2.17–2.19 ms when it starts at 0 or 48 (the session baseline, the text-cache tree, the final tree plus the extract rewrite below). The padded tree reads `regressed` against the same tree without the function, 2.17 → 2.25 ms, 15 runs a side. `churn` `flush` p50 moved the same way between two steps (2.54 → 2.70 ms, reproduced by the padded tree) and reads `unchanged` against the session baseline (2.71 → 2.70 ms). This bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for; `docs/perf/benchmarks.md` (`ecs`) describes the check.
- **Not adopted: a clear on the present pass.** With `LoadOp::Clear`, wgpu no longer clears the fresh surface texture in a pass of its own (19 render passes against 20 in `gpu`), but the clear costs the same inside the present pass: `gpu.present` p50, which `gpu` owns, read `regressed` at 0.32 → 0.46 ms, and the empty frame's GPU time stayed at 0.58 ms. Only `LoadOp::DontCare` would save it, and its token takes an `unsafe` call, the engine's first outside a test; that needs its own decision.
- **Not merged: a rewrite of the default extract and the tilemap extract** with the same batches and pixels (one pass into buffers kept between frames, 16-byte sort keys, a small asset cache, tileset entries resolved once per map). On the final tree it reads `gpu-throughput` `extract` p50 24.27 → 13.06 ms (38.6 → 68.9 FPS), `integrated` `total` p50 9.64 → 8.54 ms and `particles` `total` p50 11.05 → 6.08 ms; on the tree it was first built on, page faults per frame in `gpu-throughput` fell from 1,596 to 0 and kernel time from 8.59 to 0.20 ms. It leaves `particles` `animate_sprites` p95, which that row owns, `regressed`: 0.37 → 0.41 ms, +0.04 with an interval of +0.02 to +0.05 against a threshold of 0.02, 15 runs a side. That one is the change and not placement: the old extract read every `Sprite` late in the frame and left the column in cache for the system; a padded build at the same alignment reads `unchanged`. Peak RSS also rises by 3 MiB in `particles` and 5 MiB in `integrated`, where the extract keeps its buffers. Accepting an owned regression is the owner's decision (as in `D-084`), so the change stays out of the tree; the plan records where its patch is.
- The two modes of the `gpu` scene pass (about 12% apart between captures) went with the old text path, in the GPU diagnostic run only: 9 of 15 diagnostic runs before, 0 of 30 after. The timing runs' frame time did not move with them. The mechanism was not established.
- One check of the plan stayed unmet: a frame of 100 unchanged sections makes 386 `malloc` calls against a target of 300. The empty frame makes 185 and the benchmark's own `extract_text` 201, so the text pipeline's share is 0 (811 before).
- Tests: the layout map stays within three frames of sections over 1,000 frames of changing text; the spare list is capped; equal sections share a layout; an unchanged frame skips the prepare and any changed draw value does not; evicted text lays out to the same glyphs in a recycled buffer; a font reload empties every cache; a `TargetCache` rebuilds only when its key moves; two bloom slots keep objects of their own; every source of the post ladder has its own cache slot.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency. `D-026`, `D-058`, `D-059` and `D-060` stand as written.

## D-086 — Default extract and tilemap extract: one pass into kept buffers, a key sort, an asset cache
**Amended by D-113:** the default extract's asset lookup only; a sprite's `SpriteAssetId` indexes the registry, and the last-seen memo, the 64-slot cache and their test are gone. The tilemap extract's per-map tileset resolution and the rest stand.
**Amended by D-114:** the default extract's two passes only; pass 1 keys a culled sprite without writing its instance, and pass 2 copies only kept instances and drops the batches that leaves empty. Output for a frame that culls nothing is unchanged, and the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** The default sprite extract and the tilemap extract write into buffers the app keeps between frames and return the batches, instances and pixels they returned before. This merges the rewrite that `D-085` measured and left out ("Not merged"). The owner accepted the three readings that come with it on 2026-10-01.

- **One pass, then a key sort.** `extract_sprites_default` walks the query once and writes, for every visible sprite with a resolved asset, a `SpriteInstance` and a 16-byte key: `(z_order, entity id)` packed into a `u64` whose integer order is painter order, the instance's index, and its batch class (one class per distinct batch key of the frame). The keys are sorted only when the query did not yield them in painter order: `sort_unstable` below 1,024 keys, and above that a least-significant-digit radix sort over the bytes in which keys differ. Entity IDs are unique among live entities, so any correct sort gives the order the stable tuple sort gave. A second pass copies the instances into their batches, a span of equal z and class at once. A frame that is one batch in query order has no second pass: its instance list becomes the batch.
- **Asset lookup.** A sprite ID resolves through the last ID seen, then through a 64-slot direct-mapped cache indexed by a hash of the ID's length and its first and last eight bytes and verified by comparing the strings, then through `AssetRegistry::get_sprite`. The cache lives for one extract call. The 256-byte override block is hashed with one `write`.
- **Kept buffers.** `ExtractScratch`, a world resource around a `RefCell` that is private to the `tungsten` crate, holds the keys, the instances, the classes, the batch list and a pool of instance vectors binned by capacity. `App::new` inserts it, and after `stage_render` the app hands the frame's batches back to it, so a batch of the next frame takes a vector that fits it, in whatever order a custom extract builds batches. Without the resource, and inside a nested extract call, both extracts allocate per call.
- **Tilemap extract.** A tileset entry resolves against the asset registry once per map and call, at its first visible tile. A layer opens one batch per atlas page, in the order its visible tiles, row by row, first use each page.

**Why:** The render-path measurements of 2026-10-01 (plan `docs/plans/gpu-perf-pass.md`; captures are machine-local) put the default extract first in the three sprite-heavy rows. It collected borrowed 48-byte tuples, sorted them and built every vector anew each frame. At 400,000 sprites glibc grew and trimmed the heap for them every frame: 1,610 page faults and 8.81 ms of kernel time per frame in `gpu-throughput`. In `particles` the sort was 29.0% of the samples and the hashing of ID strings 10.7%, and the tilemap extract looked up every visible tile's sprite by string, 0.40 ms in `gpu`. `D-085` measured the rewrite on its final tree and kept it out, because it leaves one owned metric `regressed` and accepting that is the owner's decision.

The merged tree against the tree without it (`just perf compare`, the full suite, five runs a side):

| Row | Owned | Reported |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gpu-throughput` | `extract` p50 24.27 → 13.06 ms, p95 24.80 → 13.47, `improved` | `total` p50 25.88 → 14.58 ms, 38.6 → 68.7 FPS; peak RSS 240.2 → 225.6 MiB; per frame 8.81 → 0.17 ms of kernel time, 1,610 → 2 page faults and 5.0 → 0.0 `brk` calls |
| `gpu` | `extract` p50 1.27 → 0.66 ms, p95 1.54 → 0.87, `improved` | `total` p50 stays at 11.05 ms: the row waits on the GPU, and `render_acquire` takes up what the extract gives back. With four times the tiles in view (`zoom=0.5`) `extract` p50 is 2.67 → 1.18 ms |
| `particles` | `unattributed` p50 2.10 → 2.01 ms, p95 2.69 → 2.47, `improved`; `animate_sprites` p95 `regressed` (below) | `total` p50 11.05 → 6.17 ms, p95 11.79 → 7.13, 89.7 → 158.5 FPS; `extract` p50 7.76 → 2.95 ms |
| `integrated` | `total` p50 9.64 → 8.58 ms, p95 10.27 → 9.20, p99 10.64 → 9.66, all `improved`; jitter 1.00 → 1.08, `unchanged` | 104.7 → 117.7 FPS; `extract` p50 2.13 → 0.96 ms |

Over the eight rows the compare reads 2 `regressed`, 10 `improved`, 28 `unchanged` and 15 `noisy` owned verdicts. Against a suite of the tree without the change captured eight minutes earlier it reads 0, 10, 31 and 14.

**Consequences:**
- **Accepted regression, `particles` `animate_sprites` p95.** Against the tree without the change the suite reads 0.38 → 0.40 ms: `regressed`, +0.02 with a 95% interval of +0.00 to +0.05 against a threshold of 0.02. With 15 runs a side in one sitting it reads 0.37 → 0.40 ms (+0.03, interval +0.02 to +0.04), and it read 0.37 → 0.41 ms when `D-085` measured it; p50 reads `noisy` at 0.30 → 0.32. It is the change and not code placement. The old extract's second pass read every `Sprite` late in the frame and left the column in cache for `animate_sprites`, the benchmark's first system of the next frame; the new extract reads it once, in its first pass. In a profile the system's extra samples sit on its first read of each `Sprite` (54 → 171 in 900 frames), and a build without the change that puts the system at the same alignment reads `unchanged`. The cost moves between stages: the row's `total` p50 falls by 4.88 ms in the same compare. The owner accepted the regression on 2026-10-01, as for the same metric in `D-084`; this bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for.
- **Accepted, peak RSS.** The extract keeps its buffers between frames, so peak RSS rises in two rows: `particles` 133.8 → 136.8 MiB and `integrated` 167.4 → 172.6 MiB when `D-085` measured it, both `regressed` by the RSS threshold (2%, at least 2 MiB). On the merged tree's suite they read 133.8 → 135.7 and 167.4 → 169.9 MiB, both `noisy`. `gpu-throughput` falls by 14.5 MiB.
- **Accepted, two more rows below their calibration band.** `particles` (`total` p95 7.14 ms against a band of 8–16 ms) and `integrated` (9.24 ms against 10–16 ms) now run below the bands their defaults were calibrated to, as `physics`, `physics-sparse` and `churn` have since `D-080`–`D-084`. No default changes and every `workload_version` stays 1. Recalibrating stays an open proposal in `docs/perf/benchmarks.md`.
- **Output.** A golden test keeps the old extract as the reference and compares every batch and instance on seeded worlds (three z layers; lit, material, override and parallax sprites; hidden ones; despawns and respawns), with a digest pinned from the old extract before any edit: 69 batches, 420 instances, `0x7cec8f233d8bfd57`. The 19 fixed-frame screenshots of `D-085` are byte-equal to the build before the change, and `just visual` passes with its fixture untouched. A tile layer that spans two atlas pages yields its batches in first-seen order, where `HashMap::into_values` gave an arbitrary one; tiles of one layer do not overlap, so its pixels are the same.
- **API.** `extract_sprites_default` and `extract_tilemaps` keep their signatures. A caller without an `App` (a test, a headless tool) gets per-call buffers.
- **Recorded reading, `ecs` `buffs` p50.** The suite reads 0.64 → 0.66 ms against the tree without the change: `regressed`, +0.02 with an interval of +0.00 to +0.04 against a threshold of 0.02. It is the row's per-run mode (`docs/perf/benchmarks.md`, `ecs`), not the change: `buffs` reads 0.65–0.67 ms in the runs where `stats_decay` reads 0.30–0.32 ms and 0.62–0.66 ms in the others, in both builds, and the baseline suite caught the mode in one run of five where this one caught it in four. With 15 runs a side in one sitting `buffs` reads `unchanged` (0.65 → 0.65 ms) and the row 0 `regressed`. The same compares read `brain` `improved` (2.26 → 2.16 ms): placement again, the system starts 48 bytes past a 64-byte boundary where `D-085`'s final tree had it at 16.
- **Recorded reading, the scene pass at `zoom=0.5` in the GPU diagnostic run.** Outside the suite, with four times the tiles in view, the diagnostic run's scene pass p50 reads 3.16 → 4.06 ms and `render_span` p50 9.02 → 9.89 ms against the tree without the change in one sitting, both `regressed`, with both p95 `unchanged`. The pass has two values per frame in that run, about 3.1 and 4.2 ms, in both builds. The change shortens the diagnostic frame by the 1.5 ms of extract it saves (15.8 → 14.2 ms), and the share of frames at the higher value rises from about 40% to about 70%, which carries p50 from one value to the other. The GPU's work per frame is the same: without timestamp queries both builds run at 96.4 FPS with 9.45 ms of GPU engine time per frame, and the timing runs' `total` p50 is 9.28 ms in both. The two values remain with desktop compositing suspended; what sets them was not established. At the default `zoom` the same shift runs the other way and crosses no percentile the row owns: the share of frames at the higher value (8.3 ms against 6.9) falls from about 40% to about 14%, and the scene pass (p50 6.90 → 6.87 ms) and `render_span` read `unchanged`. The two scene-pass modes `D-085` describes were shares of such a pass read at p50: in the suite before the text change its frames sit at 6.8–6.9, 7.5–8.1 and 8.2–8.5 ms, with p50 in the middle group in four runs of five. The reading was found after the owner's acceptance and is not one of the three accepted readings; it is recorded as a property of the diagnostic run.
- `integrated`'s RSS growth, which is reported and not judged, reads 0.2–2.3 MiB/s per run against 0–0.3: the kept buffers step to a larger size inside the fitted half of a 3.6 s run. Over 900 frames two of three runs are flat after the first two seconds and the third steps from 171 to 175 MiB.
- After the change the string compare of sprite IDs is the largest cost left in `gpu-throughput`'s extract; only interned sprite IDs would remove it (`docs/perf/benchmarks.md`, "String sprite IDs"). The extract still does not cull.
- Tests: the pinned digest; the new extract against the reference on seeded, shuffled and already-ordered worlds; more asset IDs than cache slots; the radix sort against a comparison sort; single-batch frames as the count changes and as they alternate with split frames; reused storage with unchanged output; a nested extract falling back to buffers of its own; the instance pool handing out fitting vectors and dropping the rest; tile layers against a reference extract, a layer over two pages, one registry lookup per tileset entry.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency. `D-018`, `D-042` and `D-073` stand as written. `D-085` is not edited: its "Not merged" bullet describes the tree before this entry.

## D-087 — The last full-screen stage renders into the swapchain; the present blit stays for capture frames
**Date:** 2026-10-01
**Decision:** On a frame without an armed capture the last full-screen stage renders into the swapchain and the text overlay draws there. The present blit and `PresentSource` remain only on capture frames.

- **Which stage writes the swapchain.** With SMAA, the neighborhood pass. Without SMAA and with a post stack, the last post pass, or bloom's composite when bloom ends the stack. With neither, the scene pass, and with `msaa > 1` the swapchain is the scene pass's resolve target. The text overlay then loads the swapchain. `default_pass_order` takes a `PresentPath` (`Direct` or `Blit`), and the renderer picks `Direct` unless a capture is armed.
- **The first write clears.** The stage that first writes the swapchain begins with `LoadOp::Clear`. The scene pass, SMAA's neighborhood pass and bloom's composite already cleared their target; a stock post pass that ends the stack gets a clear it does not have offscreen. wgpu therefore adds no clear pass of its own for the fresh surface texture. There is no `LoadOp::DontCare`, so no `unsafe`.
- **Capture frames.** A frame with a capture armed (`Renderer::capture_frame`, `TUNGSTEN_CAPTURE_FRAME`) keeps the tail it had: every stage renders offscreen, SMAA's neighborhood pass writes `PresentSource`, the overlay writes the blit's source, the screenshot reads that texture and the present blit copies it into the swapchain. The screenshot's source and its pixels do not change. `PresentSource` stays allocated with the SMAA targets and is written only on those frames.
- **Direct capture.** `Renderer::capture_frame_direct` (`TUNGSTEN_CAPTURE_DIRECT=1` beside `TUNGSTEN_CAPTURE_FRAME`) keeps the direct path for the captured frame and renders it into an offscreen stand-in for the swapchain, which the screenshot reads and a blit then presents. It is a test hook: it lets a pixel test compare the two paths.
- **Timing.** A frame without a capture has no `present` pass in `gpu_passes:`, and `render_span` ends with the text overlay. The `gpu` row no longer owns `gpu.present`.

**Why:** Every frame ended with a full-screen copy of the composed frame into the swapchain: 0.32 ms of GPU time at 1080p in every row and 0.32 of the empty frame's 0.41 ms span (plan `docs/plans/gpu-perf-pass.md`; captures are machine-local). Because that pass loaded the fresh surface texture, wgpu cleared it first in a pass of its own. The copy needed nothing the stages lacked: `SceneColor`, the post targets, `PresentSource` and every pipeline already use the surface format (`D-057`, `D-058`, `D-059`). `D-085` found that the clear costs on this GPU what wgpu's clear pass costs wherever it runs, and that only `LoadOp::DontCare` would save it; this decision keeps a clear and removes the copy.

The tree before against this one in one sitting (medians of three runs; GPU time is the DRM engine time per frame without timestamp queries):

| Frame | Stage that writes the swapchain | `total` p50 | FPS | `render_span` p50 | That stage's pass | GPU time per frame |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Empty `gpu` frame | scene | 0.82 → 0.43 ms | 1,101 → 2,074 | 0.41 → 0.15 ms | 0.06 → 0.13 ms | 0.58 → 0.18 ms |
| Empty frame, MSAA 4 | scene, resolve | 1.25 → 0.85 ms | 733 → 1,070 | 0.81 → 0.57 ms | 0.48 → 0.56 ms | not measured |
| Empty frame, bloom and vignette | vignette | 2.15 → 1.90 ms | 421 → 480 | 1.76 → 1.60 ms | 0.25 → 0.45 ms | not measured |
| Empty frame, SMAA | neighborhood | 2.04 → 1.78 ms | 448 → 516 | 1.64 → 1.51 ms | 0.60 → 0.79 ms | not measured |
| `gpu` | neighborhood | 11.03 → 10.72 ms | 88.1 → 90.2 | 10.52 → 10.39 ms | 0.61 → 0.79 ms | 10.93 → 10.60 ms |
| `integrated` | neighborhood | 8.53 → 8.43 ms | 118.5 → 119.5 | 5.91 → 5.85 ms | 0.62 → 0.80 ms | 6.70 → 6.30 ms |
| `physics` | scene | not captured | 162.3 | no diagnostic run | no diagnostic run | 1.45 → 0.72 ms |

The full suite, five runs a side, reads 3 `regressed`, 6 `improved`, 31 `unchanged` and 14 `noisy` owned verdicts against the tree before, and one without a verdict: `gpu.present`, which compare lists as missing in the candidate.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-059`'s tail description.** "Writing into a new `PresentSource` target that the present blit + screenshot path source" holds on capture frames only; on every other frame the neighborhood pass writes the swapchain. The order stands: SMAA after the post stack, text after SMAA, so SMAA never samples text. `D-059` is not edited.
- **`D-047` stands.** The screenshot reads an offscreen texture and the swapchain is never `COPY_SRC`. A capture frame costs the blit that other frames no longer pay.
- **`D-078`, ownership.** `gpu` owned the present pass at p50. No measured frame has that pass, so the row's owned list drops `gpu.present`; nothing else in the list changes and no `workload_version` moves, because the benchmark's work is the same.
- **Accepted regression, `gpu` `gpu.smaa_neighborhood` p50.** 0.61 → 0.79 ms, +0.18 with a 95% interval of +0.17 to +0.19 against a threshold of 0.02; `integrated`, where the pass is reported only, reads 0.62 → 0.80 ms. The pass does more than it did: it writes the swapchain image, which costs 0.04 ms more than writing `PresentSource` (a build that loads the swapchain instead of clearing it reads 0.65 ms), and it carries the surface clear, 0.14 ms, which wgpu's own pass spent outside every pass metric. `text` reads 0.04 → 0.06 ms for the same reason (`noisy`: the delta equals the threshold). Against that the present pass, 0.32 ms, is gone: the three passes sum to 0.97 → 0.85 ms. The loading build does not avoid the reading and keeps wgpu's clear pass, and its `total` p50 reads 10.81 ms where this decision reads 10.72. The owner accepted the regression on 2026-10-01; this bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for.
- **Accepted regression, `particles` `unattributed` p95.** 2.47 → 2.71 ms in the suite: +0.24 with an interval of +0.19 to +0.29 against a threshold of 0.123. It is the direct path and not code placement: a build of this tree that takes the blit path on every frame, with the same code layout, reads 2.50 → 2.49 ms against the tree before and 2.49 → 2.71 ms against this tree, 15 runs a side in one sitting. p50 reads `unchanged` (2.00 → 2.04 ms). The cost moves between stages: in the same compare the row's `extract` p50 reads 2.94 → 2.82 ms and `animate_sprites` p50 0.32 → 0.30 ms, both `improved`, and `total` reads `unchanged` at p50 (6.15 → 6.17 ms) and p95 (7.08 → 7.08 ms). The mechanism was not established. The owner accepted the regression on 2026-10-01.
- **Accepted, one check of the plan not met.** `integrated` `gpu.render_span` p50 reads `unchanged`, 5.90 → 5.81 ms against a threshold of 0.177, where the plan asked for `improved`: the span now contains the clear that wgpu's pass kept outside it. The row's GPU time per frame falls by 0.40 ms.
- **Recorded reading, `ecs` `bounds_wrap` p50.** 0.27 → 0.31 ms: `regressed`, +0.04 with an interval of +0.04 to +0.05 against a threshold of 0.02. Code placement, as `D-085` describes for this system: the binary's text section starts 16 bytes earlier, which puts the system 32 bytes past a 64-byte boundary where the tree before had it at 48. The blit-path build, which has this tree's layout and the old frame, reads `regressed` against the tree before (0.26 → 0.32 ms) and `unchanged` against this tree (0.32 → 0.32 ms), 15 runs a side.
- `churn` `flush` reads `improved` at p50 2.68 → 2.50 ms. Most of that is the same shift, which moves `World::flush_reusing` from 48 to 16 bytes past a boundary: the blit-path build reads 2.71 → 2.54 ms against the tree before. The direct path adds 2.54 → 2.45 ms (`improved`, 15 runs a side).
- **Passes.** A `gpu` frame records 18 render passes and wgpu adds none, where it recorded 19 and wgpu added one: the copy and the implicit clear both go.
- **Pixels.** Captured through both paths, 16 bench configurations (the stock rows, MSAA 4, the full post chain, no post, GPU depth sort, 720p, the empty frame) give byte-equal images. A grab of the live window equals the capture in four static configurations, one per stage that can write the swapchain. The 19 fixed-frame screenshots of `D-085` are byte-equal to the build before that decision, and the scripted resize and SMAA switches of example 04 give 18 of 18 byte-equal screenshots on the capture path against the tree before and 18 of 18 on the direct path against the capture path, bloom ending the stack included. `just visual` passes with its fixture untouched.
- **API.** `default_pass_order` takes a `PresentPath`. `PresentPath` and `Renderer::capture_frame_direct` are new. `BloomPipeline::record_pass` and `PostStackRenderer::record_bloom_slot` keep their signatures and still take pool targets only.
- Tests: the pass order of the four direct cases, of a capture frame and of both paths across the MSAA, depth-sort, stack-length and AA matrix (one stage before the overlay writes the swapchain, it clears, the overlay loads); the query count with and without the blit; `direct_and_capture_paths_draw_the_same_image` in `examples/02_bench/tests/visual_regression.rs`, behind the visual gate, for the scene pass, its MSAA resolve, a stock post pass and SMAA with and without a post stack. Bloom ending the stack is covered by the pass-order test, the bloom smoke row and the example 04 screenshots above, not by that pixel test: the benchmark has no such chain.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency. `D-047`, `D-057`, `D-058` and `D-078` stand as written; `D-059` and `D-085` are not edited.

## D-088 — Frame dt is capped at 0.1 s
**Amended by D-129:** the single dt only; a `Time` resource carries the real clock (this cap, the smoke pin and the harness's as-given dt stand), the game clock, scale, pause and the fixed-step accumulator, and `DeltaTime.dt` reads the fixed step inside `fixed_update` and the game dt elsewhere until the freeze removes it.
**Amended by D-094:** the physics step no longer takes the whole capped dt; one `physics_step` call advances at most `PhysicsConfig::max_step_dt` (1/30 s by default). The table below holds for the scenes it lists (gravity 900, a 5-box stack and a 30-circle pile), not for deep piles or gravity 3,600. The cap, its constant and the smoke pin stand, and every other system still sees the capped dt.
**Amended by D-110:** the headless harness's dt only; `tungsten::testing::Harness` hands systems the dt a test sets as given, without the cap, as the smoke pin does. The window loop's cap, its constant and the smoke pin stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** `DeltaTime.dt` is the time since the previous frame, capped at `MAX_DT_SECS = 0.1` (`crates/tungsten/src/app.rs`, `frame_dt_secs`). A smoke run keeps its pinned 1/60 s. The cap is a constant beside `SMOKE_MODE_FIXED_DT_SECS`, not a `tungsten.json` field.

**Why:** Outside smoke mode the frame's dt was the raw wall-clock time, so a stall (a dragged window, a debugger stop, a slow load) handed every system and the physics step the whole stall as one step. Benchmarks, smoke runs and the pixel test pin dt, so none of them saw it. Measured on settled awake bodies on a static floor (gravity 900, 4 substeps):

| Scene | One step of 0.05 s | 0.1 s | 0.2 s | 0.5–1 s | 2 s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 5-box stack, residual speed | 13.4 px/s | 40.1 | 117.7 | 258–282 | 1 of 5 through the floor |
| 30-circle pile, residual speed | 12.7 px/s | 39.5 | 112.9 | 179–213 | 6 of 30 through the floor |

A box driven at 560 px/s and sleeping piles are unaffected at every stall. 0.1 s keeps a pile on the floor and leaves it about 40 px/s to settle; 0.05 s would slow every game that runs under 20 FPS.

**Consequences:**
- A frame longer than 0.1 s advances the simulation by 0.1 s, so below 10 FPS game time runs slower than wall time. Telemetry still reports the real `total` and `interval`.
- Amends the "Variable-dt with substep cap" known limit of `D-033`: the dt is still variable and still split across the fixed substeps of `D-064`. A fixed-step accumulator with render interpolation changes the core/render seam (extract needs previous transforms) and is a decision for the 1.0 criteria plan.
- Tests: `frame_dt_caps_a_long_stall`, `frame_dt_passes_a_normal_frame_through` and `smoke_frame_dt_is_pinned_whatever_elapsed` in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/app.rs`; `one_capped_stall_step_keeps_a_settled_pile` in `crates/tungsten-core/tests/physics_tunneling.rs` pins the rationale with one 0.1 s step.
- No new dependency. `D-038` and `D-064` stand as written.

## D-089 — Manifest roots: references validate on the merged graph, and a reload rebuilds it
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** Material → shader references are checked once, on the merged manifest set, and a manifest reload rebuilds that set from every root.

- **Validation.** `ResolvedManifest::load_and_merge_many` parses and resolves each root, merges them (duplicate IDs stay fatal, `D-017`) and then validates the merged graph. A missing shader is reported for the material with the smallest ID, so the error does not depend on map order. `ResolvedManifest::load` keeps its strict single-root contract, and `merge` still checks against the shaders merged so far.
- **Reload.** `asset_loader::reload_manifest` takes the root list, rebuilds the merged graph with `load_and_merge_many`, diffs it against the registries and stores it as `LoadedManifest`. A failure logs and keeps the last good state.
- **Routing.** The reload set is `App::set_manifest_roots`' list when the app declared one, else the path given to `enable_hot_reload`. An edit to any manifest of the set reloads once per drained batch of events.
- **Watcher.** `App::enable_hot_reload` only records its arguments; `App::run` builds the watcher, with the input map and every root manifest as extra files, so the order of `enable_hot_reload` and `set_manifest_roots` does not matter. `HotReloadWatcher::new` skips an extra file that a recursive root already covers.

**Why:** Each root was validated before the merge, so a material in one root could not name a shader in another, and with the per-root check gone the root order would still have decided. A reload loaded one root over the merged `LoadedManifest`: every sprite of the other roots logged "removed — keeping stale", and the stored graph shrank to that one root. `App` compared changed paths with a single manifest path, so an edit to the shared manifest was dropped even though its directory was watched.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-017`, `D-052` and `D-053` as their marker lines say. The reload matrix in `DESIGN.md` loses its two limitation sentences.
- The "Hot reload watching" log lines appear when the app runs, not when it is configured.
- Layer 1 (`crates/tungsten-core/tests/manifests.rs`) still loads each shipped manifest alone, so a shipped cross-root reference would fail there. None exists; `docs/known-issues.md` records the limit.
- Tests: `merge_material_resolves_shader_across_roots` (both root orders) and `merge_material_shader_missing_from_every_root_is_fatal` in `crates/tungsten-core/tests/composition.rs`; the reload set and the path test in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/app.rs`; `extra_file_under_a_recursive_root_needs_no_watch_of_its_own` in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/hot_reload.rs`. Checked live on example 01: an edit to `assets/manifest.json` logged one "Manifest reloaded from" line naming both roots and no "keeping stale" line.
- `reload_manifest` takes `&[PathBuf]` where it took one path. No new dependency.

## D-090 — Stock post-effect parameters belong to the post-stack slot
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** `PostStackRenderer` keeps one params UBO and bind group per post-stack slot (`ParamSlots`), built on first use and kept between frames. The params layout is shared by all 17 stock effects, so a slot serves whichever effect sits in it. A `StockPipeline` holds its pipeline only, and `PostStackRenderer::record_pass` takes the slot index.

**Why:** Each stock effect had one params buffer. Two passes of one effect with different parameters queued two writes to that buffer before submission, and both draws read the second. The write skip of `D-085` could not help, because the two payloads alternated. Bloom already kept its objects per slot.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-058` (who owns a stock effect's params) and the "Uniform writes" bullet of `D-085`: the bytes a buffer holds are tracked per slot, so an unchanged stack still writes nothing.
- A resize does not drop the slots: they hold no target views.
- Pixels of every existing stack are unchanged: the reference screenshots of example 04 (`empty`, `all`, bloom only) are byte-equal before and after, and `just visual` passes with its fixture untouched. The suite after this change and `D-091` reads no `regressed` and no `improved` owned verdict against the tree before (`D-078`, five runs a side).
- Tests: `two_passes_of_one_effect_keep_params_of_their_own`, `swapped_params_are_written_to_both_slots` and `a_far_slot_leaves_the_others_alone` in `crates/tungsten-render/src/tests/post.rs`, without a device. `repeated_effect_keeps_its_own_params` in `examples/04_shader_playground/tests/post_regression.rs` captures the fixtures `fade_pair` and `fade_twice` and needs more than half the pixels to differ; it runs behind `TUNGSTEN_VISUAL_REGRESSION`, and `just visual` runs it.
- Not covered: material override batches. `DESIGN.md` still records that distinct override batches share uniform storage.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency.

## D-091 — Stock post pipelines build from the shader cache and rebuild on reload
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** The 17 stock post effects take the same reload path as the sprite, SMAA, bloom and lit-sprite shaders.

- **Table.** `STOCK_SHADERS` (`crates/tungsten-render/src/post/mod.rs`) lists the 17 manifest IDs with their compiled-in sources; an effect module holds only its `NAME` and `WGSL`. A manifest ID equals `PostPass::kind_name`.
- **Seeding.** `Renderer::new` seeds the `ShaderModuleCache` from the table before it builds the post stack, as `D-057` describes for the other built-in shaders, and the stock pipelines are built from the cached modules. The 17 take the render-side IDs 11–27, and shaders loaded later start at 28; the renderer's constants 0–10 do not move.
- **Reload.** `upload_shader` and `reload_shader` share one helper, `apply_shader_module`, which carries the old chain and one new branch: a stock ID rebuilds that effect's pipeline (`PostStackRenderer::rebuild_stock_with_module`). The new pipeline is built before the old one is replaced, as the bloom stages do.

**Why:** The stock pipelines were built from `include_str!` with a module of their own, and no reload branch matched a stock name. An edit was validated, committed to the cache and logged as reloaded while the frame did not change. At startup the loader also validated and committed all 17, because the cache held no seed to compare with.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-057` and `D-058` as their marker lines say. The reload matrix in `DESIGN.md` gains a stock row and loses the paragraph that said the reload was not connected.
- Body edits only, as for every shader: a signature or layout change still needs a rebuild.
- Core and render still allocate shader IDs independently and the umbrella bridges by name; that finding stays in `docs/known-issues.md`.
- Pixels are unchanged: the reference screenshots of example 04 are byte-equal, and `tests/shader_coverage.rs` still validates both trees and their mirrors.
- Tests: `stock_shader_table_covers_every_post_pass`, whose `match` over `PostPass` has no wildcard, so a new variant stops compiling until it is listed. `stock_shader_body_edit_changes_the_frame` (Unix, behind `TUNGSTEN_VISUAL_REGRESSION`, run by `just visual`) starts example 04 in a staged asset tree with an edited `fade.wgsl` and needs more than half the pixels to differ from the unstaged capture. Checked live on example 01: an edit to `assets/shaders/stock/vignette.wgsl` logged "shader 'vignette' reloaded" and changed the frame.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency.

## D-092 — Physics arrival pass: a body the solver pushed is clamped against what it reaches
**Amended by D-154:** the immovable members only; a kinematic body is one, held where its substep began, and is never listed, since no solve changes its velocity.
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** Each substep checks the velocity the solver handed a body against the neighbours that velocity reaches, and corrects the body's move.

- **Admitted velocity.** The gravity loop stores each awake dynamic proxy's velocity as the narrow phase saw it.
- **Flag.** The position-integration loop lists a proxy whose velocity differs from the admitted one by more than `2 · linear_slop / sub_dt`. Two bodies under that threshold change a pair's closing travel by at most `4 · linear_slop`, the admission slack of `D-064`. The pass runs only when a body was listed.
- **Pairs.** Each listed body queries the pair grid, and the repair grid for proxies repaired since the last build, around where it was when the substep began, grown by `|v| · sub_dt + 4 · linear_slop`. Every candidate, static, sleeping or dynamic, goes through the narrow phase with the margin `D-064` gives the current velocities. A hit is an arrival pair: its normal and its allowance, the closing speed that brings it to touching within the substep.
- **Order.** A pair's mover is the member whose own velocity closes it faster. A body pushed by a mover sits one level deeper; pairs are sorted by the mover's depth, then lighter targets first: pushers first, walls last. The sort is stable and depths are relaxed at most 4 times.
- **Forward sweep.** A pair closing faster than its allowance takes the impulse that removes the excess, split by inverse mass; a static or sleeping member has none. A neighbour this pushes past the threshold joins the list for the next round, at most 4 rounds.
- **Backward sweep.** In reverse order, a pair that is tight or still closing marks its mover as pressed on its target. A mover still closing is clamped against the target's pressed chain as one body: the chain's combined mass takes the impulse. A chain that ends at a static or sleeping body takes none, so the pusher stops behind it.
- **Move.** Every body whose velocity the pass changed is placed again from where the substep began. Relax, restitution and the statics sweep follow as before.

The pair list, the contacts, their impulses and the events are left alone, and the regular repair of `D-081` picks a new pair up before the next narrow phase. A clamp is inelastic. The pass wakes nobody: a sleeper is immovable in it and the regular wake test fires in the next substep. Every loop runs in proxy, grid or sorted order.

**Why:** The narrow phase admits contacts for the velocities it sees, and the solver runs one velocity iteration per substep. A body the solver then accelerates may have no pair with the wall behind it, or a contact with it that was solved before the push arrived, and position integration carried it through, at once or a few pixels per substep. The statics sweep of `D-064` fires only above one half-extent of travel and never against a dynamic body. Probed on 2026-10-02 (512 cases per spawn order: a resting body, a pusher of 1 to 1,000 times its mass at 960 to 15,360 px/s, a static or dynamic gate of two thicknesses behind it):

| Variant | Pusher spawned first | Pushed body asleep | Pusher spawned last |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tree before | 67 | 95 | 104 |
| Regular contact list sorted wall-last, nothing else | 50 | 62 | 49 |
| Each flagged body clamped against its neighbours in grid order | 25 | 121 | 118 |
| Arrival pass, forward sweep only | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Arrival pass with the backward sweep (this decision) | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| This decision with a threshold of `4 · linear_slop` | 1 | 0 | 0 |

The forward sweep alone turns "the body through the gate" into "the pusher inside the body" (71 to 96 of 512 per order); the backward sweep stops the pusher behind it. Slow pushes at 60 to 960 px/s crossed a thin gate in 41 of 320 cases, 9 of them a static gate.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-064` and the substep order of `D-063`** as their marker lines say. `D-075`, `D-080` and `D-081` stand: the pass relies on their grids and budgets and changes none of them.
- **Results change.** The determinism test's hash moves from `0x0b8e2a27f2961571` to `0x088ec07a73c1b168` and stays bit-identical between runs; containment stays at 0 of 3,000 escaped over 2,400 steps. The three physics-bearing benchmark digests change: `physics` `86ffcabcdb15eed1` → `c10885dddd6115a4`, `physics-sparse` `5899f9c8a69d79b1` → `9df5ade5305646dc`, `integrated` `5f031f4d947964cb` → `334b519e18b543d7`. No `workload_version` moves: the benchmarks' work is the same. The other five rows keep their digests.
- **Accepted regression, `physics` and `physics-sparse` `physics_step`.** Against the tree before, five runs a side (`D-078`):

  | Row | Owned metric | Before | After | Change |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `physics` | `physics_step` p50 | 5.61 ms | 6.08 ms | +8.3% |
  | `physics` | `physics_step` p95 | 5.73 ms | 6.26 ms | +9.2% |
  | `physics` | `update` p95 | 5.79 ms | 6.31 ms | +9.1% |
  | `physics-sparse` | `physics_step` p50 | 3.45 ms | 3.63 ms | +5.4% |
  | `physics-sparse` | `physics_step` p95 | 3.50 ms | 3.69 ms | +5.5% |

  It is the pass's own work, not its bookkeeping. A build of this tree with the threshold set to infinity has the same layout and never runs the pass; it reads `unchanged` in both rows (−0.7% and −0.9%). Both rows are collision storms: `physics` lists about 195 of its 8,000 balls per substep, with about 22 grid candidates and 2.7 pairs each, and `physics-sparse` about 255 with 6 candidates each. About two thirds of the pass's samples are the grid queries and the candidate loop. `integrated` lists about 5 bodies per substep and reads `unchanged` on all four owned metrics; a settled pile lists none. This bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for, and the owner accepted the regression by landing this decision.
- **`narrow_phase` is `#[inline(always)]`.** With the pass as its second caller the compiler turned the pair loop's narrow phase into a call, which alone cost `physics` about 5.6% of `physics_step`.
- **Recorded reading, `ecs` `brain` and `bounds_wrap` p50.** 2.16 → 2.24 ms and 0.26 → 0.31 ms, both `regressed`. Code placement, as `D-085` and `D-087` describe for these systems: `brain` moves from 48 to 16 bytes past a 64-byte boundary and `bounds_wrap` from 0 to 32. The build with the threshold at infinity has the same addresses and reads the same two verdicts against the tree before, 2.16 → 2.24 ms and 0.26 → 0.31 ms.
- **Not closed,** and recorded in `docs/known-issues.md`: a pusher of 1,000 times the body's mass at 120 to 480 px/s can still crush the body through a 4 px dynamic gate (6 of 320 slow-push cases in the probe, 41 before), and at 15,360 px/s a pusher spawned last can end past the body it pushed (17 of 512). Nothing passes a static gate in either.
- **Not taken:** pairs from the substep's contacts with a grid query only for a body that gained speed. Estimated from the profile at about +6% and +2%, it narrows the completeness argument and leaves `physics` regressed.
- Tests: `pushed_body_never_crosses_a_gate` (1,536 cases in three spawn orders; 246 failed before), `slow_push_never_crosses_a_static_wall` (320 cases; 9 failed before) and the guard `free_train_keeps_its_order_and_momentum` in `crates/tungsten-core/tests/physics_tunneling.rs`; `settled_stack_never_enters_the_arrival_pass` and `pushed_body_arrives_at_a_static_wall_and_stops_its_pusher` in `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/step.rs`. The pair-contact and warm-start oracles hold with the pass running.
- No `unsafe`, no new dependency, no API change. `D-033`, `D-065`, `D-066` and `D-067` stand as written.

## D-093 — M31 instanced mesh particles and screen transitions
**Amended by D-129:** the transitions' clock only; a `Transition` advances on `Time::real_delta()`, so a fade runs over a paused game; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-02
**Decision:** Phase 4 closes with two features.

**Mesh particles.** A particle config may draw each particle as an instanced triangle mesh instead of a sprite quad.

- **Mesh asset.** The manifest section `particle_meshes` holds meshes inline: `vertices` (`[x, y]` in mesh-local pixels, the origin at the particle position) and `u16` `indices` (triangle list). There is no mesh file type. A mesh is validated when its manifest loads: 3 to 65,536 vertices, indices non-empty, a multiple of 3 and in range, every coordinate finite. An invalid mesh is `ManifestError::InvalidParticleMesh`; a duplicate ID across roots is fatal (`D-017`).
- **IDs.** `ParticleMeshAssetId` is `AssetId<ParticleMesh>`. `ParticleMeshRegistry`, a `World` resource, maps name, ID and geometry, and a name keeps its ID when its mesh is replaced.
- **Config.** `ParticleConfig.render` is `{"kind": "quad"}` (the default) or `{"kind": "mesh", "mesh": "<id>"}`. `sprite` is required for a quad config and optional for a mesh config. The loader checks the config → mesh reference where it checks config → sprite.
- **Entity.** A mesh particle is `Particle + Transform + MeshParticle + Visibility`. It has no `Sprite`, so the sprite extract never draws it as a quad. The emit system resolves the mesh name once per emitting emitter per frame; an unknown name logs a warning and that emitter emits nothing. `spawn_mesh_particle_via` is the direct-spawn counterpart of `spawn_particle_via`.
- **Draw.** `extract_mesh_particles` (engine-owned, no setter) yields one `MeshParticleBatch` per mesh. `Renderer::update_mesh_particles`, called beside `update_lights`, writes every instance into one kept instance buffer that grows by doubling. `MeshParticlePipeline` issues one `draw_indexed` per batch inside the scene pass, after the sprites and before the debug quads, with the quad camera bind group. An instance is 24 bytes: position, scale, rotation and an `Unorm8x4` color. `mesh_particle.wgsl` is internal, like `quad` and `debug_line`.

**Screen transitions.** A state change can run behind a full-screen effect.

- **Types** (`tungsten::transition`). `Transition` holds an `out_effect`, an `in_effect`, a duration per phase and an `Easing`. `TransitionEffect` is a closed enum: `Fade`, `WipeRadial`, `Dissolve`, `Pixelate`. `TransitionPhase` is `Out` or `In`; the roadmap's `Swap` is the boundary frame between them.
- **Requests.** `StateStack::request_push_transition`, `request_pop_transition` and `request_replace_transition` return `bool`. One transition runs at a time: a request made while one is queued or active returns `false` and its state is dropped. `is_transitioning`, `transition_state`, `transition_cover` and `transition_pass` read the state.
- **Dispatcher order per frame.** (1) Plain `request_*` commands apply at once, even during a transition. (2) A queued transition activates when none is active. (3) The active transition advances by `DeltaTime`; on the frame `Out` completes, its command runs through the same code as a plain request, so hooks, scene despawn and order follow `D-046`, and leftover time is dropped so that frame draws fully covered. (4) The top state updates: the old state during `Out`, the new one during `In`.
- **Cover.** A cover of 0 leaves the frame untouched and 1 hides it as far as the effect can. The four stock shaders share no progress convention, so each effect maps its cover:

  | Effect | Mapping |
  | --- | --- |
  | `Fade` | `progress = cover` |
  | `WipeRadial` | `progress = (1 − cover) · min(1, (far + softness) / 1.5)` and `softness · (1 − cover)`, with `far` the largest distance from the center to a UV corner. The shader is inverted (1 shows the frame); scaling by `far` starts the wipe at the farthest corner, and the shrinking softness closes the soft dot the shader would leave at the center |
  | `Dissolve` | `progress = cover` |
  | `Pixelate` | block size `1 + (max(max_block_px, 1) − 1) · cover`; it never hides the frame |

- **Frame composition.** `App::stage_render` appends the transition's pass to a copy of the user's `PostStack` and draws with the copy. The pass is the last post-stack slot, so it runs before the SMAA tail and the text overlay.

**Why:**
- A triangle is three vertices. `materials` is already inline (`D-058`), so the manifest watcher, the merge and the duplicate-ID check cover a mesh; a file type would need a loader, a watcher route and a coverage rule in `scripts/check-repo.py`.
- `D-051` rejected a pool because it would be a second source of truth beside the entities. The entities stay: a mesh particle is spawned and despawned through `CommandBuffer`, counted by `particle_count_refresh_system` and bounded by `max_alive` and `ParticleBudget`. Instancing is draw-side only.
- `MeshParticle` in place of `Sprite` leaves the sprite extract untouched. A `Renderer` method keeps the seven-argument `render_frame_full` signature. An internal shader has no author-facing parameters and needs no seeded render-side ID (`D-091` fixed 0–27).
- A closed `TransitionEffect` instead of a raw `PostPass`: the four effects need the mapping above and the other 14 variants have no notion of progress.
- The transition lives in `StateStack` because the dispatcher already owns that resource, and the seam keeps it in the umbrella crate. The pass is injected in `stage_render` because a state's `on_enter` may clear or rebuild `PostStack`; the pass must survive that and needs no cleanup.

**Consequences:**
- **Amends `D-051`** as its marker line says: the component set only. `D-046` stands: transitions extend the dispatcher without changing its hook matrix.
- **Hot reload:**

  | Edit | Result |
  | --- | --- |
  | Manifest adds a mesh | Registered and uploaded; configs loaded afterwards may name it |
  | Manifest changes a mesh's vertices or indices | Re-uploaded under the same ID; live particles draw the new geometry on the next frame (geometry is not snapshotted, unlike the config `Arc` of `D-050`) |
  | Manifest removes a mesh | Warning; the stale mesh stays |
  | Manifest holds an invalid mesh | The reload fails in `load_and_merge_many`, is logged, and the last good graph stays |
  | A config file changes `render` | `reload_particle` validates and swaps the `Arc`; live emitters keep their snapshot |
  | `mesh_particle.wgsl` | Internal shader; rebuild |

- **Recorded limits,** also in `docs/known-issues.md`: a transition does not cover screen-space text, which draws after the post stack (a game fades its own text with `1 − transition_cover()`, as example 03 does); mesh particles draw above every sprite, so `z_order` cannot place them between sprites; at an odd window size the radial wipe leaves the center pixel at half brightness on the fully covered frame; pixelate never hides the frame.
- **Example 03** drops its tweened overlay sprite and `TweenComplete`-driven replace: menu → gameplay fades, gameplay → pause pixelates, pause → gameplay wipes, gameplay → menu dissolves, and pause → menu stays a hard cut.
- **Accepted regression, `particles` `stage.unattributed` p50.** Against the tree before, five runs a side (`D-078`): 2.07 → 2.14 ms (+3.1%) and `regressed` in the suite taken after the mesh particle work; +0.05 to +0.07 ms and `noisy` in three more pairs of one sitting; 2.07 → 2.10 ms and `noisy` in the final suite. The effect sits at the threshold. The digest and the counters are unchanged.
