The plan's two critique rounds replaced a bundle `describe`/`put` contract, whose disagreement would have left a column short of the entity count, with one `put` staged through the machinery the command-buffer flush already uses (`D-084`), so no description exists to disagree with and the World is consistent on any panic by construction. The thirteen query tests read every arity-named shape row for row against `World::get` on a 64-entity, 16-archetype world; the eight bundle tests pin one move, the overwrite rules, `Chain`, `RigidBodyBundle`'s three and four components, the buffer forms flushing to the same archetype and the World unchanged after a `put` that panics; an order probe over example 01's level read the same 165-body sequence and hash (`5d2711f1d14e5d19`) before and after the bundles. The first release sitting on the tuple form read `gpu` `stage.extract` p50 0.636 → 0.716 ms (+12%, τ 0.050) and `gpu-throughput` 12.10 → 12.63 ms (+4%, τ 0.36) `regressed` on both direct pairs: `perf annotate` of the extract-shape criterion pair (`entity_triple_opt2_read`, +163% / +101% a row at native and aligned flags) shows the tuple row iterator (`RowsN`, `OptionalRows`) spilling its state to the stack, 5–7 stack references a row, in every variant tried (as is, a `fold` on the adapters, left-nested zips, both, both plus a row-range-led zip), where the arity loop is 43 instructions on one index register with no stack reference. A hand-written `for` over the slice form carried its accumulator through a stack slot too (+10.1% native); the nested `for_each` over the slices compiles to the arity loop (43 instructions, no stack load or store, the accumulator in a register) and reads within 1% of it at native flags, so the extract takes that form, and the second sitting read both `extract` rows `unchanged` in every pair. The lone column's +0.22 ns a row is LLVM's layout of a lone `IterMut` loop (`D-130`), and the control that was to measure a fix turned out to vectorize, so the step stopped as the plan wrote it rather than judge a candidate against a control that reads 70% under the arity function. `churn_scan` p50 read 0.312 → 0.340 ms (+0.028 [+0.022, +0.034], τ 0.020) `regressed` on both direct pairs and `unchanged` on the aligned pair with the function's address moving 0 → 16 modulo 64, the placement reading `benchmarks.md` describes. Digests matched 8 of 8 in every compare, the baseline A/A read 0 `regressed`, `ecs` `update` read `unchanged`, and `just smoke`, `just visual` (`gpu-visual.png` byte-equal) and `just physics-release` (the determinism, containment and tunneling tests) passed, so the hashes are unchanged.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-130` as its marker line says: the accessor and `query_entities` join its scope, so eleven functions are deprecated; the lone column's cost is recorded, not removed; the default extract's hot read takes the slice form; `RigidBodyBundle` lands without `PrevPosition`, which W3b adds. `D-005`, `D-066` and `D-084` stand.
- The break ledger: the `query`/`query_mut` row and the bundle-insert row landed at 0.54 (M40); the arity-named row reads deprecated at 0.54, removal at W4b.
- Pinned by `tests::ecs::query` (13 tests, three `should_panic` on the full query type), `tests::ecs::bundle` (8), the world tests' arity-against-tuple comparisons and the shims' own tests under `#[allow(deprecated)]`, the command buffer's bundle test, example 01's level, ball-pit and player tests (they pin positions), the template's tests and the `api/` snapshots, which print no attributes, so the deprecations show in the source only.
- Accepted readings: `bounds_wrap` p50 +0.046 ms on the aligned pair (`D-130`'s budget); `churn_scan` p50 +0.028 ms on the direct pairs as placement. The `tuple_query_bench` criterion bench (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target/criterion-native`) holds thirteen pairs: the ten shapes, with `single_mut`, `single_read` and `entity_mut` controlled by the arity loop rebuilt on the slice forms, and the extract shape as tuple, slice `for` and slice `for_each` pairs; `single_mut_with` is two-valued on both sides and is quoted, not judged.
- Not decided here: the 212 `get_resource(..).expect(..)` sites in the engine crates and examples; example 01's collect-then-get `query_entities` callers; a `Component` marker that would let bundle tuples nest bundles (W16a); the lone column's layout pass and the tuple row loop's codegen pass, which W15's follow-up 1 places in C2 with the per-variant hot loops saved under `perf-runs/20261006-m40-tuple-queries/step5-retry/bench/` (machine-local).

## D-136 — Fable 5.1 is recommended only for level C releases, gates and spikes; level A and B releases take Opus 5.5 at max
**Superseded by D-151:** the Opus 5.5 max clause only; level A and B releases take Opus 5.5 at xhigh for every stage; the Fable clause stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-06
**Decision:** `tier` in `scripts/roadmap.py` returns Fable 5.1 at xhigh for complexity 4, or 3 with effort XL, unless the stop is a release at level A or B; those take Opus 5.5 at max, as complexity 2–3 already do. Complexity 4 now joins 2–3 for Opus max. Level C releases, gates, spikes and stops with no level keep Fable, so W15a, W15b, W1 M1 and W1 M2 are unchanged; W3b, a level B release at complexity 4, moves to Opus 5.5 at max for `plan`, `approve` and `run`.

**Why:** The owner is running low on Fable usage on 2026-10-06. A scan of the 80 latest sessions found Fable writing about 7k output tokens per tool call against Opus's 3.3k (503k against 304k a session), while Opus carried the level B milestones M33–M36 and M39 at 200–330 tool calls each and released them. W3b's design was settled at the frame-loop gate (`D-129`) and its step and done-when sit in w03 step 2, so its plan is audit and sequencing, with the critique as a second-model check. Fable's edge is likelier on new public API shape, which is level C. The scan read counts, not outcomes; the first W3b critique's findings are the check.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-118` as its marker line says.
- The catalog hash changes, so the next sync rewrites `meta/catalog`.
- Recommendations stay advice: the owner may start any session on Fable.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: `tier` by level for complexity 4 and for 3 with XL.
- Open limit: no outcome comparison of Fable and Opus exists; revisit after W3b's critique and run.

## D-137 — W3b landed (M41): the fixed-step accumulator in `Time`, step views for input edges and event queues, the `time` section, `PrevPosition` in every bundle body with the snapshot first in `fixed_update` and an interpolating `physics_sync`, `FrameTimings::fixed_steps` with summed and zero-step rows
**Amended by D-139:** clause (3)'s rule for events sent outside the steps only; such an event reaches a `fixed_update` reader of `iter` in the first step after it, since a frame with no step holds its queues while the game clock advances (at most 256 frames); the step views and the rest stand.
**Amended by D-146:** clause (2)'s rule for a frame with no step only; a frame whose game clock stood still while real time ran (game dt zero, real dt above zero) drops the fixed view's edges, so a press or release made while paused or at scale 0 reaches no step; a frame that advances, or has no time at all, keeps them for the next step; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Decision:** The fixed-step half of `D-129`'s `Time`, with the choices it left to W3b. (1) `Time` holds the accumulator: `fixed_step()` 1/60 s (`1.0_f32 / 60.0`, the smoke pin bit for bit) and `max_steps_per_frame()` 2, set by `set_fixed_step` (panics unless finite and positive) and `set_max_steps_per_frame` (panics on 0) from the next `advance_frame`. `advance_frame` adds the game dt to an `f32` sum that saturates at `f32::MAX`, subtracts one step at a time while a whole step is left, fewer than the bound have run and the subtraction still changes the sum, then drops the remaining whole steps (`acc − acc % step`, reported by `dropped_this_frame()`) and keeps the fraction: `fixed_steps_this_frame()`, and `alpha()` in `[0, 1)`, the fraction over the step. A paused frame adds nothing, runs no step and holds `alpha`. `delta()` is the step between `enter_fixed_step()` and `leave_fixed_steps()` and the game dt otherwise, so a world driven by hand that never calls them reads the game dt as before. `interpolate()` is on unless `set_interpolate(false)`. (2) `InputState::set_fixed_view(on)` and `end_fixed_step()`: each press and release enters the frame's edges and a fixed view; with the view on, `just_pressed`, `just_released` and the mouse and scroll edge queries answer from it, and `ActionMap` with them; `end_fixed_step` clears it after each step and `begin_frame` clears the frame's edges only, a scroll release entering both. A frame with no step keeps its edges for the next step, and a frame with two shows each edge to the first. Level queries, the cursor and the deltas stay per frame. (3) `World::set_fixed_event_view(on)` and `World::end_fixed_step_events()` reach every registered queue through a table beside `flush_events`': in the frame's first step a queue reads as before, in a later step `iter`, `iter_current`, `len` and `is_empty` see only the events sent since the step before it ended, and outside the steps the whole frame. An event sent outside the steps reaches a `fixed_update` reader only in a later step of its frame (`iter_current`) or the next frame's first step (`iter`), and otherwise rotates out unseen; a fixed-stage system reads what `fixed_update` sends, and one that needs another stage's events reads them in `update` or carries them in a resource. (4) `tungsten.json`'s `time` section, `TimeConfig { fixed_step_hz: u32, max_steps_per_frame: u32, interpolate: bool }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, 60, 2, true) at `Config.time`; `TimeConfig::validate` returns `ConfigError::InvalidValue` for `time.fixed_step_hz` below 30 (a step past `max_step_dt`'s default 1/30 s) and `time.max_steps_per_frame` of 0, and `Config::load` and `App::new` call it, as they call `GameConfig::validate`; `App::new` applies the section to `Time`. No checked-in `tungsten.json` carries it. (5) `PrevPosition(pub Vec2)`: `RigidBodyBundle::put` gives `Position`, `PrevPosition` at the same point, `Velocity` when `Some`, `Collider`, `RigidBody`, so a static body has four components and a dynamic one five, with no field to leave it out; a game spawns a tuple for a body without history. `physics_prev_snapshot` copies `Position` into `PrevPosition` and `physics_sync` writes `Transform.position = prev + (cur − prev) × alpha` for an entity with `PrevPosition` while interpolation is on, `Position` otherwise and in a world without `Time`; both take the slice forms with `for_each` at both levels and never write `Position` (`D-033`). `PhysicsPlugin` registers `physics_prev_snapshot` (`PHYSICS_PREV_SNAPSHOT`) and `physics_step` after it in `fixed_update`, so the snapshot heads the stage ahead of a game's fixed systems, and `physics_sync` under `PHYSICS_SYNC` in `post_update`. `sync_position_to_transform` stays public and not deprecated for worlds driven by hand. A teleport writes `Position` and `PrevPosition` together; the physics overlay draws `Position` (`D-042`). (6) The app runs `fixed_update` once per step: before each, `Time::enter_fixed_step`, `DeltaTime.dt` the step and both views on; after each, `InputState::end_fixed_step` and `World::end_fixed_step_events`; after the last, `Time::leave_fixed_steps`, `DeltaTime.dt` the game dt and both views off. A world without `Time` runs the stage once; one without `InputState` skips its calls. `FrameTimings::fixed_steps` counts the frame's steps (1 without `Time`); `system_timings` lists each name once a frame in stage order, a later step's runs added to the first step's entries, and a frame with no step lists each `fixed_update` system at 0 ms. A frame stays the render frame. (7) `02_bench` keeps its hand-wired `physics_step` in `update` with no `PrevPosition` and no `workload_version` bump. (8) Example 01 draws each body with history at its interpolated point through one extract helper (`drawn_position`, `lerp_drawn`), puts its anchored emitters at the parent's `Transform` in a `post_update` system after the sync (`anchor_emitters`), sets `pending_effect` only on an accepted jump and clears it only in a frame's first step, takes a grounded jump on the buffered press as well as the held key, and counts the balls a frame's earlier steps queued against the cap.

**Why:** `D-129` fixed the shape on the W3 spike (`perf-runs/20261005-w3-stage-map/`, machine-local) and left these choices to W3b. The plan's two critique rounds added the event view (a second step's contact readers reread the first step's collisions), example 01's drawing step (its extract reads `Position`, so `physics_sync`'s `Transform` never reached its bodies), the overflow guard (an unguarded `f32` sum overflows and `%` of infinity is NaN) and the buffered grounded jump. An `f32` replay of the spike's loop showed 1/60 s runs one step a frame with `alpha` 0 over 600 pinned frames and a 0.1 s stall drops four steps (66.7 ms), which the smoke pin, the captures and w03's numbers assume; so every smoke frame and capture runs one step with `alpha` 0 and every new view reads as before. The bench builds without `PhysicsPlugin` and spawns its bodies by single inserts, as the physics release tests do, so no benchmarked body carries history: `physics_step_is_bit_identical_across_runs` (`0x088ec07a73c1b168`) and `pinned_step_state_is_unchanged` (`0xaee272e01ffc4e4c`) pass unchanged. At 12,000 bodies `physics_sync` reads 4.66 µs against the copy's 3.35 µs (criterion, native flags; 4.64–4.67 against 3.16–3.20 aligned), since it reads a second column and does arithmetic the copy skips; its row loop is 41 AVX instructions for eight rows with no stack load or store, the lerp on the tuple row iterator read 8.13 µs, and the owner accepted the cost (M41 Q13): about 0.11 ns a body, and no capture runs the sync. Keying example 01's `pending_effect` reset on `Time::frame()` keeps the once-a-frame reset that its harness tests without `player_presentation_system` rely on.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-040` as its marker line says. `D-129`'s rules stand as it wrote them; `D-033`, `D-042`, `D-066`, `D-094` and `D-128` stand.
- Moves: the bundle tests' component counts (static 3 → 4, dynamic 4 → 5); the five schedule snapshots gain `physics_prev_snapshot` at the head of `fixed_update`; example 01's bodies and attached visuals draw one step behind at the pin, and its snapshot gains `anchor_emitters`. The break ledger's two rows: `FrameTimings` gains a field, so a struct literal needs `..Default::default()`; `RigidBodyBundle` puts a fifth component, a behaviour change.
- Pinned by `time::tests` (32), the input (6) and event-queue (5) and `World` view tests, the `config` tests for the section, `physics::plugin::tests` (5), `ecs::bundle::tests`, the umbrella's eleven `testing::fixed_step` tests (the step dt, one press per step at 1/144 s and 1/30 s, a two-step frame's collisions, the interpolated body, the teleport, step counts and zero-step rows, the pause, a world without `Time`, the section), example 01's four fixed-step tests, the five snapshots, the `api/` snapshots, the `physics_sync_12k` criterion group and `just physics-release`.
- Accepted cost: `physics_sync` +1.3 µs a frame at 12,000 bodies over the copy (Q13).
- Not decided here: per-step cursor and scroll deltas, replays, a per-system step rate and workers inside a step (`D-129`, `D-131`); the deep-pile lever (`contact_hertz` 60 on the constant step, a 1.x backlog row); a backlog for events sent outside the steps (M41 Q12's follow-up); the kit's spawn and teleport helpers (W13); `02_bench`'s rows in `fixed_update`.

## D-138 — W15c landed (M42): additive extracts through the `Extracts` resource, the default sprite channel with tilemaps at the far plane, `extract_tilemap_layers` and lit tiles; `App::set_extract_*` kept as the explicit replace
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Decision:** Additive extracts as W15's step 3 set them, with the choices it left to W15c. (1) `tungsten::extract::Extracts`, re-exported at the crate root, is a `World` resource that `App::with_plugins` inserts beside `ExtractScratch`, before it builds any plugin. It holds three channels, sprites, quads and text; each draws its base, the channel's default or a replacement, then its contributions in the order they were added. `add_sprites`, `add_quads` and `add_text` append a contribution; `replace_sprites`, `replace_quads` and `replace_text` replace the base and keep the contributions, the last call winning. Each takes `impl Fn(&World) -> Vec<_> + 'static`, boxed as the `Extract*Fn` types, so the resource is neither `Send` nor `Sync`, as the slots were. A plugin contributes in `build` through `world.resource_mut::<Extracts>()`, so engine plugins contribute before a game's, in build order. Contributions carry no names and no `before`/`after` (Q6). (2) `App::set_extract_quads`, `set_extract_sprites` and `set_extract_text` keep their signatures and forward to `replace_*` (Q1); they panic only when the resource has been removed. `App`'s three slots and `install_default_extracts` are gone, and `Harness::new` installs nothing. (3) The sprite default is `extract_tilemaps`, then `extract_sprites_default`, the `gpu` row's order: the tiles under every sprite (Q2). It sets its tiles' `z_norm` to 1.0, the far plane, so under `gpu_depth` (`LessEqual` against a 1.0 clear) every default sprite, at `z_norm` in [0, 1), still draws over them; with no tile in view it returns the sprite extract's vector unchanged. Quads and text have no default. (4) `stage_extract` reads the resource once a frame and calls the quad, sprite and text channels in that order with a shared `&World` (`D-018`, `D-128`); a channel with no contribution returns its base's vector without a copy, and without the resource, which only its removal causes, the channels draw nothing (Q7). The HUD, the systems overlay and inspector text, debug draw, lights and mesh particles stay engine-owned, after the channels (Q5). (5) A contribution keeps the `z_norm` its closure wrote; the channel never renormalizes across sources. Under the CPU sort order is what draws; under `gpu_depth` an instance at `z_norm` 0 passes the depth test against everything drawn before it, so an appended contribution draws over the default, and a nonzero `z_norm` is tested against the default's (Q12). (6) `extract_tilemap_layers(&World, &[&str])`, beside `extract_tilemaps` at the crate root, draws the named render layers of every tilemap, map by map in query order and each map's layers in file order; a name no map has draws nothing and a collision layer never draws. Both share one body with the filter checked once per layer, both write `z_norm` 0 and both cull at `WindowSize`. (7) A tile whose sprite has a lit atlas draws lit, as a sprite does (`D-061`, Q4): a layer's batches key on atlas page and lighting in first-use order, so one page can give a lit and an unlit batch, and an unlit tile keeps its batches and bytes. (8) Example 01 keeps its explicit replace (Q3), since its parallax layers and the engine's particles are `Sprite` entities it draws itself, and draws its three tile stages through `extract_tilemap_layers`; its own tile extract is gone. Examples 03 and 04 keep `set_extract_text` (Q8), the template's `GamePlugin` adds its text through `Extracts`, and `02_bench`'s rows keep their explicit extracts.

**Why:** W15's step 3 asked for a default that draws sprites and tilemaps, with contributions a game and plugins append and a replace only on purpose. W15a's plugins build with `&mut Schedule, &mut World` (`D-128`), so a contribution has to sit in a `World` resource, and core cannot hold it, since `SpriteBatch`, `QuadInstance` and `TextSection` are render types (`D-007`, `D-016`, `D-018`). The audit found example 01 interleaving its tile layers by name with its own drawing and drawing its parallax and particle `Sprite` entities itself, so no default followed by contributions reproduces its frame; it keeps a replace, and the engine's tilemap extract gained the layer form, and lit tiles, which example 01's normal-mapped tile sprites need, so the example could drop its own. Tiles at `z_norm` 0 would hide the default's sprites under `gpu_depth` (W8a's card), which the plan's first critique round found, so the default writes its tiles at the far plane. An equivalence test on the real `level.tmj` (184 tileset sprites on two synthetic pages, every third lit, at the spawn, mid-level and the whole map) read the same sorted draw list per layer from the example's extract and the engine's, with no two engine instances on one cell; within a layer the engine groups tiles by page and lighting where the example kept contiguous runs (`terrain` 4 batches against up to 1,282 runs), which changes no pixel, since tiles on distinct cells never overlap. No benchmark row draws through the default channel, so the rows' extract work is unchanged and only the stage's dispatch moved; the release sitting captures every CPU row and a scratch probe of `gpu-throughput` drawn through the default channel with two contributions, its verdicts in the release report.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-042` as its marker line says. `D-018`, `D-061`, `D-086`, `D-113`, `D-114` and `D-128` stand.
- The break ledger's two rows are behaviour changes, not compile breaks: an app with no sprite extract of its own now draws its tilemaps under its sprites, and a tile with a lit atlas draws lit. Nothing public is removed.
- Pinned by `extract::tests` (the default's order and its no-map bytes, the far-plane tiles, contributions from two plugins and a game in order and byte for byte with a nonzero `z_norm` kept, a replace keeping contributions, the last replace winning), the three new `tilemap_extract::tests` (the layer form's order, lit batches apart on one page, every render layer named equal to `extract_tilemaps`), example 01's presentation test (layer order `[1, 2, 6, 3, 7, 5, 4, 8]`, culling through `extract_tilemap_layers`), the template's text test and the `api/` snapshots.
- Not decided here: tile layers ordered among sprites by z in the default (a 1.x backlog row, which W16b's tilemap builder may take); tilemaps at `z_norm` 0 under `gpu_depth` in custom extracts that call `extract_tilemaps` or the layer form (W8a's card); the `tile_batch_lens` capacity hint that several tilemap calls a frame share (known issues); the engine-owned pieces as contributions (W13's plan, if the kit needs them).

## D-139 — Fixed-step health pass (0.57): a frame with no fixed step holds its event queues for the next step, bounded; example 01's HUD counts each step's contacts after the contact readers; the step defaults stay 60 Hz and two steps a frame
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Decision:** (1) `EventQueue<T>` gains held events. `World::hold_events_for_fixed_step()`, new and public, marks every registered queue: that frame's flush moves the previous window into the held events instead of dropping it, and in the first fixed step of a later frame `iter`, `len` and `is_empty` read the held events, then `previous`, then `current`. `iter_current`, a later step's view and the frame view outside the steps (every `update` and `post_update` reader, `Harness::events`) are unchanged, and a flush in a frame that did not hold drops what was held. A queue holds at most 256 frames with events, dropping the oldest first. (2) The app holds in a frame that runs no fixed step while the game clock advances (`Time::game_delta() > 0`) and `fixed_update` has a system; a paused frame, a scale of 0, a world without `Time` and an app with no `fixed_update` system (`02_bench`) hold nothing. (3) Example 01's `update_text_display` moves from before `physics_step` to the end of `fixed_update`, after the contact readers, and counts `iter_current()`: Contacts is the step's own collision events, one per penetrating contact per substep. (4) The windowed defaults stay `fixed_step_hz` 60 and `max_steps_per_frame` 2 (`D-129`, `D-137`), as smoke runs and benchmarks stay at 1/60 s; nothing in `TimeConfig` changes.

**Why:** The health pass's regime test runs example 01 headless at frame dt 1/30, 1/60, 1/144 and 1/1000 s on the default 60 Hz step. A `fixed_update` reader of `iter` placed before `physics_step` got 0 of 512 collision events at 1/144 and 1/1000 s: most frames there run no step, and their flush rotated a step's events out before the next step's earlier readers ran (M41 Q12's follow-up). The HUD's Contacts read 0 at every refresh at those rates, and at 1/30 s, two steps a frame, 24 (two steps' events) or 0 (a refresh in the second step). With the hold, the reader before the step gets each event once, in order, at all four rates, and the HUD after the contact readers reads one step's events at all four. The alternative was a reader cursor (an `EventCursor<T>` per reader): exactly once per reader wherever it runs, but a new public type, a cursor in every reader and the same retention past the flush, out of proportion for an exit ramp; the owner took the hold (implicit approval, 2026-10-07). Nothing holds while the clock stands still, so a pause starts the steps' readers over, as M41 Q12 proposed, and the cap bounds slow motion, where steps can be many frames apart. At the 1/60 s pin every frame runs a step, so no queue holds: smoke runs, captures, the row digests and the physics hashes (`0x088ec07a73c1b168`, `0xaee272e01ffc4e4c`) are unchanged. The step rate was measured on the harness in a release build with example 01 and a 3,000-ball pile (three interleaved repeats, `perf-runs/20261007-fixed-step-health/`, machine-local): a step costs the same 0.32 ms of `physics_step` at 60, 120 and 240 Hz, so the `fixed_update` systems cost 26.3, 52.3 and 104.0 ms per game second on the settling pile and 68.9, 106.5 and 207.2 while it falls, and at 12,000 balls one 60 Hz step already costs 10.3 ms, 615 ms per game second, which 120 Hz would double past one core. On the slabs before 0.57 the player stopped at x = 1580.25 at 60 Hz and 1644.25 at 120 Hz and crossed every join at 240 Hz; 0.57 removes the joins in the example and leaves the solver's ghost collision to known issues, so a faster step would buy nothing there. A bound of 1 would turn every frame slower than the step into slow motion, halving the game's speed at 30 fps; the doubled cost of a two-step frame when the step itself is the bottleneck (a 12,000-ball pile's frame 13 → 40 ms in the triage) needs a bound that knows the step's cost, a follow-up. Finding 4, the unsmoothness at uncapped frame rates: the drawn bodies are exact; what example 01 animates from its step clock, `SceneTime` (cloud drift, glow and light flicker, the black hole's rings, burning emitters), moves only in frames that step, 120 of 2,000 at 1/1000 s, and in the window, immediate presentation, GPU-bound at about 150 fps on the reference machine, on 672 of 1,594 frames, while step frames' extra 0.26 ms of update hid in the GPU wait (total p50 6.63 against 6.59 ms); headless, step frames cost 0.52 ms against 0.07 with 3,000 settled balls, so presentation grows uneven only when a step is a sizeable share of a frame. The compositor's own timing cannot be told headlessly.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-040` and `D-137` as their marker lines say. `D-128`'s registration and `D-129`'s step rules stand.
- An event sent outside the steps reaches a `fixed_update` reader of `iter` in the first step after it, at any frame rate. One sent in a frame's `pre_update` still reaches that frame's first step through `iter_current` and `iter`, and `iter` again in the next frame that steps (`D-040`'s two windows); a step's own events reach a later reader of the step through `iter_current` once, as before.
- The break ledger's row: a behaviour change, not a compile break, and one public addition, `World::hold_events_for_fixed_step`.
- Pinned by `ecs::event_queue::tests` (the hold across frames with no step, the frame view and later steps without the held events, the drop without a hold, the 256-frame cap), `ecs::world::tests::the_fixed_step_hold_reaches_every_registered_queue`, the umbrella's `testing::fixed_step::at_144_hz_a_fixed_reader_reads_each_update_event_once_and_a_pause_drops_them`, example 01's regime test and `RUNTIME_SCHEDULE`, and the `api/` snapshot.
- Not decided here: a reader cursor; per-step cursor and scroll deltas (`D-137`); a catch-up bound that knows the step's cost; example 01's `SceneTime` animation at the interpolated time (known issues).

## D-140 — Bounded maintenance plans in the session; handoffs and milestone reviews retain their routes
**Amended by D-150:** the milestone clause only; a 1.0 milestone candidate, or work whose deliverable is a new public API, keeps its saved plan and critique with no approval or API review stop; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-11
**Decision:** Bounded maintenance records scope/non-goals, affected files and acceptance checks in the session before editing, without requiring a saved milestone plan or a fresh approval for routine choices already authorized. Multi-session work retains a saved handoff plan. A 1.0 milestone candidate, or work whose deliverable is a new public API, retains the program's plan, critique, approval and applicable level C API review. `docs/validation.md` owns the detailed change-to-check matrix; existing QA tooling may recommend checks from paths, without executing them or certifying results.

**Why:** The owner approved workflow research proposals 1–3 on 2026-10-11. The current decision index measured 43,894 bytes and DESIGN Status 13,689 bytes before the change; those are context-size measurements, not task-time or token savings. Ordinary maintenance needs a concrete scope and acceptance check, while a saved plan serves a handoff and milestone review settles lasting API choices. The detailed checks were distributed across operational guides and left generator and release-only physics routing easy to miss.

**Consequences:**
- Amends no decision. `D-105`'s uncommitted work, `D-070`'s informational CPU CI, `D-079`'s human publication and `D-095`'s capture provenance rules stand. No release is implied by maintenance, and no runtime dependency is added (`D-015` does not apply).
- `AGENTS.md` distinguishes full named-crate correctness audits from scoped workflow/documentation audits and points to the plan routes and canonical validation matrix. Milestone and release checks remain additional requirements.
- Decision navigation keeps each ID and amendment/supersession relationship; detailed measurements and edge rules stay in the immutable decision sections. Current snapshots link to the changelog, register and gate records rather than repeating release history.
- The QA selector prints recommendations and reasons, combines overlapping checks, includes untracked and renamed/deleted paths, and exposes unknown paths and content-dependent choices. CPU success without the visual switch cannot certify pixel comparisons.
- Real-task time, usage, repeated exploration and owner corrections still need a pilot; this implementation measures documentation bytes and verifies representative navigation only. Proposals 4–9 remain unapproved.

## D-141 — Perf captures refuse known background load before building
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** `scripts/bench.py` reuses its existing `/proc` scanner and own-process-tree rule before provenance collection or building for `run`, `suite` and `run --sweep`. A known `nxcodec.bin`, or foreign `cargo`/`rustc`, refuses the command with an environment error (exit 2) before any capture directory is created. The existing `--allow-background` flag bypasses this refusal for diagnostics; measured-run scans still record the load they observe and apply the existing validity/notes policy.

**Why:** `D-095` records repeated spoiled captures despite passing workload guards and digests. Its scanner previously ran only after the build, and the capture rules therefore required a separate manual process check before every sitting. Reusing that scanner before the build replaces those duplicate encoder/build prechecks and avoids starting a sitting already known to be unsuitable. No task-time saving is claimed.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-095` as its marker line says. Measured-run scans, tree-drift invalidation, repeats, digests, comparability and thresholds remain unchanged; load starting after preflight still invalidates the capture unless explicitly allowed.
- The scanner is not a machine-idle guarantee. Other load, concurrent sessions and the agent's own commands retain the quiet-sitting rules. Capacity probes, manual profiler/criterion runs, smoke timings and visual runs retain manual process checks because they are not scanned.
- No dependency, flag, lock, state file or new scanning policy is added. Preflight costs one existing process scan per command; `D-095` measured a scan at 4.2 ms for 344 processes on the reference machine, not a new measurement here.
- Existing `BackgroundLoad` tests inject process lists and build/capture mocks to cover refusal before build/output for run, sweep and suite, own-tree/clean acceptance and the explicit override. The existing measured-load and tree-drift tests remain.

## D-142 — Render results: a frame is presented, skipped or failed; a capture belongs to one frame and its result comes back; `App::run` returns the render failure, else the capture error
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** `Renderer::render_frame`, `render_frame_with_quads`, `render_frame_full` and `render_frame_full_timed` return `Result<RenderOutcome, RenderError>`. `RenderOutcome` (`#[must_use]`, `#[non_exhaustive]`) is `Presented { capture }`, whose `capture` is the armed capture's result (the path written, or the readback, encode or IO `ScreenshotError`), or `Skipped`: the acquire gave no surface texture (a timeout, an occluded window, or a surface still outdated after one reconfigure) and nothing was presented. `Err(RenderError)` is the failed frame the acquire returns: a surface lost again right after its recreation, which device loss produces, or a failed recreation. This records the acquire policy that came with 0.27.0 without a decision: `surface_acquire.rs` draws on success and on a suboptimal surface (reconfiguring after it, once per unchanged size), skips on a timeout, an occluded window or a second outdated status, reconfigures and retries on the first outdated, recreates and retries on the first lost, and fails on a second lost or a validation status. `capture_frame` and `capture_frame_direct` return `()` and arm a capture for the next `render_frame*` call only, which takes it before the acquire can return early (`screenshot::capture_for_frame`); a skipped or failed frame drops it unwritten. The renderer logs nothing about captures. In the umbrella (`tungsten/capture.rs`), `TUNGSTEN_CAPTURE_FRAME=N` names the N-th frame that reaches render, presented or skipped, and arms that frame only. A render failure is the run's fatal error, with its frame number, logged once; the frame ends `FrameEnd::RenderFailed` and the window loop exits. A capture error (frame N's readback, encode or write failed, frame N was skipped, or the run ended before frame N) is logged once and the run goes on. `App::run` returns the fatal error, else the capture error.

**Why:** A skipped acquire returned `Ok(())`: the app counted the frame, logged "captured frame N" and left the capture armed for the next presented frame, whichever world frame that was. Readback, encode and IO failures only warned, so a run exited 0 with no file or with another frame's, and a render error was logged every frame while the loop ran on. W1 M2 compares the direct and capture present paths (`D-087`) and needs a capture that is its frame's or fails the run; smoke and the visual tests judge exit codes (`D-022`: runtime conditions return `Result`). Failing a skipped target frame instead of sliding to the next presented one keeps N a world frame; counting skipped frames keeps today's index when nothing skips. M43 (W8a) chose both (its Q1, Q2).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-047` as its marker line says. The offscreen target, the padded readback and the `TUNGSTEN_CAPTURE_*` variables stand.
- wgpu's fatal paths stay outside the result: with no error scope, a validation error at the acquire panics in wgpu's default handler, and device loss inside a blocking poll (the capture and GPU-timing readbacks) panics; the crash hook (`D-119`) records both (known issues). There is no device recovery.
- When W2 R2 submits work before the acquire, a skip still returns `Skipped` and drops the capture, whose readback stays after the acquire.
- A skipped frame runs every other stage and writes its perf lines, as before; telemetry does not count skips.
- Tests: `tungsten-render`'s `screenshot` tests (a capture that meets a skipped or failed acquire is gone, and the next presented frame gets none), `tungsten`'s `capture` tests (each transition) and `app` tests (a failed frame ends `RenderFailed` with the error recorded; the run returns the capture error), and the visual tests `capture_past_the_last_frame_fails_the_run` and `unwritable_capture_path_fails_the_run`. The break ledger's render-result row ([w04](docs/plans/1.0/w04-api-freeze.md#break-ledger)) records the break.

## D-143 — Burst `once` and pulse `total_pulses` semantics: `once: false` re-arms through `rearm_burst`, the latch is `burst_fired`, and zero total pulses emit nothing
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** `EmissionKind::Burst { count, once }` fires `count` on the emitter's first tick. With `once: true` the emitter then drains, and `ParticleSystemDrained` comes once its particles are gone, as before. With `once: false` the burst is re-armable: it fires again on the first tick after each `ParticleEmitterState::rearm_burst()` and never drains; `rearm_burst` does nothing on a drained emitter. The fired latch is a new public field, `ParticleEmitterState::burst_fired`, and `continuous_accum` is the continuous accumulator only. `Pulse { total_pulses: Some(0) }` emits nothing and drains on its first tick, and `ParticleConfig::validate` rejects it, as it rejects a burst `count` or a `count_per_pulse` of 0.

**Why:** The latch was `continuous_accum >= 1.0`, set on firing and never reset, so a burst fired once whatever `once` said: `once: false` only suppressed `ParticleSystemDrained`, and the only way to fire it again was to write the accumulator, as the bench's `particles` row did (the benchmarks' burst-latch finding and the 0.40 follow-up). `Pulse { total_pulses: Some(0) }` fired one pulse before it drained. M43 (W8a) chose an explicit re-arm over firing again once the burst's particles are gone, which would move the `particles` workload, and over keeping the accumulator as the latch (its Q6, Q7).

**Consequences:**
- A `ParticleEmitterState` struct literal without `..` no longer compiles (the tree has none); the break ledger has rows for the field and for `once: false`.
- The bench's `rearm_bursts` calls `rearm_burst()` on the schedule on which it cleared the latch, so the `particles` row's emission, workload version and digest stay. The 13 shipped burst manifests say `"once": true` and emit as before.
- `ParticleConfigRegistry::register` does not validate, so a code-built config with zero pulses reaches the emitter, which emits nothing and drains.
- Tests: `burst_not_once_fires_again_after_rearm`, `burst_once_ignores_rearm` and `pulse_with_zero_total_emits_nothing_and_drains` in `crates/tungsten/tests/particles.rs`, and `config_rejects_zero_total_pulses` in core's particle tests.

## D-144 — Input groundwork: 28 more `KeyCode` variants, an ordered raw input stream in `InputState`, focus loss releasing held input, modifiers, pointer leave and the `ScaleFactor` resource (W1 M0b)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** `KeyCode` gains Home, End, Delete, PageUp, PageDown, both sides of Shift, Control, Alt and Super, KeyE, KeyG, KeyO, KeyQ, KeyT, KeyX, KeyZ, Digit4, Digit9, F5–F8, F10 and F12; their `input.json` names are winit's variant names, under `D-045`'s 1:1 rule. Every other key stays `Other(u32)`, whose number is stable within one build only and never serializes. `InputState` records the frame's input in order, `raw_events()`: each mutator applies its state and records a `RawEvent { seq, input }`, `seq` counting up for the life of the state, and `begin_frame` clears the stream. `RawInput` (`#[non_exhaustive]`) is `Key { code, pressed, repeat, modifiers }`, `MouseButton`, `PointerMoved`, `PointerLeft`, `Wheel` (lines or pixels), `Modifiers` and `Focus`. A press of a held key records a repeat and changes nothing; a release of a key not held records nothing; only adjacent pointer moves coalesce, into the earlier record and its `seq`. The stream is recorded, not routed. `set_focused(false)` releases every held key and button with release edges in the frame and fixed views, clears the modifiers and the suppression marks, and records `Focus(false)` alone. A key held when focus returns stays released until it is pressed again: the umbrella's bridge drops winit's synthetic releases, marks the key of each synthetic press with `suppress_until_release`, whose presses (X11's auto-repeat among them) are then ignored, unrecorded, until its release, and drops a press flagged `repeat` of a key not held. `Modifiers` is a set of Shift, Control, Alt and Super that each key event carries. `pointer_left` keeps `cursor_position`; `pointer_in_window()` reads false until the next move, which sets the position without a delta. `ScaleFactor` is the core resource `D-127` assigned: `os()` is the OS scale, which the umbrella sets at `resumed` and on `ScaleFactorChanged`, ignoring a non-finite or non-positive value; `get()` is the factor layout will use, `os` times the `ui_scale` setting W11b adds, so `get() == os()` until then; `App::with_plugins` inserts it at 1.0. Core stays free of winit (`D-007`).

**Why:** Focus loss left keys stuck where winit sends no release: it synthesizes releases before `Focused(false)` on X11 and Windows, while Wayland sends `ModifiersChanged(empty)` and `Focused(false)` alone, and macOS no release either. Dropping winit's synthetic events was not enough: X11 delivers a key still held when focus returns as non-synthetic auto-repeat presses, the first not flagged `repeat`, which would press the key again (M44's critique, round 1). The suppression mark makes every platform behave as Wayland does. UI navigation and text editing need the navigation and modifier keys, and W1 M3's routing needs the order: a press and a release between redraws stay one click, and a move before a press still positions it (w01 §7). Coalescing only adjacent moves bounds the stream without reordering it. M44 (W1 M0b) chose the keys, the focus rules, pointer leave and the stream's home (its Q2–Q5).

**Consequences:**
- Amends no decision; `D-045`'s names extend 1:1 and its rules stand. `KeyCode` stays exhaustive until W4b's sweep, so a match on it without a wildcard breaks; the break ledger's `KeyCode` row records it.
- Not here: routing the stream, `ui_*` actions, `UiEvent` and the routed gameplay view (W1 M3); logical keys, produced text and IME (`D-102`); the gamepad (W5a); applying the scale factor (W1 M2); persisting `ui_scale` (W11b); winit 0.31, still a prerelease.
- Resize and scale changes stay resources (`WindowSize`, `ScaleFactor`), read by routing after the display changes apply (w01 §7); the stream has no event for them.
- The reference session is X11, where winit releases keys itself; focus-loss release on Wayland, macOS and Windows has no host to check it (known issues).
- Tests: core's `input` tests (focus loss, order and `seq`, coalescing, repeats, suppression, pointer leave, the new names) and `display` tests (`ScaleFactor`), and the umbrella's `input_bridge` tests (the table, synthetic and repeat presses, modifiers, the scale factor).

## D-145 — Late acquire: every pass before the swapchain stage records and submits before the surface acquire; an acquire that resizes the targets records them again; `render_encode` stays the whole recording (W2 R2)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** `Renderer::render_frame*` records a frame in two command buffers around `Surface::get_current_texture`. It builds the pass order first, taking the present path from the armed capture without taking the capture, uploads the camera, prepares the text and records every pass before the split into the first buffer, which it submits before the acquire. The split is the first pass of the direct order whose color or resolve target is the swapchain, `D-087`'s stage (`passes::order::acquire_split`); a blit or direct-capture frame splits at the same index, since its order lists the same stages. A frame with neither a post stack nor SMAA splits at the scene pass and has no first buffer: only the uploads and the text preparation move. Then it acquires. A skip or a failure returns as `D-142` says, after the text pipeline's `post_frame`, so each prepare is trimmed once; the capture is dropped and the first buffer's GPU work is wasted. An acquire that resized the targets, the target pool's generation or the surface size having changed (`surface_acquire::record_again_after_acquire`), records the first part again at the new size, with the timing resources rebuilt from the resized pool, and logs a `debug` line. The passes from the split on, a direct capture's blit, the capture readback and the timestamp resolve go into the second buffer, submitted with the present. The split falls on pass descriptors: a bloom slot that writes the swapchain records its threshold, downsample and upsample passes after the acquire with its composite. The early submit sits behind one private `const`, `SUBMIT_BEFORE_ACQUIRE`; with it off, the first buffer is submitted with the second. `render_encode` stays the frame's whole recording, both buffers and any re-recording; `CpuFrameTimings::encode_pre_acquire_ms`, `FrameTimings::render_encode_pre_acquire_ms` and the `frame:` key `render_encode_pre_acquire` hold its part recorded before the acquire, and the early submit's time joins `render_submit_present`. `gpu_passes:` keeps every pass; `render_span` now spans the gap across the acquire.

**Why:** The CPU recorded nothing until the acquire returned, and a GPU-bound frame waits there: `gpu` spent 7.5 of its 10.7 ms in `render_acquire` (profiling workflow). Recording before the acquire takes that work off the frame's path after the wait, and submitting it lets the GPU start on the scene and post passes while the CPU waits for the swapchain; W1 M2's overlay later draws in the second buffer. Re-recording after a resize keeps `D-142`'s outcomes: the frame presents at the new size, where a new skip reason would drop an armed capture, and rebuilding the timing resources stops a larger size's extra bloom mips overrunning a query set sized before the acquire, which a timed frame could already do. Keeping `render_encode` whole keeps the meaning of the metric `gpu` and `gpu-throughput` own. M44 (W2 R2) chose the early submit (its Q6), the re-recording (Q7), the timing fields (Q8) and the bloom carve-out (Q13); its step 5 measured the split against the tree before it and against a build with the early submit off (`perf-runs/20261012-m44-late-acquire/`, machine-local).

**Consequences:**
- Amends no decision. `D-087`'s tail stands: capture frames keep the blit, the screenshot reads the same texture and both paths draw byte-equal frames. `D-142`'s acquire actions and outcomes stand; a skip after the early submit still returns `Skipped` and drops the capture, as its consequences foresaw.
- `PassRecorder::begin` keeps its signature. The crate-private `begin_timed` and the bloom slot take the swapchain view as an `Option` and panic on a swapchain target without one, which the split rules out.
- A stack that ends in bloom keeps its whole slot after the acquire; moving bloom's offscreen passes before it is a follow-up. No benchmark row ends in bloom.
- The camera matrix comes from the surface size before render, so a resize inside the acquire draws one frame with the old matrix, as before.
- `CpuFrameTimings` and `FrameTimings` gain a public field each, so a struct literal without `..` breaks; the break ledger records it. The runner keeps the new key as a stage of its own; `unattributed`, the stacked bars and capacity's limiting stage read their keys by name and do not count it.
- **Accepted regression, `gpu` `stage.extract`.** p50 0.61 → 0.83 ms, +0.22 with a 95% interval of +0.21 to +0.23 against a threshold of 0.05, and p95 +0.15 to +0.20 ms, in both pairs against the tree before and in the pair built with LLVM's alignment flags (M44 step 5), so not code placement; the build with the early submit off reads +0.19 ms, so recording before the acquire moves it, not the submit. The cost moves between CPU stages: in the same compares the row's `render_encode` p50 reads 2.04 → 1.63 ms `improved`, `render` 10.11 → 9.77 ms, and `total` p50 10.76 → 10.65 ms, `unchanged` with its interval below zero, while GPU engine time per frame reads `unchanged` (10.305 → 10.347 ms). The row waits about 7.7 ms in the acquire, and after the split the next frame's extract is the first heavy work after that wait; the mechanism was not established (per frame, `extract` correlates with the wait before it at about ±0.1). No other row's `extract` moves. The owner accepted the regression on 2026-10-12; this bullet is the justification the regression policy in `docs/perf/profiling-workflow.md` asks for.
- **Recorded readings.** The second submit costs `integrated`, which records 0.83 of its 0.87 ms before the acquire, 0.37 → 0.42 ms of `render_submit_present` p50, and its `render` stage, reported only, reads 1.27 → 1.33 ms `regressed`; its owned `total` reads `unchanged` in both pairs. The early submit measured neutral: the build with it off reads `gpu` `total` p50 10.59 ms against the candidate's 10.65 and `integrated` 7.99 against 7.99, with GPU engine time per frame within 0.02 ms of the candidate's; it stays on, so the GPU can start on the first buffer while the CPU waits.
- Per-worker encoders and render bundles, R2's later half, are a 1.x backlog row.
- Tests: the split across the pass-order matrix on both paths (no pass before it writes the swapchain, the direct pass at it does, the same label on both paths, the blit path's only swapchain pass last), the re-recording transitions in `surface_acquire`'s tests, `just visual`'s eleven captures byte-equal to the tree before, and step 5's sitting.

## D-146 — Input edges on a stopped clock: a frame with no fixed step whose game clock stood still while real time ran drops the fixed view's edges, so a press made while paused or at scale 0 reaches no step (Phase 5 QA pass)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Decision:** In a frame that runs no fixed step, the app reads the frame's dts as `advance_frame` set them: when the game dt is zero while the real dt is not (`Time::game_delta() == 0.0` and `real_delta() > 0.0`: the clock paused, or its scale 0, as the frame started), it clears the fixed view's edges with `InputState::end_fixed_step`, beside `D-139`'s hold, which runs only while the clock advances. A press or release made while the clock stands still reaches no step, the one read by the frame whose `pre_update` resumes the clock included; a press after the resume reaches exactly one step, at any frame rate. A frame whose clock advances keeps its edges for the next step, as `D-137` says, and so does a frame with no time at all (real dt zero, the harness's `set_dt(0.0)`): no time passed, and input has no second window as events do. The predicate reads the frame's dts, not `is_paused()`, since a `pre_update` system that resumes the clock changes the setting mid-frame, for the next frame. Level queries (`pressed`), the frame view that `update` reads, the cursor, the deltas and `raw_events` are unchanged. No new public item.

**Why:** The Phase 5 QA pass's finding A1 (M45): the fixed view's edges cleared only after a step and `begin_frame` kept them, so a press or release made during a pause reached the first step after the resume, and a pause screen's resume key, often the jump key, jumped. `a_press_on_a_stopped_clock_reaches_no_step` failed before the change with `paused: the step after the restart read [KeyA, KeyB], pressed on the stopped clock`. Events already drop on a stopped clock (`D-139`); input now does too. The alternative kept the edges and documented that a game clears its own input on resume, a rule every pause screen (W1 M3, M4) would have to remember. A zero game dt alone does not mark a stopped clock: a running frame whose real dt is zero has one too (the plan's critique, round 1).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-137` as its marker line says. `D-139`'s hold and `D-129`'s step rules stand.
- The break ledger's row: a behaviour change, not a compile break.
- Pinned by the umbrella's `testing::fixed_step::a_press_on_a_stopped_clock_reaches_no_step`: paused and at scale 0, no step after the restart reads a press or release made on the stopped clock and a press after it reaches one step; on a running clock, a press read by a zero-step frame at 1/144 s and one read by a zero-dt frame each reach the next step.
- No tracked row stops its clock, so the row digests, the physics hashes and the captures are unchanged.
- Not decided here: per-step cursor and scroll deltas (`D-137`); UI input routing over the step views, W1 M3's.

## D-147 — UI overlay and paint list: core paints one ordered list in physical pixels, the overlay pass draws its SDF primitives and glyphon text batches in paint order under one clip model and one colour convention (W1 M2)
**Amended by D-161:** the overlay order only; the pass draws the world labels' batch first, then the paint list's items, then the sections, and a frame without labels records what it did; the paint list, the clip model and the colour convention stand.
**Amended by D-162:** the paint clause only; a toggle paints a mark in its text colour after its box when it is on, and a slider paints its track, fill and thumb in place of its box; the paint order, the clip model and the colour convention stand.
**Amended by D-163:** the paint walk and text commands only; a node's alpha, the product of the opacities on its path and its root's fade, multiplies the alpha of every colour it paints; translation and scale map its subtree's rects, radii, borders, nine-slice corners and clips; a subtree at alpha 0 or under a composed scale below 1/64 paints nothing; `PaintCommand::Text` carries `scale`, the composed scale rounded down to a 32nd, and the label's content box, shrunk to match about its centre; render's text nodes bake no style colour, so glyphs take the command's; the paint order, the clip model and the colour convention stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** The UI draws through one ordered paint list. `UiTree::paint` (core) walks the layers low to high and each root's nodes in order into a `PaintList` of `Rect`, `Image` and `Text` commands in physical pixels: a node's box when its fill or border is visible, a label's text run (a `TextNodeId`, never a glyphon type, `D-126`), an image's sprite as one command or nine when the sprite entry carries `nine_slice` insets (corners at source size, edges and centre stretched), then its children. The one rounding rule of `D-127` is `to_physical`: a box becomes the integer rectangle from `floor(min · scale)` to `ceil(max · scale)`, applied once at extraction; clips are such rectangles indexed by command, clip 0 the viewport, a node with `clip_children` pushing the intersection of its box and the enclosing clip and an empty intersection skipping its subtree. Primitives scissor to their clip and text trims to its box intersected with its clip (`TextBounds`), so the two edges cannot disagree. Text batches follow the partition rule in core (`PaintList::text_batches`), w01 §6's rule turned around so that text over its own panel stays in one batch: a batch opens at a text command and every later text command joins it, and a non-text command over any of the batch's text boxes closes it before itself (a pairwise check; a bounding box split a row of buttons at every button); a batch draws where it closes, after the non-text commands inside its range, which overlap none of its text. The render crate consumes `&PaintList` through `render_frame_full*`. `UiPipeline` draws 80-byte instances (shadow and gradient fields reserved and zero) with `ui.wgsl`: a rounded-rectangle signed distance for fill and border with one-pixel analytic anti-aliasing, straight-alpha blending, and colours authored as sRGB `[u8; 4]` decoded to linear in the shader, the convention of glyphon's `Accurate` mode; images sample the sprite atlas pages through the sprite pipeline's texture layout, untextured runs a 1×1 white page. The overlay pass keeps its label `tungsten_text_overlay_pass` and its `text` timing label: it draws the UI's items in paint order, a primitive run per page and clip and a text batch per partition, each item setting its scissor clamped to the attachment (an empty intersection skips it, a text item sets the viewport's), then the text sections under the whole attachment. An empty paint list uploads nothing and records no UI command, so a frame without UI is the frame of 0.63. Each text batch owns one glyphon `TextRenderer` on the shared atlas; `trim` runs only before a full frame, forced every K = 16 changed frames, on `AtlasFull` and on a font reload; between full frames only batches whose signature changed re-prepare; a batch whose prepare failed is not drawn; a batch absent for a frame loses its signature (`D-126`).

**Why:** The glyph gate (`D-126`) and the DPI model (`D-127`) fixed the text and coordinate halves; this entry fixes the shape they plug into. One ordered list is the only shape that paints a rear window's text before a front window's background (w01 §6); drawing all panels, then all text, cannot. The clip model is the simplest one that text can share: glyphon trims on the CPU through `TextBounds`, so integer physical rectangles from one function keep primitive and glyph edges equal, and a clip index per command keeps a later change local. Colours decode in the shader because glyphon's `Accurate` atlas already does, so a panel and the text on it blend in the same space; `ColorMode::Web` was judged from a capture in M46 and not switched. The overlay pass keeps its labels because the `gpu` benchmark row owns the `text` metric at p50 (M46 Q1); a rename would drop an owned metric. Per-item scissors answer M46's critique: glyphon's `render` leaves the pass's scissor as it finds it, so a text draw after a clipped primitive batch would inherit the clip, and the acquire can shrink the surface under a list extracted before it (`D-145`).

**Consequences:**
- Extends `D-087` (the overlay is the last pass and now holds the UI before the text), `D-125` (the tree paints), `D-126` (the batches as shaped) and `D-127` (the rounding rule's home). Amends none.
- Core tests pin the paint order, the clip stack, the rounding rule at scale 1.5 and 2, the partition rule and the nine-slice; render tests pin the instance layout, the item batching and the scissor clamp; the visual tests of example 05 compare the direct and capture paths with UI visible.
- The pass order, the benchmark rows' recorded commands and the 26 visual captures of examples 02–04 are unchanged by a frame without UI.
- Open limits: hit testing still obeys every ancestor's box rather than the clip stack (M3); underline and strikethrough wait for layout runs through the `TextNodeStore` seam; atlas bytes and growth frames are not readable from glyphon 0.12; shadow and gradient fields are reserved in the instance and ignored by the shader.

## D-148 — UI theme asset, nine-slice sprites and the UI debug actions: `ui_themes` manifest files over the built-in theme, reloaded live and re-applied to the tree that follows them; `nine_slice` insets on sprite entries; `UiDebug` flipped by `engine_toggle_ui_bounds` and `engine_toggle_ui` (W1 M2)
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** A theme is a manifest asset. The manifest's `ui_themes` section names a JSON file per ID (`ui_themes: { "<id>": { "path": "ui/themes/<id>.json" } }`, `ResolvedUiTheme { path }`), and the file holds optional fields over `Theme::default()`: `tokens` (colours as `#rrggbb` or `#rrggbbaa`, authored in sRGB and kept as `[u8; 4]`, spacing, radii, fonts, text) and, per widget kind (`panel`, `label`, `button`, `image`), a `normal` look and `focused`, `disabled`, `hovered` and `pressed` variants merged over the kind's built-in ones; an unknown kind, token or field is an error. The loader fills a core `UiThemeRegistry` (ID → `Theme`) in sorted ID order, warns for a `fonts` token naming no `font_families` entry, and keeps the file paths for the watcher; a game applies a theme with `UiTree::set_theme_asset(id, theme)`, which records the ID a reload re-applies to, and `set_theme` records none. A changed theme file reloads through `reload_ui_theme`: the registry entry is replaced and a tree whose `theme_id` is that ID takes the theme again (its epoch rises, every root relayouts); a file that fails to load keeps the previous theme with one error. A manifest reload loads new `ui_themes` entries and keeps removed ones stale, as sprites are kept. Nine-slice insets are an optional `nine_slice: [left, top, right, bottom]` in source pixels on the sprite entry, carried to `ResolvedSprite` and `SpriteAsset` through `AssetRegistry::set_sprite_nine_slice` after registration (`register_sprite` keeps its signature), so skins reuse sprite IDs and atlas pages; a UI image of such a sprite paints nine commands (`D-147`). `AssetRegistry::sprite_epoch()` rises on every change to a registered sprite and never on interning, so the retained paint list repaints after a repack. The UI's debug state is one `UiDebug` resource (`hide_game_layers`, `show_bounds`) the paint reads; the engine actions `engine_toggle_ui_bounds` (F5) and `engine_toggle_ui` (F6) flip it on press edges from `ui_debug_toggle` in `PreUpdate`, beside the HUD toggle, and the HUD gains a `ui` row with the paint list's size, the layout stage's cost and the text batch telemetry.

**Why:** w01 §14 asked for the theme as an asset reloaded through the existing watcher: the cheapest high-value loop the suite offers, since a running game restyles live and high-contrast themes become data. Partial files over the built-in theme keep a theme file short and let the kind styles follow the file's tokens, so a changed accent recolours every button without naming it. Insets on the sprite entry rather than the node let a skin swap by sprite ID and share atlas pages, as the review pass proposed. A setter after registration keeps `register_sprite`'s fourteen callers unchanged (M46 critique 1), and an epoch on the registry is the one signal a retained paint list needs for a UV moved by a repack (critique 3). The hide-UI action gives clean screenshots and scene-only visual checks, and the bounds overlay is the first self-inspection tool the paint list makes cheap.

**Consequences:**
- Extends `D-031` and `D-053` (one more reload path and manifest diff), `D-045` (two more engine actions in the default map and `input.json`) and `D-048` (nine-slice insets ride the atlas's handles and UVs). Amends none.
- Layer-1 manifest tests pin the section and the field; the loader's tests pin the registry, the re-apply rule, the bad-file rule, the manifest diff and the insets' epoch; example 05 is the live fixture.
- Open limits: a theme file names font families by ID with a warning, not an error; a theme entry whose path changes without a file change is not re-read until its next edit; atlas bytes stay unreadable (`D-147`).

## D-149 — UI input routing: one `ui_route` system in `PreUpdate` routes the ordered raw stream against the last committed layout into hover, press, capture, focus and `UiEvent`s, and feeds `RoutedInput` the input the UI did not take (W1 M3)
**Amended by D-162:** the direction clause only; `ui_left` and `ui_right` step a slider that holds focus keys act on instead of moving focus, on the same repeat, and at an end take the press and change nothing; activations, presses and moves on the new kinds add `ValueChanged`; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-163:** the placement, style-layer, hit-testing and frozen-root clauses only; `ui_route` runs before the state dispatcher (`ui_state_roots`, `ui_route`, `ui_animate`, `state_dispatcher`), so a state's `update` reads this frame's routing and settles the "states read input before the routing" limit; a widget under a disabled ancestor layers `disabled`; hit testing and a slider's pointer mapping use the painted translation and scale, directional focus the layout rects; a frozen root is hit and blocks, but its widgets take no press, activation, value change or focus move, no `ui_*` action acts in it and `ui_cancel` sends no `Cancel` for it, while a frozen modal root still takes every key press; the rest stand.
**Amended by D-164:** the keyboard and pointer clauses only, while a capture is pending; every key, mouse button, pad button and stick direction press is the UI's and none navigates, the first that is not an OS repeat ending the capture with `InputCaptured` and its release staying the UI's, and the wheel is taken; prompts resolve after each routing; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** The UI routes input once a frame. `tungsten_core::ui::ui_route_system`, which the umbrella's `UiPlugin` registers as `ui_route` in `PreUpdate` after the state dispatcher, reads `InputState::raw_events()` in arrival order against the layout committed at the end of the previous frame, pointer positions divided by `ScaleFactor::get()` into UI units (`D-127`) and its timers kept with `Time::real_delta()`. A root takes input once it is visible and laid out; the `Game` and `GamePopup` roots take none while `UiDebug::hide_game_layers` is set. Hit testing walks paint order reversed through the paint clip stack: a widget counts inside its own rect and every `clip_children` ancestor's rect within the viewport, edges included, and an ancestor that does not clip no longer bounds it; a visible, laid-out root flagged `set_modal` blocks every root below it, while the roots above it, Debug roots among them, still hit. A hit's target is its nearest enabled `interactive` ancestor-or-self. Pointer: each routing first hovers again from the last position, so a widget that moved under a still cursor follows; a move updates the hovered target (`PointerEntered`, `PointerLeft`); a primary press on a target captures it (`Pressed`) and focuses it when focusable, with focus hidden; while captured only the target hovers, shown pressed while the pointer is over it; the release sends `Released`, then `Activated` when over the target, then `DoubleClicked` for that target's second click activation within `double_click_time` (0.5 s) and `double_click_distance` (4 units); another button sends only `Pressed` and `Released` with its button; focus loss, the pointer leaving the window and the target's removal, hiding or disabling end a press with `PressCancelled`. A press or the wheel over a widget of a root that takes input, or anywhere while a modal root is shown, is the UI's. Keyboard: the UI has the keyboard (`UiRouter::wants_keyboard`) while a modal root is shown, and then every key press is its, or while a widget holds visible focus, and then the presses of keys bound to a `ui_*` action are; a press is judged by the state before it. The engine-owned actions in the default map (`D-045`): `ui_accept` (Enter, Space) activates the focused widget on its press; `ui_cancel` (Escape) sends `Cancel { scope }`, the focused widget's scope or else the modal root, and outside a modal clears focus, so the next Escape reaches gameplay; `ui_next` (Tab) moves in tab order, backwards with Shift held, and `ui_prev` is listed unbound, since bindings carry no modifiers; `ui_up`, `ui_down`, `ui_left` and `ui_right` (the arrows) move by `focus_direction` (the explicit neighbour; else the nearest laid-out focusable widget in the scope whose centre lies that way, scored by the distance that way plus twice the offset across it, the first in tab order on a tie, searching again from the scope's opposite edge only in a scope flagged `set_focus_wrap`) and repeat while held after `repeat_delay` (0.5 s) every `repeat_interval` (0.1 s), at most once a routing. OS key repeats drive nothing. A laid-out modal root with no focus in it or in a root above it takes focus on its first focusable widget, and hiding or collapsing a focus scope restores the focus recorded on entering it, as removing it does. `FocusGained` and `FocusLost` follow every focus change, the game's own at the next routing. Focus is visible while `UiRouter::last_device()` is `Keyboard`: it starts so, a key press sets it, a pointer press or the wheel sets `Pointer`, a move changes nothing. Styles layer the `focused` variant (visible focus only), then `hovered`, then `pressed`, or apply `disabled` alone; `widget_state` names the top layer. Ownership: a held key or button belongs to the side that got its press, so the UI's releases never reach the routed view and the routed view's always do, and focus loss releases both. `RoutedInput`, which derefs to `InputState`, is the routed view: an `InputState` that only `ui_route` feeds, after its `begin_frame`, through the same mutators, moves, pointer leave, modifiers and focus always included, so `ActionMap` reads it and its fixed view behaves as `InputState`'s (`D-137`, `D-146`); a reader that runs before the routing sees the previous routing's edges. `InputState` stays the raw input every engine toggle reads. Events go into `EventQueue<UiEvent>` when it is registered (`D-040`), and `EventQueue::<UiEvent>::activated` reads `iter_current`, so a reader sees one activation once. `UiRouter` holds the settings, the last device, `pointer_over_ui`, `wants_keyboard` and the system cursor: the nearest one set with `set_cursor` on the captured widget or its ancestors, else on the hit's, else `Default`, which the umbrella applies to the window. When `ui_route` is scheduled, the app's exit check reads `engine_exit` in `RoutedInput`, so an Escape the UI took does not exit, and, in the raw input, a key or button press made after `PreUpdate`, which the UI never saw; smoke runs drop the window's key, button, pointer and wheel events so a resting pointer cannot move a pixel fixture.

**Why:** Routing at a stage rather than at event time keeps a press and a release between redraws one click, since the stream is ordered, and puts the UI's events before the systems that read them (w01 §7); the frame-loop gate placed the stage after the dispatcher (`D-128`) and on the real clock (`D-129`), so a paused game still navigates its pause menu. Hit testing through the clip stack makes the pointer agree with what is painted (`D-147`), which closes the known issue of a widget drawn outside a parent that does not clip and could not be clicked. Letting visible focus decide the keyboard keeps a HUD from taking Space from gameplay after a click (w01 §14's focus visibility), and judging a press by the state before it keeps the first key after pointer use a game key. Ownership by press keeps a key from sticking on either side when the UI's state changes between a press and its release. A routed view, not a `wants_keyboard` flag alone, is what filters gameplay (w01 §7), and keeping it opt-in leaves every raw reader as it is until M4 and M6 move the template and the examples. Layered states keep the focus ring on a focused button the pointer is over, where one state would hide another. `activated()` on the current window keeps one click from firing twice through `D-040`'s two windows.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125` as its marker line says. Extends `D-040` (`UiEvent` and its reader), `D-045` (eight `ui_*` actions in the default map), `D-088` (smoke runs drop window input), `D-128` (`ui_route` and `UiPlugin` in `PreUpdate`) and `D-144` (the raw stream's first reader).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{route,hit,focus,style,tree}.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/input.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/input/action_map.rs` and the harness tests in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/ui_route.rs`; `ui_route_bench` measures an empty routing and a 100-widget menu's move and click.
- Measured at M47's sitting (2026-10-13, `perf-runs/20261013-m47-ui-routing/`, five-run suites of every row against 0.64, with an A/A of each build and an aligned pair): every owned metric of every row not `regressed` in both direct pairs, but `churn` `flush` p50 in one (2.426 → 2.510 ms, τ 0.073), which the aligned pair reads `unchanged` (Δ −0.020 ms) and `World::flush_reusing`'s move from address mod 64 = 16 to 32 explains, the placement `D-083` records; first-run digests equal in every row; the 37 `just visual` captures byte-equal to 0.64's. `ui_route_bench` (criterion, native flags): an empty routing 179 ns, a 100-button menu's move and click 3.7 µs.
- Open limits: a drag past a threshold, scroll containers, popups and click-outside dismissal wait for BC and M5; no gamepad device (W5a); states read input before the routing (M4 decides); the inspector's pick reads raw input (M5); game cursors, cursor visibility and grab are a 1.x backlog row.

## D-150 — No owner-approval stops on the road to 1.0: plans end with the run prompt, level C runs in one session and critiques its own API, gates take their defaults, and graduation sessions end
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** No session on the road to 1.0 stops for the owner's approval. A plan or QA-plan session ends with the run prompt for a new session; its open questions take their stated defaults, and the owner reviews the plan on their own time. `scripts/roadmap.py` derives a stop's `plan` status while its milestone plan exists and has not started, and `run` once the plan's `status:` header reads `in progress` or `done` or its evidence log has a row; there is no approval line. A level C candidate runs every step in one session: in place of the owner's API review after step 1, the run critiques step 1's `api/` snapshot and template diff with the user-level `critique` skill and checks and records each finding in the plan, as it does the plan's. A gate session runs in auto mode, takes each open question's stated default, and signs its record (`Sign-off: Signed <date> by the gate session; open questions at their defaults`); every open question in `docs/plans/1.0/roadmap.json` carries a default a session can take. Graduation sessions end: a plan cites its workstream's criteria section until that workstream has graduated. The Flow table in `.claude/skills/tungsten-next/prompts.md` loses `approve`, `answer`, `run-c1`, `run-c-rest`, `game-spec` and `graduate`, and one Release row serves every level. Git publication stays the owner's: the ship sitting reviews the uncommitted diff, pastes the command block, approves and merges the pull request and pastes the post-merge block (`D-105`, `D-079`), and hands-on checks (playthroughs, a gamepad, an alt-tab, Windows hosts) stay the owner's too.

**Why:** The owner asked for it on 2026-10-13 (the 0.66 project-health pass, `docs/plans/archive/project-health-pass.md`, findings A1–A8). Each candidate cost an approval round trip, level C a second one after step 1, and each gate a sitting; plan mode, which gates ran in, itself waits for the user's approval before any edit. The cross-family critique already reviews every plan before it runs. The definition gate left seven graduations "as their first milestone nears" that no flow scheduled, and a graduation only moves text that a plan may already cite.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-109`, `D-118` and `D-140` as their marker lines say. `D-079`, `D-105` and `D-108` stand. Level C keeps its meaning, new public API: the Opus max plan of `D-151`, an API sketch in the plan, the step 1 critique, and no `D-108` pair.
- `plan` reads "planned, not started" on the roadmap page; ticks keyed `plan:approve` or `run:run-c1` match no stage and hide.
- A plan session that stops partway leaves a plan with no critique record; `state.sh` prints the record's state, and `/tungsten-next` resumes the plan session.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: the status rule, the Flow rows, and no stage that keeps a session.
- Open limit: the owner's later review can change a plan whose run has started; that change goes through a decision or a follow-up, as any review of landed work does.

## D-151 — Opus 5.5 max only for level C plans and gate sessions; every other session stage at most xhigh
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** `tier(stop, key)` in `scripts/roadmap.py` keeps `D-136`'s Fable rule (Fable 5.1 at xhigh for complexity 4, or 3 with effort XL, unless the stop is a level A or B release) and Opus 5.5 at high for complexity 1 with effort S. Opus 5.5 at max is left only where a stage's judgment is final and no command checks it: a level C stop's `plan` and a `gate`, at complexity 2–4. Every other session stage takes Opus 5.5 at xhigh: `run`, `qa-plan`, the `plan` of a level A, B or not yet carded stop, `experiment` and `rc-fix`, as `rc-checklist` and `release` already did; `verify` stays at high.

**Why:** The owner asked on 2026-10-13 to move max stops to xhigh wherever max does not pay for itself. Measured from this repository's Claude Code transcripts (2026-10-04 to 10-13, deduplicated by message): nine Opus max plan sessions had medians of 199,282 output tokens, 45.1 model-minutes and 4.0 s per call, against four xhigh plan sessions' 128,974 tokens, 35.0 minutes and 3.15 s; the groups did different work (the xhigh plans were M32–M34 and M42, the max plans M35–M47), so the comparison bounds cost, not quality. Ten Opus max runs had medians of 270,066 output tokens and 6.0 s per call against four xhigh runs' 251,466 and 5.15 s, and every run stop in both groups was a perf verdict or an owner question, not a reasoning failure. A run's done-when commands, the release checks and the ship sitting check its work; level A and B plans carry designs a gate or a workstream already settled, and the critique checks them. With the owner's reviews gone (`D-150`), a level C plan's API sketch and a gate's defaults are the judgments nothing else checks before later work builds on them, and the two remaining complexity 2 gates are short sessions.

**Consequences:**
- Supersedes `D-118`'s Opus max clause and `D-136`'s Opus max clause for level A and B releases, as their marker lines say; the Fable clause stands, and no remaining stop meets it.
- Before it, 43 of the 46 session stages left at 0.66 ran at Opus max; after it, and the two stops 0.66 folded into others (`D-150`'s pass), 8 of 42 do: the level C plans (W16a, W13a and W13b, W16b, W13c and W13d, W1 BC, W14b) and the feature and freeze gates. A Phase 7 stop the feature gate cards as level C moves its plan back to max.
- The catalog hash changes, so the next sync rewrites `meta/catalog`. Recommendations stay advice.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: `tier` by stage key and level, and the rule over the repository's whole catalog: Opus max if and only if a stage is a gate or a level C plan at complexity 2–4.
- Open limit: the comparison counts cost, not outcome quality, and no xhigh level C plan or gate has run yet; revisit after the first of each.

## D-152 — Records written at a release's cut stay true through its merge
**Date:** 2026-10-13
