**Decision:** The 1.0 README's owned fields count a stop that is cut, tagged, merged or skipped as closed: `scripts/roadmap.py summary` names the first stop whose derived status is not `ready`, `review`, `done` or `skip`, with that stop's status. `just release-cut` runs `python3 -B scripts/roadmap.py summary --write` after the cut, so the block carries the new workspace version, and the merge changes nothing the block shows. A register row records the cut (`Cut <date> as vX.Y.Z` with its decisions and what landed); nobody edits it after the merge, since `roadmap.py status` reads the merge from `origin/main`.

**Why:** The owned fields named the cut stop as the first open one, `ready`, until its merge, so `just repo-check` failed on a freshly merged branch: on `0.66` at `12ccd36` it did. Before the block arrived in 0.61, the hand-written "Now" lines lagged the same way, and 14 sessions between 2026-10-04 and 10-06 opened with a records-lag prompt for them. The release chain ran `just repo-check` straight after `just release-cut`, which moves the Workspace field, with nothing in between to refresh the block. Two register rows still promised "the squash commit and PR filled in after the merge".

**Consequences:**
- Amends no decision. It extends `D-109`, whose tree-derived status it reads, and the owned-field check `D-140`'s pass added to `scripts/check-repo.py`, which stands with the new first-open rule; a checkout without an unshallowed `origin/main` still compares only Workspace and Next milestone.
- `/tungsten-next`'s records-lag prompt stays for genuine lags, such as a register cell a plan session left empty.
- Tests in `scripts/test-roadmap.py`: the owned fields through a cut, a tag and a merge, and `summary --write`.

## D-153 — W16a landed (M48): a `ComponentRegistry` names the components scene entries carry, checked at load against the World with field paths; strict scene keys and transform bounds; the engine's data-only components registered by their owners; no `Component` marker
**Amended by D-157:** the open limit's W16b items only; prefab files carry the same components, read as strictly, `check_in` and `register_with_assets` check the asset IDs a value names against the World, and `particle_emitter` and `tilemap` join the engine's components; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::component_registry` holds `ComponentRegistry`, a `World` resource keyed by name and by `TypeId`, one name per type and one type per name, the names `[a-z][a-z0-9_]*` in one flat set. Satisfies D-015 rule 3 for `serde_path_to_error`, a solved primitive: one package whose dependencies were already in the graph, which names the field a value fails at. Three doors fill the registry: `App::register_component::<T>(name)` for a game's `main.rs`; `World::register_component` and `World::register_component_with` for a plugin's `build`, which insert the registry when the World has none, as `World::register_event` needs no setup; and the registry's own `register` and `register_with` for one built without a World. `register<T: DeserializeOwned + 'static>` inserts the value as is; `register_with::<D, B>(name, build)` deserializes a definition `D` and builds a `Bundle` from it and a `SpawnContext` (the entity's `Transform` and `&World`), keyed by `D`. A conflict or a name outside the pattern panics at the registering call naming both sides (`D-022`), and the same pair again returns `false`. A value deserializes by serde's own rules (a unit struct reads `null`; the type's `deny_unknown_fields` decides unknown fields) through `serde_path_to_error`, so `ComponentError::Invalid { name, field, source }` names the path where it stopped (`max`, `inner.max`, empty at the value's top) and `ComponentError::Unknown { name }` an unregistered name; checks beyond the types live in deserialization (`#[serde(try_from)]`), so `check` and `prepare` agree on every value and `build` is infallible. A scene entry carries `"components": { "<name>": <value> }`, read strictly: a component named twice, or a key repeated at any depth inside a value, fails the parse, where a plain `serde_json::Value` would keep the last. `SceneData`, `SceneEntry`, `SceneTransform`, `SceneSprite` and `SceneTween` deny unknown keys. `SceneData::load(path, world)` and `asset_loader::load_scene(world, path)` check each entry's transform (every number finite, the position within ±2^24 px), its tweens and its components against the World's registry, an empty one when it has none, and stop at the first error: a `Validation` naming `entities[<i>].transform` or `entities[<i>]: <ComponentError>`. `spawn_scene` prepares every component with `&World` before it changes anything and panics on one the registry rejects (data that skipped the load's check), then queues each entry's components after its fields in name order, so the entity lands in one archetype move and a component overrides a field of its type. The engine registers four data-only components where their readers live: `rigid_body` (`PhysicsPlugin`; a `RigidBodyBundle` at the entry's position, sizes in (0, 16384] and offsets within ±16384, `mass` with a finite inverse, dynamic only), `light` and `parallax_layer` (`App::with_plugins`, beside the default extracts, before the plugins build) and `squash_stretch` (`GameFeelPlugin`), with the shapes `component_registry`'s module doc lists. There is no `Component` marker trait in 1.0: any `'static` type stays a component and bundles join through `Bundle::with`.

**Why:** Criteria §8.7 asks for data-driven entities with game components without `World` serialization, with errors that name the file, the entry and the field; serde names a missing or unknown field but only the expected type for a mismatch, hence the field path. Without `deny_unknown_fields` a misspelt `"componets"` parsed and dropped every component. Registering where the reader lives means a plugin set without `PhysicsPlugin` or `GameFeelPlugin` drops their names with their systems, while the extracts `App` installs keep `light` and `parallax_layer`. The load takes the World, not the registry, so W16b's asset-ID checks read the asset registries from the same argument, and `SpawnContext::world` lets a `build` resolve an ID from the live World at spawn, since asset loading replaces `ParticleConfigRegistry`. The transform and body bounds keep a scene's bodies inside the broadphase's `i32` cells and out of the non-finite paths a `1e40` position or a subnormal mass opened. A spawn panic keeps `spawn_scene`'s signature, and the registry only grows, so a scene loaded against the World's registry always spawns. A default component name added in a 1.x minor could take a name a game registered and fail its startup, which `D-103` clause (2) rules out. A marker trait would break every component type for nothing the registry needs.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-046` (choice 5) and `D-103` (clause 2) as their marker lines say. Settles the registry `D-128` deferred and the marker `D-135` deferred (none in 1.0); both stand, with no marker line, as `D-133` did for `D-128`.
- The umbrella gains a `serde` dependency edge for `App::register_component`'s bound; core gains `serde_path_to_error` 0.1.20 (MIT OR Apache-2.0), one package in `Cargo.lock`. `serde_json::Value` in `check` and `prepare` ties core's API to serde_json 1, as `SceneError::Parse` already did.
- Breaks, each a W16a row in the break ledger: `SceneEntry` gains `components`; `SceneData::load` and `asset_loader::load_scene` take the World; scene files reject unknown keys, repeated component names, keys repeated inside a component's value, and transforms that are not finite or lie past ±2^24.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/component_registry.rs` and two doctests, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/assets/scene.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/plugin.rs`, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/asset_loader.rs` (components beside the fields, a component overriding a field, the panic before anything is queued, the harness spawn and despawn with the state, the four engine components through a fixed step) and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/plugins.rs` (the names per plugin set). `DefaultPlugins`' resolved schedule is unchanged, and no perf row runs a changed path.
- The plan's two critique rounds and step 1's API critique (no concerns) are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-48-component-registry.md`.
- Open limits: the load stops at the first error, while W14b's `check` reports every one and needs the game's registered names; W16b adds the registration form that checks the asset IDs a value names, registers `particle_emitter` and `tilemap` on it, and amends this entry with a marker line for prefab files; W6a extends `rigid_body`'s `kind`; W13a's animated sprite registers its own component.

## D-154 — W6a landed (M49): kinematic bodies and sensors in core physics, `SensorEntered` per pair, spatial queries and casts over the last step, and a kinematic world's substeps and wake
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Decision:** `BodyKind` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` and gains `Kinematic` and `Sensor`, with `RigidBody::kinematic`, `RigidBody::sensor` and the bundles of the same names, which carry a zero `Velocity`. A kinematic body moves by its `Velocity` each substep, without gravity, and no contact moves it: it is a proxy at inverse mass 0 with the immovable softness, and it initiates pairs as an awake body does. Its contacts with static and kinematic bodies and with tiles are dropped as static pairs are, so walls reach it through casts and queries; its contact with a dynamic body sends a `CollisionEvent` whose `a` is the dynamic body, the one the contact pushed. A sensor is never a proxy: the writeback moves it by `velocity · dt`, and at the step's end it tests the bodies and solid tiles it overlaps at their final positions (penetration above 0, never another sensor) and sends `SensorEntered { sensor, other }` (`other: None` for a tile) for each pair its previous step lacked, after the frame's collision events. A pair's key holds the sensor's entity key and the other's identity, a tile's being its map entity, layer and cell; a step without sensors forgets every pair, and a step that does not run changes nothing. `CollisionEvent` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` and gains `new`. `tungsten_core::physics` gains `SpatialFilter` (private fields and builder methods; bodies and tiles by default, no sensors), `SpatialHit` (`#[non_exhaustive]`: `entity`, `point`, `normal`, `distance`) and `cast_ray`, `cast_shape`, `overlap` and `nearest`. They take `&World` and read a private index of the last step's final positions held in a `RefCell` resource (the `D-086` precedent), built at most once per step: by the step when it has sensors, else by the first query. A body moved since the step is found where the step left it, one spawned since is not found and a despawned one is skipped; a face a tile's mask marks internal is never hit. Two rules hold a crowd off a kinematic body:

- **Substeps.** A step whose gather staged a kinematic proxy, moving or still, runs `clamp(⌈substeps · dt · 60 − 0.001⌉, substeps, 2 · substeps)` substeps, `dt` being the step after `max_step_dt`: `substeps` at 1/60 s and shorter, six at 1/45 s and eight at 1/30 s with the default four. Every other world keeps `substeps`.
- **Wake.** A kinematic body moving at any speed wakes a sleeper it touches or closes on, and a dynamic body touching it keeps its island awake through the frame-end pass, whatever `time_to_sleep` is. The wake reaches through that island as the frame's unions have built it so far: a sleeper one of its bodies touches or closes on wakes at any speed and joins it. A still kinematic body wakes nothing and keeps nothing awake, as a static one.

The scene component `rigid_body` takes `"kinematic"` and `"sensor"` (`mass` stays dynamic-only, `velocity` defaults to zero on the three moving kinds, `restitution` does nothing on a sensor), and `PhysicsDebugOverlay` outlines them in two new public colours, `color_kinematic` and `color_sensor`.

**Why:** `D-132` made W6a's kinematic bodies with contact events, sensors with enter events per pair, nearest and overlap queries, and ray and shape casts against bodies and tile layers with the hit point and normal Must items for the acceptance game, and asked for a crowd probe on a kinematic body before W13c's mover builds on it. The probe (`crates/tungsten-core/tests/physics_containment.rs`: 500 circles pressing on a still and then walking body in three spawn orders, a walk into a sleeping crowd at 150 and 10 px/s, a body pinned against a wall, each at frame dts of 1/60, 1/30, 0.05 and 0.1 s) stopped the milestone on its first run. At 1/30 s and above, four of six press cases put a crowd centre inside the walking body (Box BodyFirst 12.44 px deep, BodyLast 11.33, Shuffled 13.75; Circle BodyFirst 16.76): `soft_params` caps a contact's stiffness at a quarter of the substep rate, so a 1/120 s substep holds the kinematic contacts to 30 Hz, half what they have at 1/60 s. The 10 px/s walk sank 7.70 px at 1/60 s and 9.48 px above, against 0.26–0.27 px with sleeping off: a body pushed slower than the wake threshold left the sleeper ahead asleep, and the sleeper held it at inverse mass 0 against the kinematic body's. The probe as landed, with both rules, reads the press at 1/30 s and above 1.62–2.74 px deep with no centre inside, the walk at 10 px/s 0.26 px in all eight cases and at 150 px/s 0.37 px (it was 1.03 px above 1/60 s); at 1/60 s, which the rules leave alone, the press still reaches 5.81, 5.76 and 5.01 px in three of six cases. `D-094` declined a substep bound for every world because it doubles the physics cost between 60 and 30 FPS; this one holds only in a world with a kinematic proxy, depends on the world and the dt alone and at most doubles the count, and the default fixed step of 1/60 s never takes it. A still kinematic body counts so that one starting or stopping changes no other body's step. The island wake is the whole-island wake `D-065` declined for contacts in general, taken only through a moving kinematic body, so a pressed row wakes body by body. Sensor keys are ordered because `RigidBody::kind` is public and two entities can swap which one is the sensor, and a tile's identity is not its warm-start key, which hashes the tile's centre and so moves with the map's public `origin`. Queries read the last step through `&World`, so a system can query inside its own query loop and `PhysicsBuffers` keeps its auto traits.

The milestone's sitting (base 0.67 against the candidate twice in one sitting, five runs a side) reads no owned metric of any row `regressed` in either direct pair: `physics` `physics_step` p50 6.146 → 6.166 and 6.160 → 6.150 ms, `physics-sparse` 3.704 → 3.670 ms in both, `integrated` `total` p95 8.352 → 8.420 and 8.414 → 8.546 ms, all `unchanged`, with the first-run digests matching on all eight rows. The change moves 28 of the untouched rows' 29 hot functions modulo 64, by different amounts, so no padded baseline could have isolated placement, and none was needed.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-033`, `D-064`, `D-065`, `D-066`, `D-081`, `D-092` and `D-094` as their marker lines say. `D-063`, `D-067`, `D-075`'s build, `D-080` (its statics-only grid holds static proxies, never a kinematic one), `D-082` and `D-132` stand.
- Breaks, each a W6a row in the break ledger: `BodyKind` and `CollisionEvent` turn `#[non_exhaustive]`, `BodyKind` with two more variants and `CollisionEvent` with `new`, which replaced the workspace's ten struct literals while its two exhaustive matches gained a wildcard arm; `PhysicsDebugOverlay` gains two public fields.
- Unchanged: the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c`, the eight benchmark digests, `Proxy`'s 80 bytes (a test pins it) and the template's schedule. No new system, stage or dependency; `PhysicsPlugin` registers `EventQueue<SensorEntered>`.
- Cost: a world with a kinematic proxy steps a 1/30 s frame in eight substeps (the press probe: 301–312 µs a step at 1/60 s, 530–556 µs at 1/30 s and above). A frame in which no moving kinematic body touches a body adds one test per sleeper pair; no benchmark row holds a kinematic body or a sensor.
- Recorded limits, in `docs/known-issues.md`: sensors test overlaps at the step's end, so one that moves farther in a step than its size plus a target's can miss it (`cast_shape` covers that case); queries see the last step; a `Position` write moves a kinematic body or a sensor as a teleport that wakes nothing; at 1/60 s a crowd presses up to 5.81 px into a kinematic body, over half a crowd radius; a body pinned between a kinematic body and a wall ends 19.97–21.53 px inside the kinematic body, and its centre crosses a static box's face though not a tile wall's; casts catch on the join of touching static boxes as contacts do.
- Tests: the kinematic and sensor tests in `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/step.rs` (the substep counts and the wake through a pressed row among them), `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/query.rs`, `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/plugin.rs` and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/physics_debug.rs`; `crowd_presses_on_a_kinematic_body_from_every_side`, `kinematic_body_walks_into_a_sleeping_crowd` and `kinematic_body_pins_a_body_against_a_wall` in `crates/tungsten-core/tests/physics_containment.rs`, release only.
- Not taken: moving a kinematic mover's pressed chain with it in `D-092`'s backward sweep, untested and blind to a body whose solved velocity never crosses the pass's threshold. `contact_hertz` stays the 1.x lever.
- The plan's two critique rounds and its amendment after the probe's first run are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-49-kinematic-sensors-queries.md`.
- Open: sensor exit events and swept sensors wait for a user (W13d's zones, the game's bolts); W6b adds layers and masks to bodies, sensors and `SpatialFilter` without a break; a benchmark with kinematic paddles or sensor bins (G1, G2) is a workload change with a decision of its own.

## D-155 — W13a and W13b landed (M50): `tungsten-kit`, a fourth crate on core alone re-exported as `tungsten::kit`, with opt-in plugins for an animated sprite, lifetimes, despawn outside a region or the view and a sound per event, `cursor_to_world`, and a positions-only transform hierarchy propagated after the physics sync and after the tweens; the engine's system names in core
**Amended by D-158:** the `PostUpdate` clause only; a kit system that drives a body or counts a window on the fixed-step clock runs in `FixedUpdate` after the history snapshot and before `physics_step`, and a trigger zone's after the step; the rest stands.
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `crates/tungsten-kit`, package `tungsten-kit`, is the workspace's fourth crate. It depends on `tungsten-core`, `glam`, `serde` and `log`, all already in the graph, so no third-party package joins and the notices do not change, and never on `tungsten-render` or the umbrella: every need goes through `Schedule`, `World` and core's components and resources (`D-007`, `D-016`). The umbrella takes it as an optional dependency behind a default `kit` feature and re-exports it as `tungsten::kit`, so a game keeps one dependency and the kit's API is inside the 1.0 promise through that re-export (`D-103`). An item joins the kit when it is generic and has a user (the template, a migrated example or the acceptance game), with rustdoc that carries an example and a harness test; an item with no user by the freeze moves to 1.x. The kit's modules are private and its root re-exports each item, so each has one public path; `#![deny(missing_docs)]` holds it to rustdoc, and its value types keep private fields behind constructors, so no settings struct needs `#[non_exhaustive]`. Every item is opt-in, one unit-struct plugin per item that a game adds after the engine's, and `DefaultPlugins` holds none. Each kit system runs in `PostUpdate` under a public name constant, and its constraints on the engine's names and across the kit's plugins are if-present, so leaving a plugin out never fails startup. The kit's harness tests live in the umbrella (`crates/tungsten/src/tests/kit.rs`, under `cfg(all(test, feature = "kit"))`), since a kit dev-dependency on the umbrella would be a cycle; its own tests drive a bare `World` and `Schedule`. The items:

- `AnimationPlugin` (`animation_tick`) advances every entity with an `AnimationState` and a `Sprite` by `Time::delta()` against the `AnimationRegistry`, which leaves the World for the pass and goes back, never cloned, and writes the current frame's sprite into `Sprite.asset_id` when the two differ; without a registry it does nothing, and an unknown animation leaves the sprite as it is. It registers the scene component `animation`, `{ "id": "<animation id>" }`, unknown keys failing, which spawns `AnimationState::new(id)` (`D-153`).
- `Lifetime::new(seconds)` holds a `Timer::once`; `LifetimePlugin` (`lifetime_tick`) ticks it by `Time::delta()` and queues `despawn_recursive` once it has finished.
- `DespawnOutside::region(min, max)` (the closed box) and `DespawnOutside::view(margin)` (more than `margin` world units outside `CameraState::visible_world_aabb` at `WindowSize`; without either resource the entity stays) test `Transform.position` as a point; `DespawnOutsidePlugin` (`despawn_outside`) runs after the physics sync, the late propagation pass and the camera, and queues `despawn_recursive`.
- `SoundOnEvent::<E>::new(name, sound)` and `select(name, pick)` register `EventQueue<E>` and a system under the name the game passes, which plays each event once whatever its order against the senders: it remembers how many events the current window held when it last ran and skips that many of the previous window at the next frame. It plays at the manifest volume through `AudioCommands::play_with`, never looping; an unknown sound ID logs one warning per ID and plays nothing.
- `cursor_to_world(&World)` maps `InputState::cursor_position()` through the new `CameraState::screen_to_world`, `position + R(rotation)(screen / zoom)`, `view_projection`'s inverse, so a rotated camera maps; `None` without a cursor position, an `InputState` or a `CameraState`.
- The transform hierarchy: `Parent` and `Children` (no public constructor; the kit keeps them), `LocalOffset(pub Vec2)`, `spawn_child(world, parent, offset, bundle)`, `despawn_recursive(commands, entity)` and `HierarchyPlugin`. Positions only: a child's `Transform.position` is its parent's plus its `LocalOffset`, down the chain, and rotation and scale stay each entity's own. `spawn_child` is immediate, one archetype move with the child's position at the parent's plus the offset and the bundle's rotation and scale kept, and panics on a dead parent, a parent without a `Transform` and a bundle holding a `RigidBody`, despawning the refused child first. Two passes walk from every root (an entity with `Children` and no `Parent`), parents before children, through each child that still hangs from its parent (alive, its `Parent` naming the entity whose list holds it): `transform_propagate` after `physics_sync` and before the particle count refresh, the tween tick, the squash trigger and the camera, which also panics on an entity with both a `Parent` and a `RigidBody`, despawns with its subtree a child whose parent has died and prunes the entries the walk skipped; and `transform_propagate_late` after the tween tick and before the camera, which only writes positions. `despawn_recursive` queues a `CommandBuffer::call` that at the flush despawns the entity and every child that still hangs from it, so a child whose `Parent` an earlier command removed survives, and drops the entity from its parent's list.

The engine's 17 system names move verbatim into `tungsten_core::schedule::names`, and `tungsten::plugins` re-exports each, so no path a game uses changes; the camera, game-feel and tween plugins stay the umbrella's.

**Why:** Criteria §8.4 and the frame-loop gate's tiers (`D-132`): the acceptance game's bolts, walk cycles, hits, kills and pickups and aura ring use these items, and example 01 carries a copy of each, which W12b migrates. A crate on core alone cannot name the umbrella's `post_update` systems, so the names move to core; the kit needs their names, not their code, which answers `D-133`'s question about moving the three plugins. The default extract draws a sprite from its unrotated top-left at `Transform.position` and turns it about its centre (`sprite.wgsl`), so an offset turned about the parent's `Transform.position` would leave the parent's drawn shape; positions only keeps the extract as it is and leaves rotation and scale to their writers, tweens and squash. The engine reads positions before it has finished writing them: the particle emit reads an emitter's position before the tween tick writes positions, and the tween tick cannot move ahead of the particles without changing the engine's order, so one pass after the sync puts a child emitter where its body is before the emit (the lag example 01's `anchor_emitters` fixed at M41), and one after the tweens puts a tweened parent's children where it ended before the camera and the extract. `spawn_child` is immediate, so the frame's propagation and extract see the child and no `Last` stage is needed, which answers `D-128`'s reserve and keeps `Stage` closed. The sound system's name is the game's, not derived from `std::any::type_name`, which is no unique identifier.

The milestone's sitting (0.68 against the candidate twice in one sitting, five runs a side, on `integrated` and `particles`; no row runs kit code) matched the first-run digests on both rows of every pair. `integrated` read `unchanged` in both direct pairs (`total` p50 7.964 → 7.918 and 7.944 → 7.952 ms, p95 8.432 → 8.354 and 8.462 → 8.468). `particles` read no owned metric `regressed` in the second direct pair and `particle_count_refresh` p50 `regressed` in the first (0.620 → 0.654 ms, τ 0.020), while the base's own A/A read that metric 0.620 → 0.652 (`noisy`) and the second pair 0.652 → 0.624; the four `particles` functions sit 112 bytes later in the candidate, +48 mod 64. The plan's check allowed no `regressed` metric in either pair, so the run recorded its perf step as skipped and left that reading to the owner before the release. The owner accepted it on 2026-10-14 as the recorded same-build drift of `particle_count_refresh` p50, not a regression; nothing moves.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-006`, `D-107`, `D-128` and `D-133` as their marker lines say. `D-103`, `D-132` and `D-153` stand.
- No break: the kit's surface, `tungsten::kit`, `CameraState::screen_to_world` and the names defined in core are additions for the freeze (the break ledger's W13a and W13b row). `api/tungsten-kit.txt` is a fourth snapshot. Unchanged: `api/tungsten-render.txt`, `DefaultPlugins`' resolved schedule and the template's and the examples' schedule snapshots, the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c`, the two rows' digests, `gpu-visual.png` and the post-stack, transition and UI overlay references. No example, the bench included, adds a kit plugin.
- Tests: the kit's unit tests in `crates/tungsten-kit/src/tests/` and six doctests, one per item; the umbrella's `kit_tests` (thirteen: the resolved `post_update` line of `DefaultPlugins` with the five plugins, each item through the harness, an interpolated body's children, a tweened parent's, the recursive despawn's flush order and both refusals); `screen_to_world_inverts_view_projection` in core; `scripts/test-check-repo.py`'s kit selector case.
- Recorded limits, in `docs/known-issues.md`: children of a parent despawned at a command flush draw one frame after it; positions only; one sound per event at the manifest volume with no voice limit until W6c.
- Open, each waiting for a user (`docs/plans/1.0/w13-kit.md`): reparenting and detaching, spawning children through the command buffer, rotation and scale inheritance with a pivot design, a `KitPlugins` set, scene components for `lifetime` and `despawn_outside`, and an exactly-once event reader in core. W13c, W13d and the spawner join the kit under the same rules.
- The plan's two critique rounds and step 1's API critique (no concerns) are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-50-kit-basics-hierarchy.md`.

## D-156 — A static or tile support that goes, moves or changes wakes the sleeping islands near it, and an island sleeps only after a frame with every member below the threshold
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** A fifth wake path joins `D-065`'s four. The sleep table records the static and tile proxies, key and box, at the end of every frame in which an island sleeps (`SleepTable::supports`). At the next frame start, while that record holds, `sleep_frame_start` compares the frame's supports with it: in place while the keys keep their order, where a support that moved or changed shape gives its old and its new box, and through a sorted multiset difference when one appears or goes, where each box in only one list counts. Every sleeping island with a member whose box overlaps a changed box grown by the speculative slack, `4 × linear_slop`, wakes. A support is a proxy that is neither dynamic nor kinematic, a static entity body or a collision tile; a kinematic body keeps its own wake (`D-154`), and a sensor is no proxy. The sleep test changes too: an island sleeps when its slowest member's timer is above 0, so every member was below `sleep_threshold` this frame, and at least `time_to_sleep`; at `time_to_sleep <= 0` an island sleeps at the end of the first frame in which every member is below the threshold.

**Why:** The 2026-10-14 review (physics F1 and F4) found that every wake path ran through dynamic members. Statics and tiles hold no sleep entry, and sleepers and statics form no pairs, so despawning a floor, moving it by its `Position` or replacing a tilemap without the tiles under a pile left the island frozen in the air, and a static moved into a sleeper overlapped it silently until something else woke it; example 01 woke its moving platforms' riders by hand. Waking every island on any support change would be simpler, but example 01 writes its moving platforms' `Position` every frame, so nothing there would sleep. The record is taken only while something sleeps and compared in place, so a world whose supports stand still pays one pass over them a frame while an island sleeps, and a scan of the sleep flags otherwise. A body above the threshold restarts its timer at 0, so the old test, which kept an island awake only while its slowest timer was below `time_to_sleep`, let a `time_to_sleep` of 0 or less sleep an island moving at any speed. Measured on the 0.70 tree: `just physics-release` keeps the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c` and containment 0/3000, and every probe line of the containment and tunneling tests equals the 0.69 tree's apart from timings; `integrated` and `particles` read no owned metric `regressed` against 0.69 in either direct pair, both A/A pairs read none `regressed` or `improved`, and the first-run digests match (`perf-runs/20261014-r070-review-fixes/`).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-065` as its marker line says. `D-082`, `D-154` and the rest of `D-065` stand.
- Tests: `a_static_support_that_goes_or_moves_wakes_the_islands_on_it` (a floor despawned, moved down and moved aside, and a support far from the island moved without waking it), `a_tile_support_removed_wakes_the_island_on_it` and `a_time_to_sleep_of_zero_never_sleeps_a_moving_body` in `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/physics/step.rs`.
- A behaviour change, not a compile break (the break ledger's 0.70 runtime row); `PhysicsConfig::time_to_sleep`'s rustdoc states the new edge.
- Open, in `docs/known-issues.md` (physics F10): a support change tests every sleeper against every changed box, so moving a tilemap under a large sleeping pile costs a one-frame spike, as island wakes already do. A kinematic body or a sensor moved by its `Position` still wakes nothing (`D-154`'s recorded limit).
- The plan, `docs/plans/archive/review-fixes-0-70.md`, holds the evidence.

## D-157 — W16b landed (M51): prefab files in a `prefabs` manifest section, spawned by ID at once or at the command flush; Tiled object layers whose classes name prefabs, their properties written over the prefabs' components; the asset-checked registration form with `particle_emitter` and `tilemap` on it; a `#[non_exhaustive]` `TilemapData` with a builder; the kit's spawner
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** Five parts, all in core but the loading and the two components, so a prefab is spawned on core alone (`D-007`, `D-016`) and the extract still reads `&World` (`D-018`):

- **The asset-checked form.** `ComponentRegistry::register_with_assets(name, assets, build)` and `World::register_component_with_assets` add to `register_with` a check of the asset IDs a definition names against a World, `Fn(&D, &World) -> Result<(), MissingAsset>`. `check` stays deserialization alone, so a tool without a World still checks shapes; `check_in(name, value, world)` adds the asset check, and `prepare` runs it against `SpawnContext::world()` before `build`, so `build` may `expect` the IDs it is given and an asset that went between a load and a spawn fails the spawn. An ID the World lacks is `ComponentError::MissingAsset { name, field, kind, id }`, from a `MissingAsset { field, kind, id }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, with `new`). `SceneData::load` and `PrefabData::load` call `check_in`. The engine registers two components on it where their readers live (`D-153`): `particle_emitter` (`ParticlesPlugin`; `{ "config": <particles ID>, "seed": <u64> }`, the seed optional, unknown keys failing; a `ParticleEmitter` with the config's `AssetId` and the seed, and a fresh `ParticleEmitterState`) and `tilemap` (`App`, beside the extracts; `{ "id": <tilemaps ID> }`; a `TilemapInstance` with the entity's position as the map's top-left).
- **Prefab files.** A manifest section `prefabs` (`PrefabEntry`, `ResolvedPrefab`, `ManifestError::MissingPrefabFile`; `D-017`'s uniqueness) lists files under `assets/prefabs/`, each a scene entry's fields without `transform`: `PrefabData { sprite, visible, tag, tweens, components }`, at most one tween, parsed as strictly as a scene and checked against the World at load, `PrefabError::{Io, Parse, Validation}` naming the file and the field. `tungsten::asset_loader::load_prefabs` loads them in sorted ID order into the `PrefabRegistry` that `App` inserts; `load_all` runs it after the particle configs and tilemaps a prefab's components name, then checks every map's objects, a failure failing the load as an unknown tileset sprite does. `reload_prefab` hot-reloads one for later spawns: a bad edit keeps the previous prefab, and live entities stay as they are. A manifest reload keeps that order: new maps, new particle configs, then new prefabs against them, then the new maps' objects, a failure logged with the map keeping its tiles.
- **Spawning.** `World::prepare_prefab(id, position)` builds a `PreparedPrefab`: the prefab's own fields checked as a load checks them, each component prepared with a `SpawnContext` at `Transform::from_position(position)`, and the sprite's name interned last, so an error leaves the World as it was; `PrefabError::{Unknown, Invalid, Position, Component}`, the position bounded to ±2^24 as a scene transform's. `PreparedPrefab::with(bundle)` adds components after the prefab's own; `spawn(world)` spawns it now and `CommandBuffer::spawn_prefab(prepared)` queues it for the flush, each as one archetype move (`D-084`); `World::spawn_prefab(id, position)` prepares and spawns. The entity takes, in order, the `Transform`, the `Sprite`, `Visibility`, the `Tag`, the `Tween`, the components in name order and the `with` bundles, so a later value wins over an earlier one of its type, as in a scene entry. A prefab spawn adds no `SceneEntity`: a state passes its own through `with`.
- **Tiled objects.** `TilemapData::load` reads `objectgroup` layers into `object_layers: Vec<ObjectLayer>` (`ObjectLayer { name, objects }`, `MapObject { id, name, class, position, size, properties }`, both `#[non_exhaustive]`), in file order, each layer's offset added. An object's class is Tiled's `type`, or `class` from a Tiled 1.9 file; a tile object's `position` is its top-left under the tileset's `unspecified`, `bottomleft` or `topleft` alignment; `properties` keep each Tiled value as JSON, a class as an object. The load refuses, as `TilemapError::Invalid` naming the layer and the object, a templated object, a classed rotated one, a `type` and `class` that differ, a property named twice, an object without `id`, `x` or `y`, a number that is not finite, a negative size and a position past ±2^24; Tiled's `visible` filters nothing. `World::check_map_objects`, `prepare_map_objects(map, origin)` and `spawn_map_objects` spawn each classed object's prefab at `origin + position`, in layer and file order and all or nothing, with the object's properties written over the prefab's components by dotted path in name order: the first segment names a component, added as `{}` when the prefab lacks it; a later segment steps into an object's member or, when all digits, an existing array element; a class property merges member by member and no property removes one, so a property that switches a tagged value's variant fails the component's check at the leftover field. An object without a class is a marker the game reads. Errors are `PrefabError::{UnknownMap, Object}`.
- **The builder and the spawner.** `TilemapData` turns `#[non_exhaustive]`, its fields public for reading and bulk writes, with `new(tile_width, tile_height, width, height)`, `add_tile`, `add_layer`, `set_tile` and `fill`, which panic on a zero dimension or an out-of-range layer, cell or tile (`D-022`). The kit gains `Spawner::new(prefab, interval)` with `with_count`, and `SpawnerPlugin`, whose `spawner_tick` runs in `PostUpdate` after the physics sync and the late propagation pass when they are registered (`D-155`): each finished period of game time prepares the prefab at the spawner's `Transform.position` and queues it, a prefab that fails logging once per ID with its period counted.

**Why:** Criteria §8.7 and the frame-loop gate (`D-132`): the acceptance game spawns its bolts, gems, enemies and props from prefabs, places its spawners from its generator and builds its arena in code each run, and example 01 is Tiled objects' one user, so its ten ambient emitters moved onto an object layer of one prefab class and the template's player onto a prefab. `D-153` left the asset check to this form: an unknown particle config in a scene or a prefab now fails the load with its field, not the first emission, and the spawn checks again because asset loading replaces registries. A spawn by ID returns a `Result`, since the ID comes from code or data; `spawn_scene` keeps its panic on data its load checked. The queued form takes a prepared prefab because a system's buffer is a resource of the same World it prepares against. The spawn checks the prefab's own fields again because a `PrefabData` deserialized in code and inserted skips the load, and `Tween::new` debug-asserts its duration (step 1's API critique, C.2). A classed rotated object fails because Tiled turns an object about its origin and the engine a sprite about its centre, and a templated one because its fields live in a `.tx` file the loader does not read. Class properties merge and never remove a member, so a variant switch fails at the leftover field instead of a rule guessing which members belong to which variant (the plan's round-2 critique). `#[non_exhaustive]` lets `TilemapData` gain fields after the freeze without a break, as `object_layers` is one now.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-032` and `D-153` as their marker lines say. `D-103` covers the two default names and the prefab and object formats as `D-153` amended it; `D-132`'s tilemap builder is delivered; `D-155`'s rules take the spawner; `D-053`'s reload matrix gains its Prefab row and the Tilemap row its object check.
- Breaks, each a W16b row in the break ledger: the manifest types gain the section and `ManifestError` its variant; `TilemapData` turns `#[non_exhaustive]` with `object_layers`, so a literal outside core takes the builder; Tiled files that loaded and now fail; the two new default component names. The rest is additions for the freeze.
- No new dependency, system, stage or schedule change: `DefaultPlugins`' and the template's resolved schedules are unchanged. The template and example 01 gain a `serde` edge, already in `Cargo.lock`.
- Unchanged: the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c`, the first-run digests of `integrated` (`334b519e18b543d7`), `gpu` and `gpu-throughput` against the 0.70 tree, `gpu-visual.png` and the post, transition and UI overlay references.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/assets/prefab.rs` (the table of file errors, spawn order, the queued form, map objects with their properties and every rule's error), the asset form in `component_registry.rs`, `scene.rs`, `manifest.rs` and `tilemap.rs` (object rules, alignments, the builder and its panics), `tests/manifests.rs` (every shipped prefab file parses); the kit's `spawner.rs`; the umbrella's `tests/prefabs.rs` (loading, hot reload, the manifest reload's blocks, both components through the harness), the components' values in `tests/asset_loader.rs`, the names per plugin set in `tests/plugins.rs` and the spawner in `tests/kit.rs`; the template's harness spawning its player from the manifest's prefab; example 01's ambient test against its generated table.
- Recorded limits, in `docs/known-issues.md`: a spawner's entities carry no state marker, so a state's exit leaves them.
- Open (`docs/plans/1.0/backlog-1.x.md` and the workstream files): nested prefabs, scene entries that instance prefabs and patching live entities on hot reload; rotated and templated Tiled objects; a rotation or scale in prefab files and overrides of a prefab's sprite, tag and tween; a `spawner` scene component and state ownership of spawned entities (W13c, W13d); the kit's `animation` component on the asset form (W12b).
- The plan's two critique rounds and step 1's API critique are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-51-prefabs-tiled-objects.md`.

## D-158 — W13c and W13d landed (M52): the kit's platformer controller and top-down mover on kinematic bodies in `fixed_update` before `physics_step`, health with an invulnerability window counted on the fixed step, `damage` and `Damaged`, a hit flash on `damage_flash` that restores the sprite's material and override block, trigger zones over `SensorEntered` keyed by the game's types, a path follower and a per-entity state machine; example 01's player stays its own
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `tungsten-kit` gains W13c and W13d under `D-155`'s rules (private modules, one public path at the root, rustdoc with an example, opt-in plugins that a game adds after the engine's, if-present constraints, value types behind constructors and `with_` builders that panic on bad arguments), with no new dependency. What drives a body or times a hit runs in `FixedUpdate`: after the history snapshot, `health_tick`, then a `StateMachinePlugin`'s system, then `path_follow`, then `platformer_controller` and `top_down_mover`, then `physics_step`, then a `TriggerZone`'s system; `hit_flash_tick` runs in `PostUpdate` after `tween_tick`. A game's input for a controller runs in `FixedUpdate` before it. The items:

- **Controllers.** `PlatformerController::new(run_speed, jump_speed)`, with `with_gravity`, `with_coyote_time` (0.1 s), `with_jump_buffer` (0.1 s), `with_jump_cut` (0.5) and `with_filter`; input through `set_run`, `press_jump` (which also holds the jump until `set_jump_held(false)`), `set_jump_held`, `launch`, `push` and `lock_input`; `grounded`, `can_jump` and `jumped` read its last step. `TopDownMover::new(speed)` with `with_filter`, `set_direction` and `direction`. Each moves a kinematic body (`D-154`) with a `Position`, a `Velocity` and a `Collider`, skipping any other with one warning: its first step moves nothing, so the physics step indexes what it was spawned among; then it casts against the last step's positions in up to four slides that stop 0.01 px short and project the rest onto the surface met, and writes `Velocity = displacement / dt`, `dt` being `Time::delta()` bounded by `PhysicsConfig::max_step_dt`. The platformer probes 1 px down for a floor (`n.y <= -0.7`) unless it is rising, applies a launch, then the input (run, and a buffered press on the ground or within coyote time jumps), then the pushes, then the jump cut once on a release while rising, then gravity (its own or `PhysicsConfig::gravity.y`) over the step's mean velocity, so a jump lies on the exact parabola at each step and peaks at `jump_speed² / 2g`; a floor met while falling lands it, a ceiling stops its rise, a wall its held speed, and on a kinematic ground it adds the ground's `Velocity · dt` uncast unless it jumped, was launched or was pushed up. Timers tick last on the game clock, a countdown treating 10 µs as over, so a window of whole steps ends on its last step. Its default `SpatialFilter` sees bodies and tiles: dynamic bodies block it and hold it up, and it never pushes one.
- **Health.** `Health::new(max)` with `with_invulnerability`, `current`, `max`, `is_dead`, `is_invulnerable` and `heal`; `damage(world, target, amount) -> Hit` (`Ignored`, `Hurt`, `Killed`, `#[non_exhaustive]`) acts at once, starts the window and the target's flash, and sends `Damaged { entity, amount, killed }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`, with `new`) when `HealthPlugin` registered its queue. `HEALTH_TICK` counts the windows down by the step in `FixedUpdate`.
- **Hit flash.** `HitFlash::new(color, seconds)` keeps the sprite's `material_id` and the entity's `UniformOverrideBlock` (or that it had none) once per flash, sets the material named by `HIT_FLASH_MATERIAL`, `damage_flash`, and writes the colour into `vec4[0]` and the amount into `f32s[0]`; `HIT_FLASH_TICK` lowers the amount linearly over the flash's game time, leaving the first frame full, then restores the material and block and removes a block it inserted. Without the material nothing flashes and the tick warns once.
- **Trigger zones.** `TriggerZone::<Z, T>::new(name)` registers `EventQueue<TriggerEntered<Z, T>>` and a system after the step that sends one `TriggerEntered { zone, other }` for each of the step's `SensorEntered` whose sensor carries a `Z` and whose other body a `T`; a tile never triggers, and only entering is reported.
- **Path follower.** `PathFollower::new(points, speed)` with `looping`, `ping_pong` (the last call wins) and `with_easing`, and `is_finished`: each segment takes its length over the speed, eased by its progress, leftover time carries into the next, and the body's `Velocity` is set to reach the path's point at the step's end, zero once a stopping path is finished.
- **State machine.** `StateMachine::<S>::new(initial)` with `state`, `previous`, `elapsed` and `go`, and `StateMachinePlugin::<S>::new(name)` with `on_enter`, `on_exit` and `on_update`, plain `fn` hooks, a second of one kind for one state panicking. Each step visits the machines in entity order: a first visit enters the state `go` asked for, else the initial one; a pending `go` runs the old state's exit hook, then the new one's enter hook, and resets `elapsed`; every visit then runs the update hook and adds the step. A `go` made during a visit waits for the next step, and a hook that despawns its entity or removes its machine ends the visit.

Example 01's player stays a dynamic body with its own input and ground detection.

**Why:** Criteria §8.4 and the acceptance game's mechanics rows (`D-132`): its player moves top-down, its enemies and boss take damage and flash, its gems are trigger zones and its boss runs a state machine; the platformer controller and the path follower are Must only if example 01 moves onto the kit by the freeze. A controller that moves a kinematic body by casts avoids `D-154`'s recorded overlap of a body pressed against a kinematic one, and running it before `physics_step` lets its velocity move the body in the same step. A first step that moves nothing answers the plan critique's finding that queries find nothing before a world's first step. Windows count on the fixed step because a `PostUpdate` count charged a window for the frame time before its hit, so two hits a step apart landed or not by the frame rate (critique finding 3); the flash restores the block because a block present overrides the material's authored defaults in the renderer (finding 4). `damage` acts at once so a caller knows the outcome in the same step, which leaves `D-155`'s exactly-once reader open. Q22 took its default: the four controller features, and slopes, one-way platforms and ladders to the backlog.

Example 01's move (the plan's step 3) stopped on its stop condition: with the player on the controller, the route test reported `gate_step -> gate_crest` not traversable. The slab over `gate_step` ends at x 7744 with its underside at y 983, 41 px above `gate_crest`'s top at their shared edge, and the player is 56 px tall, so no box that does not overlap a solid passes the corner; the route's `LAUNCHES` row starts the player inside the crest's tile column, from which the dynamic solver pushes it out and a kinematic controller cannot leave. The step's files were restored.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-155` as its marker line says. `D-103`, `D-132`, `D-153` and `D-154` stand.
- No break: the items, their plugins and the six name constants are additions for the freeze (the break ledger's W13c and W13d row); `api/tungsten-kit.txt` gains lines and loses none. Unchanged: `api/tungsten-core.txt`, `api/tungsten-render.txt` and `api/tungsten.txt`, `DefaultPlugins`' resolved schedule, the template and its snapshot, every example, the determinism hash `0x088e_c07a_73c1_b168`, the containment hash `0xaee2_72e0_1ffc_4e4c`, the benchmark rows and the pixel references.
- Tests: the kit's unit tests and one doctest per item in `crates/tungsten-kit/src/`; the umbrella's `kit_tests` pin the new `fixed_update` and `post_update` lines and drive each item through the harness, among them a platformer in a fresh world against tiles, a box and a ceiling, the jump's height and cut, coyote time and the buffer, a ride on a ping-pong platform, a knockback and a pull, a top-down mover's slide and contact, one hit sequence at three frame rates, the flash's batches and the zones, paths and machines.
- Recorded limits, in `docs/known-issues.md`: two controllers do not see each other's motion within a step, a body spawned since the last step is unseen until the next, nothing pushes a kinematic body out of an overlap, a ground's carry is uncast, a lit sprite does not flash, a flash beats a game tween on its block and loses a change made to the material or block while it runs, and zones report entering only.
- Example 01 keeps its player until W12b, which moves it with the level's `gate_step` corner fixed; until then the platformer controller and the path follower have no user, and they move to 1.x if none appears by the freeze (`D-155`'s rule).
- Open: slopes, one-way platforms and ladders (`docs/plans/1.0/backlog-1.x.md`); a controller that pushes dynamic bodies, or a body-kind query filter, after W6b's layers and masks; scene components for the new items; `damage_flash` for a game outside this repository; sensor exit events; state ownership of spawned entities, the acceptance game's first gap.
- The plan's critique and step 1's API critique are recorded in `docs/plans/archive/1.0/phase6-milestone-52-controllers-helpers.md`.

## D-159 — W11b landed (M53): the player's settings and bindings live in the user folder, written through one atomic write, between `tungsten.json` and the environment; a corrupt user file falls back to defaults beside a `.bad` backup; `UserFolder` is set in code
**Amended by D-163:** the settings list only; `Settings` gains the text scale (`text_scale`, 0.75..=2.0, the file's `text_scale` key), which `apply_settings` sets on the `UiTree`, an unset value 1, and `App::new` sets on the tree it inserts; the rest stand.
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** Five parts, all in core except the folder resolution, the app wiring and the runtime apply, so core gains no `wgpu` or `winit` type (`D-007`, `D-018`); no new dependency.

- **One write.** `tungsten_core::persist::write_atomic(path, bytes)` writes a `create_new` temp file named with the pid and a nonce in the target's folder, `sync_all`s it, renames it over the target, then on Unix `fsync`s the folder, best effort. On Windows a rename refused with OS error 5, 32 or 33 is retried, five attempts with sleeps of 20, 40, 80 and 160 ms, then `WriteError::Busy`; any other failure is `WriteError::Io`, the temp file removed and the target unchanged. The action map, the settings and the save slots share it, and it is public for a game's own files. Power-loss durability on Windows is not claimed.
- **Settings.** `Settings`, a `World` resource, holds the display mode, the resolution, vsync, `ui_scale` (`UI_SCALE_RANGE`, 0.5..=3) and the master volume (0..=1), each an `Option`, `None` meaning the shipped default. `<settings>/settings.json` holds only the set values and no version field: it grows by keys, and unknown keys are ignored. `Settings::load` never fails: a missing file gives defaults; one that cannot be read for another reason gives defaults with a warning and stays; an invalid one (not UTF-8 JSON, not an object, a known key of the wrong type, a value no setter would keep) gives defaults for the whole file and is renamed to `settings.json.bad`, replacing an older backup. Its warnings wait in `take_load_warnings`. Setters clamp and ignore values that are not finite; `save` returns `SettingsError::NoFolder` without a folder.
- **The layer.** `Config::apply_settings(&Settings)` writes each set display value over the file's; a set vsync also clears `display.present_mode` and `render.present_mode`, so `auto` follows it, as F9 does; then a config `Config::load` built takes its environment overrides again, so they stay last, while one built in code stays free of them. `ScaleFactor` gains the player's UI scale: `get()` is the OS scale times it (`D-127`, `D-144`).
- **Bindings.** `ActionMap::with_user_layer(Option<&Path>)` makes three layers per action: the engine defaults, the shipped `input.json` (fatal when invalid, hot-reloaded) and `<settings>/input.json`. `persist` then writes only the actions whose bindings differ from the layers below, to the user file; `None` leaves no path, so `persist` returns `MissingSourcePath`. A corrupt user file falls back to the layers below and is renamed to `input.json.bad`. A hot reload of the shipped file lays the current user layer over it again. Core F4: a binding `input.json` cannot name (`KeyCode::Other`) is skipped on save with a logged warning, an action left with none is left out of the file, and `"button0"` loads, so every saved button loads.
- **The app and the folder.** `GameConfig::user_folder: UserFolder` (`Platform` by default, `At(dir)`, `Off`; `#[serde(skip)]`, set in code) and the resolution order: smoke mode, an invalid id and `Off` give no folder; then `At(dir)`; then `TUNGSTEN_USER_DIR`; then no id gives none; then the platform's folders. `App::with_plugins`, after the logger, loads the settings (defaults without a folder), keeps the config as it arrived as the baseline, applies the settings to it, loads the bindings with their user layer and inserts `Settings`, the `ScaleFactor` with the UI scale and a set master volume as `SetMasterVolume`. `tungsten::apply_settings(world)` is the runtime apply: it computes the target from the baseline as startup does, copies the display mode, resolution, vsync and present mode into the queued display request or the current state, and replaces that request or clears it when it matches; an unset value returns to the shipped one, the UI scale and master volume to 1.0. It is a function, not a system, so no schedule changes.

**Why:** W11b's scope and owner question 15's default (`D-150`): a corrupt user file resets to defaults, and the engine persists the display settings, `ui_scale`, the master volume and the bindings. A file the game wrote never stops it, while a broken shipped file stays a developer's error (`D-008`, `D-045`). Saving only the difference lets a game update's new defaults reach every action the player left alone. A code-set folder beats the environment so the template's tests stay pinned in a developer's shell (they used to read `~/.local/state/tungsten-template-basic`), and smoke mode beats both, so captures read no user file (RC-S3). A set vsync clears the present mode because a concrete present mode wins over vsync in the renderer. The plan's critique shaped the runtime apply: computing it from the shipped baseline lets an unset value come back and makes a runtime apply agree with a restart, editing the queued request lets the last apply before a frame win over a hotkey's, and re-applying the overrides only to a loaded config keeps startup and runtime on one rule.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-008`, `D-045` and `D-119` as their marker lines say. `D-022`, `D-127` and `D-144` stand.
- Not persisted: the present mode, frame latency and frame-rate cap stay developer settings in `tungsten.json`; the F9 and F11 hotkeys persist nothing, and `apply_settings` replaces their changes to the persisted fields. A window resized by hand returns to the set or shipped resolution at the next `apply_settings`, since a resize persists nothing.
- A save's skipped bindings are logged when the save runs, a logger existing by then; `ActionMap::take_load_warnings` holds the user layer's load warnings, which `App::new` logs.
- The API changes and additions are in the W4 break ledger; `ActionMapError` gains no variant (W4b's sweep can add a serialize variant).
- Template: a `toggle_mute` action on M that sets or unsets the master volume, applies and saves; `log` in its manifest; its tests set `UserFolder::Off`, and `mute_survives_a_restart` uses a temp folder.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/{persist,settings,config,display}.rs`, the action map's and `key_serde`'s, `crates/tungsten/src/tests/{user_dir,settings,app}.rs` and the template's `tests/game.rs`.
- Open: volume per bus joins `Settings` in W6c; the settings screen and its capture-next-input mode are W1 M4's; gamepad bindings W5a's; the user files are not hot-reloaded.

## D-160 — W11c landed (M53): save slots hold a game's own serde types in a versioned envelope in the user folder's `saves`, migrated by the game's own code; slot names follow `game.id`'s rule in lowercase; every failure is a typed error
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::save` adds `SaveData`, a trait over the game's `Serialize + DeserializeOwned` type with a `SCHEMA: u32` and a `migrate(from, Value) -> Result<Value, Box<dyn Error + Send + Sync>>` that by default refuses every older schema, and `SaveSlots`, a `World` resource `App::new` inserts over the user folder's `saves` (`D-119`). A slot is `<saves>/<slot>.json`, written through `write_atomic` (`D-159`): `{ "envelope": 1, "slot", "schema", "engine_version", "saved_at_ms", "data" }`, the engine version core's, the time Unix milliseconds or 0 for a clock before 1970; the folder is created on the first save. `load` reads envelope 1 only (`UnknownEnvelope` else), deserializes data saved under `T::SCHEMA`, calls `T::migrate` once for an older schema and then deserializes (`Migration` on either failure), and refuses a newer one (`NewerSchema`); a failed load never panics and never changes the file. `list` reads the header of every `*.json` that is not a dotfile (the write's temp files are), sorted by name, and skips an unreadable one with a line in `SlotList::warnings`; `delete` removes a slot. A slot name is 1–64 of `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `.`, `_` and `-`, starting with a letter or digit and not a Windows device name: `game.id`'s rule, lowercase only. `SaveError` (`#[non_exhaustive]`) has `NoFolder`, `InvalidName`, `NotFound`, `Corrupt`, `Serialize`, `UnknownEnvelope`, `NewerSchema`, `Migration`, `Io` and `Write`; without a folder (smoke mode, `UserFolder::Off`, no `game.id`) every call is `NoFolder`. `tungsten_core::save::Value` re-exports `serde_json::Value`, so a game writes a migration without a `serde_json` dependency of its own.

**Why:** Owner question 16 made save slots Must for 1.0 (`D-102`), and the acceptance game's unlocks row needs them. The game owns its types and migrations because only it knows what changed; the engine owns the envelope, so it can list slots and refuse a file it cannot read without knowing the game's types. Lowercase names keep two slots from sharing one file on a case-insensitive file system, and names may come from a player, so a bad one is a runtime error (`D-022`). Refusing a newer schema and never rewriting a failed slot keep a newer build's save safe from an older build, which is why the template's autosave turns read-only for the run when its load fails (the plan's critique finding 1).

**Consequences:**
- Narrows DESIGN's "Save / load" non-commitment to World snapshots: saving the `World` by reflection stays a backlog row (saves by reflection).
- No break: the module, its re-exports at the core root (`SaveData`, `SaveSlots`) and the resource are additions for the freeze (the W4 break ledger's new-surface row).
- Template: gameplay continues from slot `autosave` (`SaveGame { player }`, schema 1), pause saves it, and a slot that fails to load other than as missing is never overwritten that run; its tests `continue_from_the_autosave` and `a_slot_that_fails_to_load_is_never_overwritten`.
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/save.rs`, the `SaveData` doctest and `crates/tungsten/src/tests/save_slots.rs` (a schema bump through the game's migration across two `App`s on one folder, a failed migration).
- Open: no slot metadata beyond the header (a thumbnail, a play time) and no cloud sync; a save is written on the calling thread, which suits the small saves 1.0 targets.

## D-161 — W1 WL landed (M54): world labels are pushed each frame to a core `WorldLabels` resource, projected, culled, capped and scale-snapped by the umbrella's extract, and drawn as one glyphon batch below the paint list on the shared layout cache, counted under the trim rule
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `tungsten_core::text` adds `WorldLabel` (`#[non_exhaustive]`: text, a world `anchor`, an `offset` in UI units, a `pivot` in the label's box, default (0.5, 1.0), font ID, size and line height in UI units, sRGB colour and its own `scale`) and `WorldLabels`, a `World` resource `App::new` inserts: immediate mode, pushed during the frame and drained by every extract, drawn or not. `CameraState::world_to_screen` inverts `screen_to_world`, rotation included. The umbrella's extract (`extract_world_labels`, after the paint list) projects each anchor onto the viewport render draws at (the surface's size, else `WindowSize`), adds the offset times `ScaleFactor::get()`, culls a point outside the viewport grown by the cull margin (64 UI units by default) or a drawn scale that snaps to 0, keeps the first `cap` (512 by default) survivors in push order and counts the latest pushes past it as dropped, and snaps `label.scale × ScaleFactor` to the nearest 1/8. While `UiDebug::hide_game_layers` is set it drains and keeps nothing. The runs go to the renderer as plain `WorldLabelRun`s (`tungsten_render`, `#[non_exhaustive]`) through `Renderer::update_world_labels`, which keeps a copy for the next render, and to the harness's `FrameDraw::world_labels`; the counts go to `RenderCounts` (`world_labels`, `world_labels_culled`, `world_labels_dropped`). `TextEngine` keeps the labels as a second prepared group beside the sections on the one layout map: a label lays out unbounded on one line under its layout key and draws as one `TextArea` at `scale` with `left = x − width × scale × pivot.x` and `top = y − height × scale × pivot.y`, bounded by the viewport, so each label pops and scales as a whole (`D-126`). `TextPipeline` gives the group its own `TextRenderer` on the shared atlas; a non-empty group counts as one batch for the trim rule, prepares on every full frame and whenever its runs or the viewport changed, and is not drawn after an `AtlasFull` prepare, which forces the next frame full. The overlay pass draws the labels first, then the paint list's items, then the sections, so the UI's game layers cover labels. An empty group prepares and draws nothing. `TextStats` gains `labels`, `label_glyphs` and `layouts`, which reach `RenderCounts::text_label_glyphs` and `text_layouts` and the HUD's `ui` row.

**Why:** The acceptance game's damage numbers and the name tags of example 02 need text at world points (w01 §10–§11, §14). Projecting at extract keeps render free of the `World` and the camera's anchor in one place (`D-018`), and immediate mode needs no retained IDs for text that lives one frame. One batch below the paint list is the glyph gate's T1b shape (`D-126`): the labels share the atlas and the content-keyed layout cache (`D-085`), so equal damage numbers shape once, and a game-layer panel covers them as a HUD panel should. glyphon keys a rasterized glyph by its physical size, so a continuously animated scale would rasterize new glyphs every frame; eighths bound a pop to a few sizes. Counting the group as a batch stops `integrated`, which has no UI, from trimming the atlas on every changed frame now that its tags are labels (Q19). Touching both groups' layouts whenever the counter advances keeps a static group's layouts alive while the other changes every frame, which a per-group count would have aged out.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-085` and `D-147` as their marker lines say; extends `D-126` (a batch with whole-label scale and position).
- Example 02's name tags push `WorldLabel`s with their offset and scale divided by `ScaleFactor`, so they keep the benchmark's physical size; `integrated` stays at `workload_version` 1.
- Tests: core `world_to_screen_inverts_screen_to_world` and `world_label_builders_set_their_fields`; the umbrella's extract tests (projection, cull, cap, one-frame life, hide, snap); render `label_layouts_plateau_under_unique_text`, `labels_share_layouts_with_sections`, `label_pivot_places_the_box` and `labels_count_as_a_batch`; example 05's `labels` fixture against `ui-labels.png` and its direct and capture paths.
- Breaks for the freeze: `RenderCounts` and `TextStats` gain fields (struct literals); the rest is additive.
- Open: per-glyph effects stay the T2 backlog row; `push` takes an owned `String` (a pooled push is a backlog row); labels have no depth against sprites and draw over the whole scene.

## D-162 — W1 BC landed (M55): checkboxes and horizontal sliders are the closed leaf kinds `Toggle` and `Slider`, valued in node data, changed by the router and reported as `UiEvent::ValueChanged`; their indicator paints in the kind's text colour
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Decision:** `WidgetKind` gains `Toggle` and `Slider`, focusable, interactive leaves with the roles `CheckBox` and `Slider` (`Role` gains both), built by `UiTree::toggle(parent, on)` and `UiTree::slider(parent, range, value)`. A toggle holds a `bool`; a slider holds an `f32` in a `SliderRange` (`tungsten_core::ui::control`, re-exported from `tungsten_core::ui`): `new(min, max)` is continuous and panics on a bound that is not finite or `min > max`; `with_step(step)` panics on a negative or non-finite step and allows `min + k·step` for whole `k` up to `max`, and `max` itself. Snapping clamps to the bounds and takes the nearest allowed value, the lower on a tie, with `max` a candidate of its own; values compute in `f64` from `min` and a whole `k`, never accumulated, and store `f32` clamped to the bounds. A build value that is not finite starts at `min`; a set one changes nothing. `toggled`, `slider_value` and `slider_range` return `None` for any other widget; `set_toggled`, `set_slider_value` and `set_slider_range` clamp and snap, act only on a change (the first two return it), mark paint and send no event. Neither kind holds text: a caption is a sibling `Label` and the accessible name is the one the game sets. An `Auto` axis takes a natural size from the resolved line height, a toggle one line square and a slider ten lines by one; a `Px`, `Percent` or `Fill` axis wins. `KindStyles` gains `toggle` and `slider`, and the theme file the `toggle` and `slider` kinds with the same five looks: a toggle's box takes the background, border and radius and its mark the text colour; a slider's track takes the background, its fill and thumb the text colour, the thumb the border colour, width and radius; the built-in looks draw from the tokens. A control's resolved text change relayouts its root only when it measures differently; a colour-only change marks paint. Paint (`Rect` commands only): a toggle paints the generic box, then, when on, a mark inset on every side by a quarter of its shorter side with the box's radius less the inset, not below 0; a slider paints no box but a track `h / 4` thick across its rect on the centre line with radius `min(r, h / 8)`, a fill from its start edge to the thumb's centre, and a square thumb of side `t = min(h, w)` centred at `start + t / 2 + fraction · (w − t)`. Routing: an activation, a primary release over a toggle or `ui_accept` while it holds focus keys act on, flips it and sends `ValueChanged { widget, value: UiValue::Bool }` right after its `Activated`, before a `DoubleClicked`; a primary press on a slider, after its capture and focus, and each move while that press holds it set the value at the pointer, the fraction `(x − (start + t / 2)) / (w − t)` clamped to 0–1 between exact ends and snapped, off the slider and past its ends too, with no grab offset; the release and a cancel change nothing; `ui_left` and `ui_right` step a slider that holds focus keys act on to the next allowed value that way (a tenth of the span when continuous; when that value stores as the current `f32`, the first allowed value at or past the adjacent `f32`, else the end) instead of moving focus, on the navigation repeat, and at an end take the press and change nothing; `ui_up`, `ui_down`, `ui_next` and `ui_prev` move focus as before. `ValueChanged` follows each change of the stored value by the router, one per change, after the press's own events; `UiValue` is `#[non_exhaustive]` with `Bool`, `Number`, `as_bool` and `as_number`; `EventQueue::<UiEvent>::value_changed(widget)` returns the last value of the current window.

**Why:** M4's settings screen (display mode, the scales, the volume, one rebind) needs a checkbox and a slider, which the definition gate put in 1.0 (criteria question 8, `D-102`; w01 §8 and §11). Closed leaf kinds keep the kind match and the style model as they are: two more `KindStyle`s with the same five looks and the text colour as the indicator's, so no style property or state joins and a theme recolours a control as it recolours a label, while a leaf passes its text style to nothing. Holding no text node, a control has nothing to re-set for a colour, so the slider's hover and press looks repaint without a relayout. The router alone reports changes, so a game shows stored settings through the setters without echoing them back; one activation path keeps a click and `ui_accept` one rule, and `Activated` still follows, so `D-149`'s pointer contract holds for every interactive widget. Left and right stepping a focused slider leave up, down and Tab to walk a vertical settings list, and the first key after pointer use stays the game's. `f64` arithmetic from `min` and a whole number of steps keeps `-f32::MAX..=f32::MAX` finite and repeated steps exact; `max` as its own candidate keeps it reachable when the step index loses precision past 2^53 steps (M55's API critique); the adjacent-`f32` rule keeps every press moving when a step is finer than the `f32` spacing (M55's plan critique).

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125`, `D-147` and `D-149` as their marker lines say; extends `D-040` (`value_changed`) and `D-148` (two theme kinds).
- Breaks for the freeze: `KindStyles` gains two fields, so a struct literal needs them or `KindStyles::for_tokens`; the rest is additive (w04's ledger).
- Tests: `crates/tungsten-core/src/tests/ui/{control,paint,route,style,theme_file}.rs`, the harness tests in `crates/tungsten/src/tests/ui_route.rs` and example 05's `tests/screens.rs`. Example 05's interactive run builds a "Settings" window; its captured fixtures are unchanged.
- Settles `D-149`'s open limits: the drag threshold stays with M5's movable windows; popups and click-outside dismissal join progress bars, collapsible sections, tooltips and window resizing in the backlog row "Broader controls beyond checkboxes and sliders".
- Open: a caption that activates its control, the whole settings row as the focus target, the wheel, Home, End and the page keys on a slider and an analog stick's reach (M4 decides); vertical sliders and sprite-skinned controls.

## D-163 — W1 M4 landed (M56): state-owned UI roots that hide or freeze while covered and fade with the transition; paint-only opacity, translation and scale with a root fade and frozen roots; UI animation tracks on the real clock; `ui_route` before the dispatcher; a label's colour-only change repaints; disabled ancestors' look; content-box text; text scale; prewarm
**Date:** 2026-10-15
**Decision:** Gameplay screens build on the core UI with nine additions, plain data and pure functions in core, the umbrella mapping roots to states and bridging the prewarm to the renderer, which reads no `World` (`D-007`, `D-018`).

- **Paint-only properties.** `UiTree::set_opacity` (0..=1, NaN ignored), `set_translation` (UI units, non-finite ignored) and `set_scale` (1/64..=64, a value not finite or not positive ignored), with getters, mark paint only. Roots take `set_fade` (0..=1) and `set_frozen`. A node's alpha is the product of the opacities on its path times its root's fade; a node maps its subtree by `p ↦ c + s·(p − c) + t`, `c` its layout centre and `t` its translation rounded to whole physical pixels at the last paint's scale, composed down the tree. A text run draws at the composed scale rounded down to a multiple of 1/32 (unsnapped below 1/32) in its exact box shrunk to match about its centre, so it never outgrows its box or its clips. `UiTree::visual_rect` is where a widget paints and is hit.
- **Frozen roots.** A frozen root keeps its look, is hit and blocks; its widgets take no press, activation, value change or focus move, a press one held is cancelled at the next routing, no `ui_*` action acts in it, and as a modal root it still takes every key press, so an Escape during a fade neither cancels nor reaches gameplay.
- **Text and style.** A colour-only text style change repaints and re-sets nothing; a label measures and commits at its content box (UI F4); a widget under a disabled ancestor applies its `disabled` variant and `set_enabled` marks the subtree (UI F2).
- **Animation tracks.** `UiTrack` (`#[non_exhaustive]`) moves one `UiProperty` (opacity, translation, scale, background, border, text colour) with core's `Easing`, `TweenRepeat` and `TweenDirection` (`D-054`) and a `u32` tag. `UiTree::animate` writes the start value at once and replaces a track on the same property silently; `stop_animation` keeps the value; removal drops tracks; a non-finite value or a non-positive scale is refused with a warning; a duration that cannot run finishes on the next tick. `ui_animate_system` ticks on `Time::real_delta()` (`D-129`), the remainder carried across cycles, and sends `UiEvent::AnimationFinished { widget, property, tag }` for `Once` and `Times`, read with `EventQueue::<UiEvent>::animation_finished`. Colour tracks write the node's `StyleOverrides`; none relayouts.
- **State-owned roots.** `StateStack::own_root(root, RootCover)` gives a root to the state being dispatched, by its stack index (two instances of one `StateId` keep their own), or else to the top state. Covered, a `Hide` root hides and a `Show` root stays shown and frozen; uncovered, it takes back the visibility it had and thaws; the owner's pop or replace removes it after `on_exit`. While a transition is queued or active every owned root is frozen with `fade = 1 − transition_cover()`. The sync runs at the dispatcher's end and in `UiPlugin`'s `ui_state_roots` before `ui_route`, and returns at once with no owned roots.
- **The schedule.** `pre_update: …, display_input, ui_state_roots, ui_route, ui_animate, state_dispatcher`; `UI_STATE_ROOTS` and `UI_ANIMATE` join core's names, and `ui_route` and `ui_animate` take `before_if_present(STATE_DISPATCHER)`, so the order holds whichever plugin registers first. The bench, without `UiPlugin`, keeps its order.
- **Text scale.** `UiTree::set_text_scale` (0.25..=4.0) multiplies label size and line height where a text node is set and a control's natural size, resolved styles unscaled; `Settings::text_scale` (`TEXT_SCALE_RANGE` 0.75..=2.0) persists it.
- **Allocation-free text.** `UiTree::set_plain_text(id, &str)` compares a one-span label without allocating.
- **Prewarm.** `UiTree::prewarm(root)` asks for a one-shot raster of the root's runs, shown or hidden, once laid out; `paint_prewarm` paints them; the app hands the list to `Renderer::prewarm_ui_text` before the frame's prepare (`FrameDraw::ui_prewarm` records it), which prepares the runs on a renderer of their own on the shared atlas, never drawn, returns `TextPrewarm` and logs `UI prewarm: <runs> text runs, <glyphs> glyphs in <ms> ms`.

**Why:** The acceptance game's title, pause, level-up, results and settings screens all wait on W1 M4. Owning roots by stack index puts a screen's UI under its state's lifetime without `SceneEntity`'s shared-`StateId` limit, and fading every owned root with the cover keeps the UI, which draws after the transition pass (`D-093`), in step with the scene. Freezing rather than hiding keeps a covered game screen visible under a pause menu and makes a screen that is fading in or out inert. Routing before the dispatcher lets a state's `update` read `activated()` and `RoutedInput` for this frame, where the old order failed silently; the second sync before `ui_route` freezes roots for a transition an `Update` system queued after the dispatcher (the plan's second critique round). Paint-only properties and colour tracks repaint without relayout, and the unbaked style colour turns a fade into a batch re-prepare rather than a reshape. Rounding the text scale down to a 32nd bounds the raster sizes a pop rasterizes and keeps text inside its box (the plan's first critique round). The prewarm takes a menu's cold glyph cost out of the frame that opens it.

**Consequences:**
- Amends `D-125`, `D-147`, `D-149` and `D-159` as their marker lines say; extends `D-040` (`AnimationFinished`), `D-046` (state-owned roots), `D-093` (owned roots fade with the cover) and `D-126` (the prewarm prepares on the shared atlas under the trim rule; an `AtlasFull` counts as a batch's).
- Breaks for the freeze: `PaintCommand::Text` gains `scale` and its `rect` is the content box; behaviour: `ui_route` runs before the dispatcher, a colour-only change repaints, a widget under a disabled container looks disabled, a padded label measures at its content width (w04's ledger).
